Nothing in the app currently calls `synthesizeAiOpportunities` automatically post-approval either — that trigger doesn't exist yet, same as the notification cron gap already documented below.

## Client authentication (confirmed 2026-08-03)

First step of Priority 1 (real client path: auth → Company/Goal creation → Evidence Intake → client-facing Report view, with Privacy & Consent built into that same flow — confirmed 2026-08-03, replacing the earlier priority order once the full checklist audit revealed the client-facing product essentially doesn't exist yet). Real gap found before this was built: `companies.user_id` is a `NOT NULL` FK to `users.id`, but no client-facing sign-in route existed anywhere — only `reviewer-login`. Every client-facing page built so far used the interim `?companyId=`/`?goalId=` URL-addressing hack instead. Building the real Evidence Intake / Privacy & Consent flow on top of that hack would have meant the consent gate existing but the underlying data still not actually protected — flagged explicitly before building anything, confirmed to fix properly rather than extend the interim pattern.

**Design — mirrors reviewer-login's magic-link + code pattern exactly, with one deliberate difference**: genuine self-serve signup, not gated to pre-provisioned accounts. [src/app/client-login/actions.ts](src/app/client-login/actions.ts) — `requestClientMagicLink()` calls `signInWithOtp()` directly with no pre-check (reviewer's flow gates on an existing `role="reviewer"` row first; clients should be able to sign up, so `shouldCreateUser: true`, Supabase's default, handles first-time emails automatically). `verifyClientCode()` mirrors the reviewer code-fallback exactly, same reasoning (Gmail's link-prescanning can consume a real single-use magic link before the human clicks it). [src/app/client-login/page.tsx](src/app/client-login/page.tsx) is a near-identical UI to `reviewer-login/page.tsx`, adapted copy. The existing `/auth/callback` route needed no changes — it was already written generically ("shared by any auth flow that needs a callback").

**No DB trigger syncs `auth.users` to `public.users`** (confirmed by reading the migrations directly, not assumed) — only `scripts/grant-reviewer.ts` manually upserts that row, and only for reviewers. `ensureClientUserRow()` closes this gap for clients: called from the `(app)` layout on every authenticated request, idempotent (no-ops if the row exists), and — important — never overwrites an existing role, so a reviewer account visiting a client page doesn't get silently downgraded to `client`.

**`(app)/layout.tsx`** ([src/app/(app)/layout.tsx](<src/app/(app)/layout.tsx>)) — real gating, mirroring `(reviewer)/layout.tsx` function-for-function: unauthenticated visitors redirected to `/client-login`; authenticated-but-wrong-role visitors (a reviewer account) get an explicit denial message, not a silent redirect loop; authenticated clients see a nav bar (Dashboard/Business Profile/Reports & History/Account Settings) plus a sign-out button. [src/app/(app)/sign-out-button.tsx](<src/app/(app)/sign-out-button.tsx>) is the client-flavored copy of the reviewer's own sign-out button.

**Verified live, end-to-end, real session (2026-08-03)**: a real signup was driven through the actual UI — `/client-login` → email submitted through the real form → real `signInWithOtp()` call succeeded → code-entry UI appeared → verified via a real OTP (obtained through Supabase's admin `generateLink()` API rather than reading an inbox, since the test email had no real mailbox to check — this generates a genuinely valid code through the same underlying mechanism, not a shortcut around it) → real session established → redirected to `/business-profile` → the `(app)` layout correctly rendered the authenticated shell, nav, and email → confirmed via direct DB read that `ensureClientUserRow()` created a real `public.users` row with `role: "client"`. Sign-out correctly cleared the session and redirected to `/client-login`; a subsequent direct navigation to `/business-profile` was correctly redirected back to `/client-login` (confirming the gate is a real server-side check, not just a client-side UI convenience). Test account deleted afterward.

**One real, unrelated bug found and diagnosed during this verification, not a code bug**: the very first live attempt used `@example.com` test addresses and failed with an opaque `500` from `signInWithOtp`. Isolated with three direct script calls before assuming anything: the same call succeeded instantly for a real, already-provisioned email (the founder's own reviewer account), and separately succeeded for a brand-new email at a real, deliverable domain (`gmail.com`) — narrowing the cause to `example.com` specifically, a reserved non-routable domain (RFC 2606) that Resend's relay rejects at submission time. Not a client-auth bug; a test-data artifact. Retested cleanly with a real-domain test address afterward.

**`(app)` layout's "authenticated but wrong role" denial branch — live-tested, 2026-08-05** (closes the gap originally left at code-review confidence to avoid sending an unwanted magic-link email to the founder's real inbox). Real fix: used a disposable test account instead of the founder's own — `elvanis-test-reviewer-denial-2026@gmail.com`, created via the admin API (no email sent by creation itself) and granted `role="reviewer"`. Driven through the actual `/client-login` UI end-to-end: submitted the real form (a real magic-link send did fire, but to the disposable test address, never the founder's inbox), then completed verification using a fresh OTP pulled via `supabase.auth.admin.generateLink()` rather than reading any inbox (same technique already proven for the original client-auth verification) — note the first OTP pulled *before* the real send was stale, since `signInWithOtp` invalidates any prior token for that user; regenerating *after* the send fixed it. The established session correctly rendered the denial message ("...is signed in but does not have client access (role: reviewer)") with no client content or dashboard leaking through, Sign Out correctly cleared the session and redirected to `/client-login`, and a direct DB read confirmed `ensureClientUserRow()` never downgraded the role (still `reviewer` after the visit) — exactly the behavior the code was written to guarantee. Test account deleted afterward (both the `public.users` row and the `auth.users` account), same disposable-test-data precedent as the original client-auth verification.

**Priority 1 — CLOSED OUT, 2026-08-04.** The full real client chain (sign in → create company/goal → submit evidence via fill-in-template → reviewer approves + delivers → client sees the real report) is now built and verified live end-to-end, no stub anywhere in that path.

**Company/Goal creation** ([src/app/onboarding/](src/app/onboarding/)) — a top-level route deliberately outside the `(app)` group, so `(app)/layout.tsx` can redirect there unconditionally (a signed-in client with no `companies` row yet) without any loop risk. `actions.ts`'s `createCompanyAndGoal()` uses the session's own RLS-respecting client (`src/lib/supabase/server.ts`), never the admin client — the entire point of building real client auth was that this first client-owned write goes through the same protection every other client row gets. Minimal field set (company name + primary goal), not the full Business Profile field set — `CompanyProfileForLens`/`GoalContext` only require those two; everything else is nullable and lenses already handle sparse profiles correctly.

**Evidence Intake, fill-in-template path** ([src/app/evidence-intake/](src/app/evidence-intake/)) — real per-lens forms (curated free-text fields for Financial/Execution/Product, a direct `CommercialSelfReport` mapping, and a genuine two-mode AI & Governance section: a documentation-description textarea in document-review mode, or all 7 real `GOVERNANCE_DIMENSIONS` maturity-level dropdowns in questionnaire mode). `actions.ts`'s `submitEvidence()` verifies company ownership via the session client, then calls `runAudit()` (which persists via the admin client internally, same pattern as the three modules). Numeric `metrics` and Commercial's `independentResearch` (the real Tavily automation already exists) are both deliberately left empty for this pass — logged as a real, deliberate deferral, not silently dropped.

**Client-facing Report view** ([src/app/(app)/reports/[reportId]/page.txt](<src/app/(app)/reports/[reportId]/page.tsx>)) — replaces the `generateReport()` stub. **Real RLS mismatch found and worked around correctly**: the `reports` table's client-facing SELECT policy only allows `status = 'sent'`, not `'approved'` — matching the deliberate design where `deliverReport()` is a separate, explicit step from `approveReport()` (a report is reviewer-done but not yet client-visible until delivered). A plain session-client query for a not-yet-sent report returns zero rows under RLS, indistinguishable from "doesn't exist" — fixed by using the admin client ONLY to check ownership+status (so a real client sees "still being reviewed" instead of a bare 404), then the session client for the actual findings content, which only ever succeeds once `status = 'sent'`, exactly as designed. Findings are correctly filtered to `approved`/`edited` only (never `draft`/`rejected`), using `reviewer_edited_content ?? ai_draft`. The 30/60/90 roadmap — a real, confirmed gap (`reports.roadmap_30_60_90` exists in schema, nothing anywhere ever generated it) — is derived deterministically at render time from the top-3 findings' severity (critical/high → 30 days, medium → 60, low → 90), matching this codebase's established "deterministic wherever code can already produce the answer" principle rather than inventing a new AI-generation feature unprompted.

**Privacy & Consent** — real [privacy policy](src/app/privacy/page.tsx) and [ToS](src/app/terms/page.tsx) pages (spec §1.8 content: named AI provider, no third-party sharing, no model training, human-review step disclosed, deletion process; advisory disclaimer + Tender Readiness not-legal-advice notice), upload-point micro-copy shown directly on the evidence intake form, and a real acknowledgment checkbox gating the submit button — `submitEvidence()` stamps `companies.privacy_acknowledged_at` at first real submission (not at company creation), only once.

**Real bug found and fixed live during verification**: `ensureClientUserRow()`'s original check-then-insert (SELECT, then INSERT if missing) was not atomic — Next.js's layout can genuinely be invoked more than once for overlapping requests, and two concurrent calls both saw "no row exists," both tried to INSERT, and the second hit `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "users_pkey"`. Fixed with a real upsert (`ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING` via `ignoreDuplicates: true`), atomic at the database level — no read-then-write race window at all, and still never overwrites an existing role.

**Verified live, end-to-end, real session and real data (2026-08-04)**, company `Nimbus Ledger Test Co` (`0379a978-4c46-4836-a211-1cd7b2580b87`), report `ad52b4f3-3a31-44b1-ba6f-884e0d4e6d5a` — left in the database as real proof, same precedent as every other genuine end-to-end verification artifact this session (not synthetic scratch debris): real signup through the actual UI → real company/goal creation → real evidence submitted through the actual fill-in form → real `runAudit()` run (2 of 5 lenses succeeded — Financial and AI & Governance persisted 11 real findings; Execution/Product/Commercial failed on the same pre-existing `goalRelevance` enum-hallucination class already documented earlier this session, confirmed by isolating Execution directly with identical evidence and capturing the exact invalid value the model returned (`"directly_affects"`, not in the enum) — `Promise.allSettled` resilience worked exactly as designed, the audit persisted correctly with a partial lens set rather than failing entirely) → reviewer flow via direct function calls (`approveReport()`'s new edit-window guard correctly required backdating first, confirming the fix from earlier this session is real and active) → all 11 findings accepted → `approveReport()` succeeded → `deliverReport()` succeeded → navigated back to the report URL as the same client session → real content rendered: title, goal, top-3 priorities, the deterministic 30/60/90 roadmap (correctly bucketed by severity), and every finding grouped by lens with diagnosis/rootCause/recommendedAction/severity.

**Genuinely still deferred, logged as real remaining scope (see the Evidence Intake scope decision above)**: native CSV/PDF upload/parsing with per-source signature matching, numeric metrics input, Commercial's independent-research auto-trigger, the full Business Profile field set, and a reviewer-workspace "Deliver" button (the `deliverReport()` backend function has no UI caller yet — verified via direct function call, same precedent as other backend-proven/no-UI-yet gaps this session).

## `goalRelevance` hallucination — root-caused and fixed, 2026-08-05

The `directly_supports` fix (2026-08-01) closed one real gap but was never the full story — `generateValidatedJson`'s safety net kept catching the same class of failure (Execution/Product/Commercial on 2026-08-04, see immediately above), which meant the underlying cause was still live, just being caught cleanly instead of corrupting data. Investigated properly this time rather than accepting "the safety net catches it" as good enough, per explicit direction not to leave this as a known gap.

**Root cause, confirmed by reading all three failing lenses' own prompts, not guessed**: every one of Execution's, Product's, and Commercial's own `GOAL-RELEVANCE GUIDANCE` sections describes the `cash_flow_margin_efficiency` goal in identical terms — a real, material, often-quantifiable COST or DRAG on the goal ("labor-hours cost of decision latency," "whether low adoption... is wasting acquisition spend," "pricing pressure forcing discounting") that is nonetheless not the finding's *primary* obstruction of the goal. Neither existing negative value honestly fits: `directly_blocks` overstates it (nothing is actually preventing the goal), `indirectly_affects` understates it (the cost link is direct and traceable, not tangential). This is the same class of gap `directly_supports` fixed, just on the negative side rather than the positive one — a schema incompleteness, not a prompt-wording problem, exactly as the original fix's own docblock already correctly diagnosed for its own case.

**Reproduced live, not just reasoned about**: built evidence at exactly this semantic boundary (meeting-load cost under Execution, low-adoption-wasting-spend under Product, pricing-pressure-forcing-discounts under Commercial, all under `cash_flow_margin_efficiency`) and ran each several times. Before the fix, identical evidence made the model swing unpredictably between `indirectly_affects` and `directly_blocks` across repeated runs — both wrong, and the instability itself (not a clean, consistent pick) is the same signature that originally motivated `directly_supports`. Did not attempt to recover the exact original real-client evidence that first surfaced this (the fill-in-template intake path never persists `evidenceFields` to any table — confirmed by querying `evidence_submissions`/`evidence_fields` for the real company involved and finding zero rows, so nothing to reconstruct); synthetic evidence built directly from each lens's own documented goal-relevance guidance was a more reliable way to hit the exact same semantic gap than guessing at the original wording.

**Fixed at the schema level** ([src/lib/lenses/types.ts](src/lib/lenses/types.ts), [schemas.ts](src/lib/lenses/schemas.ts)): added a fifth `GoalRelevance` value, `"directly_affects"` — deliberately reusing the model's own repeatedly-hallucinated name rather than inventing a new one, since it's already the intuitive fit and now matches what the prompts ask for. Updated everywhere the enum is load-bearing: all 5 lens system prompts (rule 5/6 guidance with a concrete per-lens example, plus the OUTPUT SCHEMA JSON literal), the reviewer workspace's edit-form dropdown, `run-audit.ts`'s `scoreForDefaultRanking` weight map (`directly_blocks: 4, directly_affects: 3, directly_supports: 2, indirectly_affects: 1, unrelated: 0` — both problem-values now outrank the healthy `directly_supports` value for top-3 "needs action" purposes, consistent with the existing `blocks > supports` reasoning), and `prioritization.ts`'s `isFixFirstCandidate()` (a high-severity `directly_affects` finding is now as much a fix-first candidate as a high-severity `directly_blocks` one — the distinction between them is causal centrality, not reviewer priority).

**Re-verified live after the fix, same exact scenarios**: all three previously-unstable cases (Execution meeting-load, Product low-adoption, Commercial pricing-pressure) now consistently and correctly classify as `directly_affects` across repeated runs — no more oscillation, no more invented values. Financial's existing `directly_supports` case (76% gross margin, healthy) was re-run as a regression check and still classifies correctly — the fifth value didn't disturb the fourth. `tsc --noEmit`, lint, and a full grep for stale 4-value enum literals all clean.

**Evidence Intake scope decision, confirmed 2026-08-03**: "real Evidence Intake" per §5 has two distinct halves — a fill-in-template path and a native-export upload/parsing path (CSV/PDF, with per-source signature matching for Xero, QuickBooks, HubSpot, Salesforce, Jira, Intercom, Zendesk, plus a generic-parsing fallback). Building fill-in-template first was flagged as a real scope-narrowing decision before starting, not assumed — confirmed: fill-in-template is genuinely in scope for the "minimal end-to-end client path" pass (the full real chain — sign in → create company/goal → submit evidence via fill-in-template → see the resulting client-facing report — with no stub anywhere in that path, including the Report view itself). **Native file upload/parsing (CSV/PDF + the 7 named source signatures + generic-parsing fallback) is explicitly logged here as confirmed, deferred, real remaining scope — not something that quietly disappears once the fill-in-template path works.** `evidence_files.parsed_field_map`/`parsed_status` and `export_source_signatures` already exist in schema for exactly this, unused until this follow-on piece is built.

## Real email sending (confirmed 2026-08-04, Priority 2)

Second priority after the minimal client path, explicitly authorized ("go ahead — proceed with Priority 2, real Resend email sending") — sending real messages is an action this app never took autonomously before this explicit go-ahead. Closes the gap flagged by the full checklist audit: every notification-creating function already logged a real `notifications` row and stopped there ("a separate, explicit, confirmed step"); until now nothing ever took that step.

**[src/lib/notifications/send-email.ts](src/lib/notifications/send-email.ts)** — the only file allowed to call Resend's API directly, same "one file per provider" pattern as `ai-client` (Groq) and `search-client` (Tavily). **[src/lib/notifications/dispatch.ts](src/lib/notifications/dispatch.ts)**'s `sendPendingNotifications()` is the actual "separate, explicit, confirmed step" every existing check had been deferring to: queries every `notifications` row with `sent_at IS NULL`, looks up the recipient's email via `recipient_id` (always a `users.id`, confirmed by reading every existing call site directly, never a `company_id`), sends via Resend per an `event_type`-specific template, and stamps `sent_at` on success. One send failing never blocks the others — same `Promise.allSettled`-style resilience as everything else in this codebase. Wired into the cron tick ([src/app/api/cron/tick/route.ts](src/app/api/cron/tick/route.ts)) as a 6th check, deliberately run *after* the other 5 (not alongside them in the same `Promise.all`) so a notification row created by this exact tick gets sent immediately rather than waiting for the next one.

**`report_ready` — a real, previously-unbuilt notification path, closed as part of this pass**: `deliverReport()` ([src/lib/reviewer/workspace.ts](src/lib/reviewer/workspace.ts)) now logs a real `report_ready` notification row for the client (recipient = the company's owning user) the moment a report is delivered — it still does not send the email itself inline, same "log now, send on the next dispatch pass" pattern as every other check, but the row now actually exists where before `deliverReport()` did nothing notification-wise at all.

Added `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` (`.env.local`/`.env.local.example`, defaults to `http://localhost:3000`) — used to build real links in email bodies, since the dispatcher runs server-side with no browser `origin` to derive one from.

**Verified live with two real sends, both confirmed by their `notifications.sent_at` stamp**: (1) a `regulatory_content_review_due` test notification sent to the founder's real reviewer inbox (`msally.abas@gmail.com`) via the actual dispatch pipeline; (2) `deliverReport()`'s new notification-insert logic verified separately against a real, already-approved report (`d8f84c84-...`, the same report documented earlier this session as legitimate historical proof) — correctly created a `report_ready` row for the right recipient. **Real, unrelated cleanup done in the same pass**: `sendPendingNotifications()` also picked up 9 pre-existing `notifications` rows from earlier sessions' testing, all pointing at `@example.com` test accounts (same reserved, non-routable domain already diagnosed as the cause of the `signInWithOtp` 500 during client-auth verification) — Resend correctly rejected all 9 with a clear `422 validation_error` rather than a silent failure, confirming the per-item error isolation works as designed. Since `@example.com` addresses can never succeed, these were genuine dead-end test debris (not real user data, same category as scratch files) — deleted rather than left to retry forever on every future cron tick.

## Priority 3 — Account Settings, Business Profile, Dashboard, Reports & History, module time-per-audit (confirmed 2026-08-04)

Third priority, explicitly authorized ("go ahead and start Priority 3... flag anything that turns out bigger or more tangled than expected"). The two SLA-enforcement fixes originally listed under this priority were already completed earlier (moved forward, before Priority 1, per explicit re-sequencing) — not redone here. Digital Presence Scan and the procurement-answer generator/evidence-pack export were initially deliberately **not** included in the first pass — both were genuinely bigger and more ambiguous than the rest of this list; see the dedicated sections below. The procurement-answer generator and evidence-pack export were subsequently unblocked (real research supplied the missing question taxonomy, real direction resolved the export-format question) and built 2026-08-05 — see "Tender Readiness's procurement-answer generator + evidence-pack export" below. Digital Presence Scan remains deferred.

**Module-level time-per-audit display** — [src/app/(reviewer)/review-module/[requestId]/](<src/app/(reviewer)/review-module/[requestId]/>) now selects `created_at`/`approved_at` and renders a single duration ("time in review"), mirroring the core Report pattern but without the full-cycle/reviewer-only split — modules have no client-facing edit window (they're created directly in `pending_review`), so `created_at` already *is* the point review became possible.

**Full Business Profile fields** ([src/app/(app)/business-profile/](<src/app/(app)/business-profile/>)) — replaces the "only the one goal-linked field" stub with the real full field set (industry, business model, employee count, stage, website, social/review links, revenue range band, customer type, tools/stack, team structure). `updateCompanyProfile()` writes a `company_profile_history` row per *changed* field only (comparing against the values the form loaded with, not re-fetched) — closing the "Profile edit history" checklist line, which had zero rows before this. The page itself is now fully session-derived (`auth.getUser()` → `companies.user_id`), not `?goalId=` — closing a gap flagged back when client auth first landed ("the layout now gates real sessions, but the pages themselves haven't been rewired... yet"). `updateDesiredFutureState()` ([src/lib/goals/desired-future-state.ts](src/lib/goals/desired-future-state.ts)) was switched from the admin client to the session-scoped one in the same pass, closing its own previously-flagged known gap — ownership is now enforced by `goals`' existing RLS policy, not trusted from the caller.

**Real Dashboard** ([src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx>)) — latest top-3 priorities + 30/60/90 roadmap status, drawn from the most recently `sent` report. (Active Execution Sprint progress was correctly absent at the time this was built — no Execution Sprint feature existed yet; a real tile was added once the feature was built, see "Execution Sprint Dashboard — built, 2026-08-06" below.) The roadmap-derivation logic (`deriveRoadmap()`) was extracted to [src/lib/reports/roadmap.ts](src/lib/reports/roadmap.ts) so the Dashboard and the client Report view share one implementation instead of two copies that could drift.

**Real Reports & History list** ([src/app/(app)/reports/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/reports/page.tsx>)) — lists core audit reports and all three standalone modules, `status = 'sent'` only, sorted by delivery date. Comparison-over-time is correctly excluded (explicit V2 scope). **Real bug caught before shipping**: the first version linked module items to `/review-module/[requestId]` — the *internal, reviewer-only* workspace route, which a client session can't reach (it's gated to `role = 'reviewer'`). Fixed by not linking module items at all, showing "Detail view coming soon" instead — no client-facing detail view exists yet for module results (only the internal reviewer workspace renders module findings), a real, separate gap from "list them at all," not silently worked around by linking to a route that would just bounce the client. **Also flagged, not fixed**: `module_requests`' own RLS policy is looser than `reports`' — it lets the owner read any status, not just `sent` (confirmed by reading the migration directly), unlike `reports`' `sent`-only policy. This list filters to `sent` at the application level for consistency with the "only visible once actually delivered" principle already established for core reports, but the underlying RLS policy itself wasn't changed — that's a schema decision deserving its own confirmation, not a silent side effect of building a list page.

**Real Account Settings** ([src/app/(app)/account-settings/](<src/app/(app)/account-settings/>)) — name and notification preferences, both real. **Flagged and deliberately not built**: password management (this app is passwordless throughout — magic-link + code, both client and reviewer auth — there is no password to manage) and real billing/subscription changes (no payment provider is integrated anywhere in this codebase; `plan_tier` is displayed, not editable, since there's nothing real to upgrade to). Notification preferences are **genuinely functional, not decorative** — [src/lib/notifications/dispatch.ts](src/lib/notifications/dispatch.ts) now checks `users.notification_preferences` before sending any client-facing event type (`report_ready`, `re_audit_reminder`, `evidence_incomplete`), defaulting to "send" when unset so an account that never touched this page doesn't silently go dark. Reviewer-facing event types are deliberately not gated by preference — a reviewer opting out could break their own reviewing workflow, not asked for. An opted-out notification still gets `sent_at` stamped (it was correctly handled, not a delivery failure to retry) — just never actually emailed.

**Verified live against real data** (`Nimbus Ledger Test Co`, the same real account/report from the Priority 1 end-to-end proof): Dashboard correctly rendered the real top-3/roadmap from the real delivered report; Reports & History correctly listed it; Business Profile's save was confirmed via direct DB read — both the `companies` row and two new `company_profile_history` rows (`industry`, `business_model`) were written correctly; Account Settings' save was confirmed the same way, and the notification-preference gate was proven functional end-to-end — a real `re_audit_reminder` notification created for the opted-out user was correctly skipped by `sendPendingNotifications()` (`skippedByPreference: 1`, zero emails actually sent) rather than just displaying a toggle with no effect.

### Deliberately flagged, not attempted this pass — Digital Presence Scan

Per §5: "Company name/URL input, no login. Public signal scraping (website, socials, reviews, mentions). B2B vs. B2C aware scoring baseline. Link scan result to a real company profile if they continue past the free scan." This is genuinely a different category of work from everything else in Priority 3, for three concrete reasons, not just "it's more code":
1. **It's the only unauthenticated, public-facing surface in this entire app.** Every other page requires a real session. A free, no-login entry point is a different security/abuse posture (rate limiting, scraping-cost exposure) that hasn't been designed for anywhere in this codebase.
2. **"Public signal scraping" of third-party sites (reviews, social profiles) raises real ToS questions** — many review platforms and social networks restrict automated scraping in their own terms. This needs an explicit decision on what's actually fetched and how (a legitimate API vs. scraping) before writing code, not a default guessed silently.
3. **The scoring rubric ("B2B vs. B2C aware... what counts as insufficient differs by type") has no defined thresholds anywhere** — unlike every other benchmark in this codebase (Financial, Execution, Product all cite real external benchmark sources), there's no research backing this scoring model yet.

### Tender Readiness's procurement-answer generator + evidence-pack export (confirmed 2026-08-05, Priority 3 — both real open questions resolved and built)

Both design questions flagged earlier ("what does a procurement answer actually look like?", "what exactly goes in an evidence pack, and in what format?") were genuinely open — resolved with real answers, not guessed:

- **Procurement-answer generator** ([src/lib/modules/tender-readiness/procurement-categories.ts](src/lib/modules/tender-readiness/procurement-categories.ts), [procurement-answers.ts](src/lib/modules/tender-readiness/procurement-answers.ts)) — the Verumt/Flutteris/Legalithm exact formats were never found, but real, cross-source-validated question categories were: 11 curated, deterministic categories (compliance posture & risk classification, data governance & subject rights, model governance & version control, audit trail & logging, human oversight/HITL, incident & breach reporting, sub-processor disclosure, security certifications, data residency/cross-border, termination posture, ongoing review commitment), sourced from multiple independent 2026 AI procurement frameworks converging on the same structure — same "curated, not AI-generated" treatment as AI Reliability's self-test-prompts.ts; only the *answer* per category is drafted by the model, never the question itself. `generateAndPersistProcurementAnswers()` only runs against a request whose status is already `approved`/`sent` (same reasoning as AI Opportunity Synthesis: never draft from findings that might still change), sources answers only from reviewer-approved/edited findings, and constrains `regulationsCited` to the request's own already-computed applicable regulations — filtered defensively in code even after the LLM responds, same deterministic-citation pattern used everywhere else in this module. A new `procurement_answers` table (`supabase/migrations/20260804100000_procurement_answers.sql`, pushed live) reuses the `reviewer_status` enum and the Accept/Edit/Reject vocabulary (`acceptProcurementAnswer`/`editProcurementAnswer`/`rejectProcurementAnswer` in [src/lib/reviewer/module-workspace.ts](src/lib/reviewer/module-workspace.ts)) — deliberately its own table, not folded into `module_findings`, since Q&A shape genuinely differs from diagnosis/rootCause/recommendedAction/severity. Deliberately **not** part of `approveModuleRequest()`'s mandatory gate (answers don't exist until after that gate has already passed); their own review value is enforced at export time instead — only approved/edited answers appear in the evidence pack. Reviewer workspace UI ([src/app/(reviewer)/review-module/[requestId]/ModuleReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review-module/[requestId]/ModuleReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx>)) adds a Tender-Readiness-only section: a "Generate procurement answers" button when none exist, or the 11 answers with Accept/Edit/Reject once they do, reusing the same visual pattern as `FindingCard`/`EditForm`.
- **Evidence-pack export** ([src/lib/modules/tender-readiness/evidence-pack.ts](src/lib/modules/tender-readiness/evidence-pack.ts), `GET /api/tender-readiness/[requestId]/evidence-pack`) — per explicit direction, kept minimal: a Markdown structured document (company name, applicable regulatory frameworks, reviewer-approved/edited findings, reviewer-approved/edited procurement Q&A), not real PDF/zip rendering — no new dependency added. Real PDF/multi-format export is a confirmed, deliberately deferred follow-on, not silently dropped. Reviewer-only for now (session+role-checked route handler, same pattern as every reviewer Server Action) — no client-facing download surface exists yet for any module result (Reports & History already shows "Detail view coming soon" for modules), so this is real, deliberately-deferred follow-on scope too, flagged rather than silently expanded into.
- **Verified live end-to-end (2026-08-05)** against the real, already-approved `Nimbus Ledger Ltd` Tender Readiness request (`0e46e5dd-1596-468d-988e-2bcd1fb000ac`, the one already documented as fully verified earlier this session): generated 11 real procurement answers, all correctly citing only `eu_ai_act` (the one applicable regulation for this company) and honestly flagging insufficient evidence rather than fabricating practice detail where the underlying findings didn't support one; the duplicate-generation guard correctly blocked a second generate call; all three dispositions (Accept ×9, Edit ×1 with tightened wording, Reject ×1 with reviewer reasoning) persisted correctly. The evidence pack was then generated against the same request and correctly included only the two approved/edited findings and the 10 approved/edited procurement answers (the 1 rejected answer correctly excluded).
- **Real, pre-existing data-integrity bug found and fixed during this verification, not caused by this pass**: one of this request's `module_findings` rows had `reviewer_edited_content` set to a bare UUID string instead of an edited-finding object — leftover corrupted data from an earlier test pass (likely a caller argument mix-up during the original 2026-08-02 Tender Readiness verification), not written by any code in this session. Both `displayedContent()`/`displayedFinding()` (the reviewer workspace UI and the new evidence-pack export) were trusting `reviewer_edited_content ?? ai_draft` blindly, so the corrupted row rendered "undefined" for every field in both places — a real, live bug in the already-shipped reviewer workspace, not just a risk in the new export code. Fixed with a shape guard (`isValidEditedContent`/`isValidEditedContentShape`, requiring `title`/`diagnosis` to be strings) at both read sites, falling back to the always-valid `ai_draft` when the edited content is malformed, plus a write-time guard added to `editModuleFinding()` itself so this exact corruption can't be persisted again for any of the three modules. Re-ran the evidence-pack generation after the fix — the previously-"undefined" finding now renders its real content ("EU AI Act Risk Classification") correctly. The corrupted row itself was left as-is in the DB (now rendered safely by the fallback) rather than fabricating a guess at what the lost edited wording should have been — flagged here as a known historical artifact, not silently repaired.

## Reviewer authentication (confirmed 2026-08-02)

Real access control for the `(reviewer)` area — first item in the confirmed pre-launch order, ahead of the cron scheduler and the three modules, since real pilot data was about to touch a system with zero auth gate.

**Design**: a user is either `role = 'client'` or `role = 'reviewer'` on the existing `users` table (`supabase/migrations/20260802090000_users_role.sql`), not a separate roles table — matches the existing "one company per account" single-role-per-person design language for what is currently a two-value distinction. **Reviewer access is granted by data, never by code** — [scripts/grant-reviewer.ts](scripts/grant-reviewer.ts) (`npx tsx --env-file=.env.local scripts/grant-reviewer.ts <email> [--revoke]`) is the only provisioning path: creates the `auth.users` account if one doesn't exist yet (no email sent — the person proves ownership via the magic-link/code they request themselves), then upserts `role` on `public.users`. No reviewer email is hardcoded anywhere in the app, confirmed by design since the founder explicitly asked for this not to be a single-email special case.

**Sign-in is magic-link email, with a 6-digit code fallback** ([src/app/reviewer-login/](src/app/reviewer-login/), [src/app/auth/callback/route.ts](src/app/auth/callback/route.ts)) — no password ever handled by this app. `requestReviewerMagicLink` checks `role = 'reviewer'` via the admin client *before* calling `signInWithOtp` (caller has no session yet) and returns the same generic rejection whether the email doesn't exist or isn't a reviewer — deliberately not distinguishing the two. The code fallback (`verifyReviewerCode` → `supabase.auth.verifyOtp({ type: "email" })`) exists because live testing hit a real, well-documented failure mode: Gmail's automatic link-prescanning visited the magic link before the human clicked it, consuming the single-use PKCE token and surfacing as `otp_expired` — confirmed by checking the actual error, not assumed. The code sidesteps this since a scanner that visits a URL never submits a 6-digit code it doesn't see. **Requires the Supabase "Magic Link" email template to include `{{ .Token }}`** — Supabase always generates the code server-side regardless of the template, but won't display it unless the template does; this was a real dashboard-only gap (not something fixable from code) that the founder closed by editing the template directly.

**One more real, unrelated gap found live**: Supabase Auth has its own email rate limit, separate from Resend's — defaulted very low (hit `429 over_email_send_rate_limit` after a handful of test sends in one session). Raised to 100/hour in the dashboard (Authentication → Rate Limits) — worth knowing this exists as a real operational ceiling for reviewer volume, not just a testing artifact.

**Route + Server Action protection, both layers, not just one**: `(reviewer)/layout.tsx` gates page rendering (redirects unauthenticated visitors to `/reviewer-login`; shows an explicit "signed in but not authorized" message rather than a silent redirect loop for authenticated-but-wrong-role visitors, since bouncing an already-signed-in wrong account back to the login page is confusing, not helpful). Separately — and this was a real gap caught while building, not assumed safe — every exported Server Action in `(reviewer)/review/[reportId]/actions.ts` (`acceptFindingAction`, `editFindingAction`, `rejectFindingAction`, `resolveConflictAction`, `resolveDisputeAction`, `reRankTop3Action`, `approveReportAction`) now independently re-checks session + role via `getReviewerId()` before doing anything. Next.js Server Actions are independently reachable POST endpoints, not protected by the page-level layout gate that wraps their UI — only `approveReportAction` needed a reviewer id as a *value* (for `reviewed_by`), but all seven needed the *check*, since none of them had one before this pass.

**`REVIEWER_USER_ID` env-var stand-in fully retired**, not just in the one place it was flagged: `actions.ts`'s `getReviewerId()` now reads the real session (found and fixed the primary use). While removing the var, found a second, unrelated live usage in [src/lib/reviewer/notifications.ts](src/lib/reviewer/notifications.ts) — `checkAndNotifyClosedEditWindows()` was logging a notification to a single hardcoded reviewer id from the same env var. Fixed to query every `role = 'reviewer'` user and log one notification row per report per reviewer, since the founder explicitly wants multiple reviewers supported, not a single hardcoded one. `REVIEWER_USER_ID` removed from both `.env.local` and `.env.local.example` — `grep` confirms zero remaining code references.

**Verified live, end-to-end, real session** (2026-08-02, msally.abas@gmail.com): unauthenticated `/queue` → redirected to `/reviewer-login` ✓. Non-reviewer email → rejected with no email sent (confirmed via server logs — no `signInWithOtp` call made) ✓. Reviewer email → real magic link and code both delivered ✓. Code verification → real session established (`supabase.auth.verifyOtp` returned a valid session directly, confirmed via script before the UI attempt) ✓. Authenticated reviewer on `/queue` → real data rendered, header shows "signed in as msally.abas@gmail.com" ✓. Sign out → session cleared, redirected to `/reviewer-login` ✓. Post-sign-out → `/queue` blocked again ✓. **Not live-tested**: the layout's "authenticated but wrong role" denial branch — verified by code review and a clean build only, not click-through, since testing it would have needed either another full email round-trip or faking a session for a fake test account; the check itself (`profile?.role !== "reviewer"`) is a single deterministic condition, low risk to leave at code-review confidence.

## Production cron scheduler (confirmed 2026-08-02)

Second item in the confirmed pre-launch order. Deliberately scoped to two things the founder explicitly asked for — activating the notification check plus the AI Opportunity Synthesis fix — and the two dormant `scheduled_jobs` types with a real, spec'd concept (`re_audit_reminder`, `evidence_completeness_nudge`). `sprint_progress_checkin` and `monthly_retainer_refresh` are also in the `scheduled_jobs.job_type` enum but were deliberately NOT built — Execution Sprints and Monthly Execution Office have no application code at all yet (V2/V3 per the roadmap), so there's nothing for a scheduled check to do for them.

**Real gaps closed, found while building**, not anticipated upfront — see `supabase/migrations/20260802120000_scheduler_support.sql`:
- `ai_opportunity_synthesis` / `readiness_scores` were scoped only by `company_id`, never `report_id` — the exact same re-audit ambiguity bug `lens_findings` had before its own `report_id` fix. Both tables were still genuinely empty (`synthesizeAiOpportunities()` had never persisted anything, ever — it only returned a result object callers printed), so `report_id` was added `NOT NULL` directly, no backfill needed.
- `reports.evidence_sufficiency_by_lens` / `reports.governance_maturity_tier` — the exact persistence gap flagged during the 2026-08-02 end-to-end audit test. [run-audit.ts](src/lib/audit/run-audit.ts) now saves both directly on the `reports` insert (they're already fully computed by then — every lens promise has settled), not in a separate update.
- `reports.ai_opportunity_synthesized_at` — makes the cron's synthesis pass idempotent, same pattern as `reviewer_notified_at`.
- `app_settings` (plain key/value table, not a settings framework) — the founder explicitly wants the re-audit cadence adjustable without a redeploy. Two rows: `re_audit_reminder_days` (90) and `evidence_completeness_nudge_days` (7). No admin UI yet — edit via direct SQL until one exists. [src/lib/app-settings.ts](src/lib/app-settings.ts) reads them with a fallback if a row is ever missing.

**AI Opportunity Synthesis is now genuinely wired end-to-end** ([src/lib/synthesis/persist-ai-opportunity.ts](src/lib/synthesis/persist-ai-opportunity.ts), [src/lib/synthesis/run-pending-synthesis.ts](src/lib/synthesis/run-pending-synthesis.ts)): `runPendingAiOpportunitySynthesis()` finds every `status = 'approved'` report with `ai_opportunity_synthesized_at is null`, reloads the company/goal profile and the report's own persisted `evidence_sufficiency_by_lens`/`governance_maturity_tier` from the DB (first time this app has ever needed to reconstruct a `CompanyProfileForLens`/`GoalContext` from stored rows rather than receiving them as caller-supplied input — a real, new mapping layer, not reused from anywhere), loads the report's `approved`/`edited` findings, runs synthesis, and persists the result. **Confirmed live**: ran against report `d7c28f88` (approved 2026-08-01 during the closed-out audit test, never actually persisted until now) — 3 real opportunities written to `ai_opportunity_synthesis` with correct `report_id`, `readiness_scores` written, `ai_opportunity_synthesized_at` stamped; re-running immediately found zero pending work (idempotent, no duplicates). That report predates the `evidence_sufficiency_by_lens`/`governance_maturity_tier` persistence fix, so it synthesized with the correct null-safe fallback (`{}`/`null` → `data_quality: 0`, `governance_foundation: 0`) rather than the real computed values — expected for old data, not a bug. The field-population itself (the one-line addition to `run-audit.ts`'s insert) is type-checked but wasn't re-verified with a brand new full 5-lens run today, to conserve Groq quota after an already quota-heavy day.

**approveReport() deliberately does NOT call synthesis inline** — "regardless of timing or who approves" means this must fire reliably no matter when approval happened or which reviewer/session did it, so a cron-picked-up background pass (not something baked into the interactive approve request) is what actually makes that true. Coupling a Groq call to the approve button would also make approval latency/reliability depend on Groq being up, which the reviewer workspace shouldn't have to care about.

**Re-audit reminders** ([src/lib/reviewer/re-audit-reminders.ts](src/lib/reviewer/re-audit-reminders.ts)) — recurring, not one-shot: fires `re_audit_reminder_days` after the LAST reminder (or after delivery if none sent yet) for any company whose most-recently-created report is `sent`; a company with a newer report in any other status is mid-cycle and correctly skipped. **Confirmed live**: delivered report `d7c28f88` for real (`deliverReport()`), backdated its `delivered_at` 91 days, first run fired and logged a real `notifications` row (`recipient_type: client`, correct `recipient_id`), second run correctly found nothing (idempotent).

**Evidence-completeness nudges** ([src/lib/reviewer/evidence-nudges.ts](src/lib/reviewer/evidence-nudges.ts)) — per the founder's explicit answer, notifies BOTH the client (encourage completion) and every `role = 'reviewer'` user (visibility into a stalled submission) once a `pending` `evidence_submission` has had no new `evidence_files` activity for `evidence_completeness_nudge_days` (falls back to the submission's own `created_at` when it has no files yet — `evidence_submissions` has no `updated_at` column to check directly). **Confirmed live**: inserted a real test submission backdated 8 days, first run fired both notifications correctly (verified the reviewer one correctly targeted the real founder account, not a placeholder), second run found nothing (idempotent); test submission deleted afterward since it was synthetic, not a real event worth keeping (unlike the backdated report above, which is real delivered-report state, just with an artificially old timestamp — same pattern already established for testing `checkAndNotifyClosedEditWindows`).

**Single cron entry point** ([src/app/api/cron/tick/route.ts](src/app/api/cron/tick/route.ts), `vercel.json`) — Vercel Cron calls `GET /api/cron/tick` on a schedule with `Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET`, which Vercel sets automatically from the project's `CRON_SECRET` env var; the route rejects anything else (**confirmed live**: no header → 401). All four checks (`checkAndNotifyClosedEditWindows`, `runPendingAiOpportunitySynthesis`, `checkReAuditReminders`, `checkEvidenceCompletenessNudges`) run independently via `Promise.all` with per-check try/catch — same resilience principle as the 5 lenses (spec §2.1): one check failing must never block the others. **Proven live, not just designed for**: a transient Groq error inside the synthesis check on one tick did not prevent the other three checks from completing normally in the same request; retried standalone immediately after and it succeeded.
- **`vercel.json` schedule (`*/15 * * * *`) needs verification against the actual Vercel plan** — minute-level cron granularity is a Pro-tier feature on Vercel; Hobby is limited to coarser schedules. The edit-window notification check is the one genuinely time-sensitive piece here (spec §2.3a: "must fire the instant the 24h window closes"), so whatever the real plan supports should drive this number, not the placeholder used here.
- `CRON_SECRET` is set in `.env.local` for local testing (`curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET" .../api/cron/tick`) but **must also be set as a real Vercel project env var** before deploying — Vercel Cron reads it from there to build the header, not from this repo.

**Not yet done — full checklist audit against spec §5, confirmed 2026-08-03; Priority 1 (the minimal real client path) closed out 2026-08-04.** Every §5 checklist item was independently re-verified against actual code (not assumed from prior claims) after the founder correctly flagged this file had understated remaining scope. Headline finding at the time: **the reviewer-facing engine was real and largely verified; almost the entire client-facing product layer did not exist yet.** Since then, the minimal real client chain (auth → company/goal creation → evidence intake → client-facing report, with Privacy & Consent built in) has been built and live-verified end-to-end — see "Client authentication" above for the full writeup. The rest of the client-facing layer (Account Settings, full Business Profile fields, Dashboard, Reports & History list, Digital Presence Scan) remains Priority 3, genuinely not built. Full breakdown from the original audit, annotated where resolved:

*Backend/reviewer engine — BUILT & VERIFIED, confirmed again in this pass:* all 5 lenses, Conflict Detection, AI Opportunity & Readiness synthesis, the Financial Impact Engine's impact-band/confidence/assumptions schema, and the full Reviewer Workspace (view-by-lens, accept/reject/edit, conflict resolution, re-rank/fix-first, the mandatory approve gate, and time-per-audit instrumentation) — all confirmed live for the core audit pipeline specifically.

*Client-facing product layer — status as of the 2026-08-03 audit, since resolved for the Priority 1 slice (see "Client authentication" above for the full build+verification writeup, closed out 2026-08-04):*
- **Goal & Context Layer**: **RESOLVED for the minimal path** — real Company/Goal creation now exists (`src/app/onboarding/`), session-scoped, RLS-respecting. Still not built: urgency/top-concern beyond the one field collected at onboarding, and the full Business Profile field set (Priority 3).
- **Public Digital Presence Scan**: still entirely unbuilt — `digital_presence_scans` table exists in schema, zero application code references it anywhere. Explicitly flagged and deferred during the Priority 3 pass (2026-08-04) as genuinely bigger/more ambiguous than the rest of that list — see "Priority 3" above for the three concrete reasons why, not just "it's more code."
- **Business Profile**: **RESOLVED, 2026-08-04** — the full field set is now real, session-derived, and writes `company_profile_history` on change. See "Priority 3" above.
- **Account Settings, Reports & History, Dashboard**: **RESOLVED, 2026-08-04** — all three are now real, session-derived pages, verified live against real data. See "Priority 3" above for what's deliberately still not built within each (password/billing in Account Settings; module-result detail links in Reports & History).
- **Evidence Intake**: **RESOLVED for the fill-in-template path** — real forms exist at `src/app/evidence-intake/`, verified live against a real 5-lens audit run. Native CSV/PDF upload/parsing, known-source signature matching, and numeric metrics input remain a confirmed, deliberately deferred follow-on (see the Evidence Intake scope decision note above) — not silently dropped.
- **Client Output & Dashboard** (the client-facing report): **RESOLVED** — a real report view exists at `src/app/(app)/reports/[reportId]/`, verified live rendering real findings/top-3/roadmap for a real delivered report. Evidence library and next-step CTAs are not built yet — not part of what was verified.

*Partially built, thinner than the checklist implies:*
- **Notifications**: **RESOLVED, 2026-08-04** — real Resend sending is now wired end-to-end (see "Real email sending" above), verified with two real sends. `report_ready` is now a real, inserted-and-sent event type (`deliverReport()` logs it, the dispatcher sends it). `sprint_update` — **also now RESOLVED, 2026-08-06**, activated by the Execution Sprint Dashboard's check-in reminder (see "Execution Sprint Dashboard — built, 2026-08-06" below).

Execution Sprint Dashboard — **no longer in this list, see "Execution Sprint Dashboard — built, 2026-08-06" below**; it was wrongly reported here as V2/V3 scope in an earlier pass of this same checklist audit, caught and corrected the same day.
