## Onboarding wizard + goal definition wizard (confirmed 2026-08-05, pulled forward from V2)

Second explicit request this pass: pull three V2 items forward into V1 rather than wait. Two of them — "Cleaner onboarding polish" and "Goal definition wizard" — turned out to converge on the same underlying rebuild, plus closed a real spec/code gap found along the way: **"Multi-step guided workflow" was marked "moved to V1" in the spec doc's own V2-detail section, but was never actually implemented** (confirmed by grepping CLAUDE.md for any record of it — none existed; onboarding was a single flat form until this pass).

**Built as one multi-step wizard** ([src/app/onboarding/OnboardingWizard.tsx](src/app/onboarding/OnboardingWizard.tsx), replacing the old flat `OnboardingForm.tsx`) — 5 real steps (Company → Goal → Refine → Details → Review) with a progress indicator, per-step validation gating (can't advance without a company name or a selected primary goal), card-style goal selection with a one-line description per goal (new `GOAL_DESCRIPTIONS` in [src/lib/lenses/goals.ts](src/lib/lenses/goals.ts)), a "refine your goal" step with per-goal example-metric placeholder text (new `GOAL_METRIC_EXAMPLES`), and a review step before final submit.

**Real, previously-uncaptured `GoalContext` fields now actually collected**: `goals.secondary_goal`/`target_metric`/`time_horizon`/`success_definition` have existed in the schema since the original migration, but no UI ever wrote to them — only `primary_goal`/`urgency_level` were ever captured. `createCompanyAndGoal()` ([src/app/onboarding/actions.ts](src/app/onboarding/actions.ts)) now accepts and persists the full set. Reuses the existing `validateDesiredFutureState()` length/degenerate-input check (spec §1.9a) for the new free-text fields (`targetMetric`/`successDefinition`) rather than duplicating validation logic — it's generic text validation despite the name, never an AI judgment of answer quality, consistent with this codebase's established pattern. Deliberately does NOT touch `desiredFutureStatePrimary`/`Secondary` — that field keeps its own dedicated capture mechanism on the Business Profile page per spec §1.9a's explicit "one capture mechanism only" rule, not duplicated here.

**Verified live end-to-end (2026-08-05)** with a disposable test client account (`elvanis-test-onboarding-wizard-2026@gmail.com`, deleted afterward) driven through the real UI: all 5 steps rendered and behaved correctly (goal descriptions shown, the "refine" step's placeholder text correctly matched the selected goal's examples, the secondary-goal dropdown correctly excluded the already-selected primary goal, the review step correctly summarized every entered field), submission redirected to `/evidence-intake` as expected, and a direct DB read confirmed every field — `primary_goal`, `secondary_goal`, `target_metric`, `time_horizon`, `success_definition`, `urgency_level` — persisted exactly as entered. **One real, self-inflicted test-tooling snag along the way, not a product bug**: re-typing into an already-filled email field without clearing it first produced a mangled duplicate-text value, which correctly failed Supabase's email validation with a generic error — diagnosed by screenshotting the actual field contents rather than assuming a real backend fault, then retried cleanly.

## Deeper Commercial/Market lens (confirmed 2026-08-05, pulled forward from V2)

Third V2 item pulled forward this pass. Scoped deliberately: real, buildable-now analysis-depth improvements, not a fabricated "competitive benchmark library" — that specific V2 sibling item ("Benchmark rule library") is explicitly marked "grows with real cases" in the spec doc and stays genuinely deferred, per the standing rule against guessing at data-dependent features.

Two concrete, non-case-history-dependent improvements, both verified live:

1. **Search-query quality fix** ([src/lib/lenses/commercial-research.ts](src/lib/lenses/commercial-research.ts)) — closes a real gap flagged during the original 2026-07-31 build ("the broader-scan query is somewhat generic right now and sometimes surfaces generic marketing-blog content rather than genuine competitive intel"). The single generic "new competitors and market changes in X" broader scan is now two more targeted queries — one for competitive moves among established players (pricing/funding/positioning changes), one specifically for new entrants — plus `customerType` (already on `CompanyProfileForLens`, previously unused here) is now woven into query context alongside industry/businessModel for sharper targeting.
2. **Root-cause depth** (`commercial.ts` system prompt, new rule 8) — a competitive-pressure finding's `rootCause` must now explain *why* the dynamic is landing the way it is (e.g. reasoning about the client's own stated lack of differentiation) rather than restating that a competitor exists or is aggressive, but explicitly only when the evidence actually supports that depth — a thin evidence base should stay a thin, honestly-scoped finding, not get padded with invented strategic analysis.

**Verified live** with synthetic evidence (a client reporting real pricing pressure and lost deals citing "better value" with no other differentiator mentioned): the improved broader-scan queries returned more targeted, competitive-intelligence-flavored results than the old generic query; the deepened `rootCause` correctly and honestly diagnosed "the client's inability to differentiate themselves from [competitor] beyond price" grounded directly in the lost-deals evidence, not fabricated. `runCompetitorResearch()` still has no real caller in application code — Commercial's independent-research auto-trigger remains its own, already-flagged, genuinely deferred piece (see the Priority 1 writeup above), not silently expanded into here; this pass improved the function's own output quality, verified via the same direct-call pattern it's always been tested with.

## Fourth V2 pull-forward batch: saved draft intake, basic re-run, source-signature research, dormant similar-patterns (confirmed 2026-08-05)

Fourth batch of V2 items pulled forward this pass, plus three honest status checks requested before building anything (none of the three were built; correcting no discrepancy, confirming one real gap):

**Status checks, answered honestly:**
- **Recommendation library by issue type (seed version)** — spec §4a.2 marks it "seed version moved to V1, hand-built from your own domain expertise" — **but zero code references exist anywhere in `src/`, confirmed by grep.** A real gap between a spec decision and actual code, same class as the "Multi-step guided workflow" gap found and closed in the previous pass. Not built in this pass either (not requested) — flagged here so it doesn't silently stay unbuilt indefinitely.
- **Proposal/scope generator** — not built, correctly still marked V2 in the spec doc. No discrepancy.
- **Service Layer (Discovery/Delivery Session, Concierge tier, F2F Workshop)** — confirmed still fully unbuilt. `users.plan_tier` is displayed read-only in Account Settings and nothing else; no booking/scheduling UI, no tier gating, no session logic anywhere in `src/`.

**Saved draft intake** ([src/lib/evidence/draft.ts](src/lib/evidence/draft.ts), `evidence_intake_drafts` table) — targeted at Evidence Intake specifically (not onboarding, which is short enough that draft-saving matters less) since it's the longest, most-worth-pausing client-facing form (12 free-text fields across Financial/Execution/Product, 4 Commercial fields, plus AI & Governance). Debounced autosave (1.5s), pre-populated on page load, cleared on successful submission. **Verified live**: real save/load/clear round-trip confirmed correct under a real RLS-scoped session (not the admin client) — the actual `evidence_intake_drafts` policy (`company_id in (select id from companies where user_id = auth.uid())`) was exercised directly, not just code-reviewed. The full browser click-through UI (resume banner, "Draft saved" indicator) was verified via code review + clean lint/typecheck but not re-driven through a fresh browser round-trip this pass — a real Supabase per-email send-rate-limit was hit after several real magic-link sends earlier in this same session (confirmed: a direct `signInWithOtp()` call succeeded when the browser-triggered one didn't, isolating it to the rate limit, not a code bug) — same acceptable-risk precedent as other structurally-simple, already-proven UI patterns left at this confidence level earlier in this codebase's history.

**Basic re-run/refresh button** ([src/lib/audit/rerun-audit.ts](src/lib/audit/rerun-audit.ts)) — real prerequisite gap found and closed, not worked around: the fill-in-template evidence intake path had never persisted its raw evidence anywhere (confirmed by querying `evidence_submissions`/`evidence_fields` for real companies and finding zero rows, same gap already documented during the `goalRelevance` investigation) — there was nothing to actually "re-run" against. Closed via `reports.source_evidence_snapshot` (`supabase/migrations/20260805110000_reports_rerun_support.sql`) — the exact evidence payload a run was called with, stored verbatim; `submitEvidence()` now populates it. `rerunAudit()` reloads that snapshot plus the company's CURRENT profile/goal (never a cached copy — same "living record" principle as every lens prompt), re-executes all five lenses, and creates a new report in `pending_review`, linked via `rerun_of_report_id`. **Deliberately reviewer-triggered, not client self-serve** — spec's own free-tier rule ("re-audits are always paid") has no enforcement mechanism yet (no payment provider integrated anywhere in this codebase), so an unrestricted client button would silently create free unlimited re-runs; a reviewer-triggered version has no such collision and is still genuinely useful (refreshing findings after a prompt/schema fix, QA, reviewer judgment). A client self-serve version is real, deliberately deferred follow-on scope once billing exists — flagged here, not silently dropped. UI: a "Re-run analysis" section in the report review workspace, disabled with an honest message for any report that predates snapshot support. **Verified live end-to-end**: a real `runAudit()` call correctly persisted the snapshot; `rerunAudit()` against that report correctly re-executed all five lenses (5 findings, zero failed lenses) and correctly linked `rerun_of_report_id`; a synthetic report with no snapshot was correctly refused with a clear message rather than fabricating evidence to proceed.

**Improved source-signature detection, partial** (`supabase/migrations/20260805120000_export_source_signatures_seed.sql`) — real, researched signature data for all 7 named tools (Xero, QuickBooks, HubSpot, Salesforce, Jira, Intercom, Zendesk), sourced from each tool's own current help/developer documentation via live web search, not recollected from training data (export formats change over time — this codebase's standing rule is never to fabricate a concrete factual claim like a column header list). Honest about a real asymmetry found during research: Xero invoice export/import, Jira's CSV export, Zendesk's ticket export, and QuickBooks' checks import all have genuinely fixed, non-customizable schemas — `detectionConfidence: "high"` for those. HubSpot contacts, Salesforce report exports, and Intercom conversations are user-configurable at export time with no fixed column set — `detectionConfidence: "partial"` for those, with a deliberately smaller `requiredHeaders` subset rather than overclaiming a reliability these tools don't actually offer. The "learn from real messy uploads" half stays explicitly gated, per direction — no real client uploads exist yet to learn from, and no parsing/matching engine consumes this data yet (native file upload/parsing remains its own confirmed, deferred piece — see the Evidence Intake scope decision above). **Verified live**: all 7 rows confirmed persisted correctly via direct DB read, each with its real `detectionConfidence` and header data intact.

**Dormant similar-patterns-surfacing infrastructure** ([src/lib/synthesis/case-library.ts](src/lib/synthesis/case-library.ts)) — real, related prerequisite gap found and closed: the user's own framing ("same pattern as `case_library`: built now, activates automatically once real case volume exists") assumed `case_library` storage was already active — **it wasn't; zero write calls existed anywhere in the codebase**, despite spec §4a.3 claiming "storage happens from day one." Without storage actually running, retrieval would stay dormant forever, not just until real volume exists. Closed via `recordCaseLibraryEntry()`, called from `deliverReport()` (a delivered report is a genuinely completed audit case) — tags the entry with `goal:*`, `industry:*`, `stage:*`, and `lens:{lens}:severity:{critical|high}` for each non-rejected finding, `stored_for_retrieval: true` immediately (storage was always meant to be V1; only retrieval was V2-gated). `findSimilarPatterns()` is the retrieval half — deliberately conservative: computes Jaccard-style tag overlap against every OTHER company's stored cases, but returns `[]` entirely unless at least 3 genuinely distinct other companies show real overlap, same "never overclaim from thin data" discipline already used for every benchmark/jurisdiction/classification decision in this codebase. **Not wired into any client-facing (or reviewer-facing) UI yet** — per explicit direction ("never show a real client content derived from fabricated data as if genuine"), surfacing sparse early pattern data risks looking like a real insight when it's mostly noise; that's a separate, later decision once real case volume actually exists, not assumed here. **Verified live with synthetic data** (per explicit authorization — "use simulated/synthetic test data... that's fine and expected"): a real company with 0 other overlapping companies correctly returned `[]`; 3 synthetic companies created with 2 full-overlap + 1 partial-overlap cases correctly surfaced all 3, correctly sorted descending by similarity score (1.0, 1.0, 0.33); dropping back to 2 distinct companies correctly returned `[]` again, proving the floor is real and deterministic, not just present in the common case. All synthetic test data (companies, users, case_library rows) deleted afterward.

## Fifth V2 pull-forward batch: Recommendation library, Service Layer, module_requests RLS fix, similar-patterns UI, finalized pricing, test-as-a-stranger pass (confirmed 2026-08-06)

Fifth batch pulled forward this pass, plus a full internal-stranger verification and two schema/security closures. PR #2 (the fourth batch) was merged to `main` by the user before this batch started; work here is on a fresh branch (`module-rls-fix-and-pricing-batch`) off `main`.

**Recommendation library by issue type, seed version** ([src/lib/recommendations/recommendation-library.ts](src/lib/recommendations/recommendation-library.ts)) — closes the real gap flagged in the previous batch (spec §4a.2 said "seed version moved to V1," zero code existed). 16 curated, hand-written entries across all 5 lenses (thin margin, low runway, high customer concentration, decision latency, missing financial visibility, PR-pickup delay, low feature adoption, high churn signal, low NPS, competitive pricing pressure, lost-deal pattern, weak differentiation, no AI governance ownership, no incident response process, unmanaged vendor/model risk, missing human oversight) — same "curated, not AI-generated" treatment as `self-test-prompts.ts`/`procurement-categories.ts`; only the finding *matching* is code, never the recommendation text itself. `matchRecommendationLibraryEntries(lens, title, diagnosis)` is deterministic keyword-substring matching, never LLM-judged — consistent with every other "don't let the model decide something code can decide" fix in this codebase. Wired into the reviewer workspace's `EditForm` as a "Suggested playbook" box shown when a finding's title/diagnosis matches an entry.

**Real bug found and fixed during build, same class as every prior false-positive**: the initial `thin_margin` entry matched on the single word `"margin"`, which also matched a *healthy* margin finding (76% vs. 70% floor), incorrectly suggesting a "fix thin margins" playbook on good news. Same root cause as the `directly_supports`/`directly_affects` schema gaps — a generic signal standing in for a directional one. Fixed by switching to specific multi-word phrases (`"margin below"`, `"thin margin"`, `"margin compression"`, `"margin erosion"`, `"eroding margin"` — never bare `"margin"`); the same audit was applied preemptively to `low_feature_adoption` (removed the direction-agnostic `"below what we'd expect"`). **Verified live** via a direct script: 5 test cases including the exact healthy-margin case that broke it — 0 false matches post-fix, correct matches on the genuine cases.

**Service Layer: Discovery/Delivery Session, Concierge tier, F2F Workshop** ([src/lib/service-layer/](src/lib/service-layer/), `session_requests` table) — closes the "confirmed still fully unbuilt" gap flagged in the previous batch's status check. Built as a **request-and-human-follow-up mechanism, deliberately not a fake booking/payment flow** — no calendar integration, no payment provider exists anywhere in this codebase, and fabricating either would violate the standing "never fake functionality" rule. `requestSession(companyId, sessionType)` ([src/lib/service-layer/session-requests.ts](src/lib/service-layer/session-requests.ts), session-scoped client, real `"use server"` file) inserts a `session_requests` row (`discovery`/`delivery`/`f2f_workshop`) and logs a `session_requested` notification (new enum value, `supabase/migrations/20260806100500_notification_event_type_session_requested.sql`) for every `role = 'reviewer'` user — reuses the existing dispatch pipeline, no new send path. `<SessionRequestButton>` ([src/app/_components/SessionRequestButton.tsx](src/app/_components/SessionRequestButton.tsx)) is the shared client component, placed on Evidence Intake (Discovery, pre-submission, never required) and the client Report view (Delivery — post-report only, per spec §1.9; F2F Workshop — only rendered once a Delivery Session has already been requested, since it's explicitly an upgrade *of* that session, never offered independently or before evidence exists). Reviewer Queue gets a new "Session requests" panel (list + Mark scheduled/completed/Decline buttons, [src/app/(reviewer)/queue/page.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/queue/page.tsx>)/[actions.ts](<src/app/(reviewer)/queue/actions.ts>)). `plan_tier` (`free`/`concierge`) is now a real, settable field, not just displayed: a badge + dropdown in the Reviewer Workspace header ([ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review/[reportId]/ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx>)) calling `setPlanTier()` ([src/lib/service-layer/plan-tier.ts](src/lib/service-layer/plan-tier.ts)) — reviewer-set, not client-self-serve, since there's no billing to gate it against yet; this is the "deeper reviewer attention" signal becoming visible for the first time, per the original Service Layer design note ("currently policy, not an enforced/visible signal").

**Verified live end-to-end** (see the stranger pass below for the full walkthrough): Discovery Session button confirmed working pre-submission; Delivery Session button confirmed working on a real delivered report; F2F Workshop confirmed correctly HIDDEN before any Delivery Session request existed, then confirmed correctly APPEARING immediately after one was made; plan-tier badge/dropdown confirmed to persist to the real `users.plan_tier` column via direct DB read.

**`module_requests` RLS gap fixed at the schema level** (`supabase/migrations/20260806090000_module_requests_rls_fix.sql`) — closes the gap flagged during the Priority 3 Reports & History build ("`module_requests`' own RLS policy is looser than `reports`'... a schema decision deserving its own confirmation, not a silent side effect of building a list page"). `module_requests`/`module_findings`/`procurement_answers` policies changed from `for all using (owner)` to `for select using (owner and status = 'sent')`, exactly mirroring `reports`' own established client-facing policy. Safe to tighten with zero functional risk — confirmed by grepping every write site in `persist.ts`/`module-workspace.ts` first and verifying every actual write already goes through the admin/service-role client, never the session client the old looser policy was protecting. **Verified live**: a real RLS-scoped session query for a non-`sent` module request now correctly returns zero rows; a `sent` one still returns correctly.

**Similar-patterns UI, surfaced in the Reviewer Workspace** ([src/app/(reviewer)/review/[reportId]/page.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review/[reportId]/page.tsx>)) — the dormant infrastructure built last batch (`findSimilarPatterns()`) now has a real caller and a real UI section ("Similar patterns across other companies"), reviewer-only (never client-facing — showing a client another client's cross-company data would be a real leak, not a UX choice). Confirmed dormant/empty as expected against real current case volume (fewer than 3 distinct overlapping companies exist in the live DB), matching the function's own designed floor — not a bug, the correct behavior until real volume exists.

**Finalized v1 launch pricing** (docs §1.5, rewritten) — confident v1 launch numbers, not yet pilot-validated, explicitly flagged as such in the spec doc: Standard Core Audit free; Concierge £300 (bundles Discovery + Delivery); Execution Sprint £3,000; Tender Readiness £2,500; AI Reliability Audit £2,000; Data Protection Compliance £2,000; F2F Workshop £750 add-on; Monthly Execution Office stays a placeholder (£500/month, explicitly not finalizable without repeat-client signal).

**Digital Presence Scan dead-code sweep** — grepped the full codebase for any UI/route referencing the never-built feature; none found (the table exists in schema only, as already documented). No changes needed, confirmed clean rather than assumed.

**Hardcoded-URL audit ahead of the `app.elvanis.com` cutover** — grepped for literal `localhost`/Vercel-URL strings outside `.env*`/config; the only production code path building a URL is `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` (already env-driven, defaults to `http://localhost:3000` locally per its original design). No hardcoded assumption found that would break on the domain switch.

**Full test-as-a-stranger pass — completed 2026-08-06**, using disposable accounts (`elvanis-test-stranger-pass-2026@gmail.com` client, `elvanis-test-stranger-reviewer-2026@gmail.com` reviewer) with no foreknowledge shortcuts — real UI clicks, real typed evidence, not pre-seeded data. This tests **whether the system works**, not whether it's intuitive to someone who's never seen it (that's the still-open external-pilot gap below) — an explicit scope distinction confirmed before starting. Verified live, in-browser, in order: full 5-step onboarding wizard (company "Riverbank Analytics Ltd", goal Cash Flow/Margin Efficiency) → Discovery Session button → saved-draft autosave AND resume-after-reload (screenshotted proof) → a real audit submission using the evidence actually typed in-character (report `f3356934-9e52-460c-bd4b-b2f02594634d`) → full reviewer pass as the disposable reviewer (plan-tier badge, Edit form with recommendation-library suggestion confirmed showing no false match, Accept ×4, Approve) → report delivery → the delivered report rendered correctly as the client (top-3, 30/60/90 roadmap, all 4 findings) → Delivery Session button → F2F Workshop's conditional gating (hidden → shown, confirmed both states) → Reports & History correctly listing the delivered report → Dashboard correctly rendering the same real top-3/roadmap data.

**Two real, non-app findings from this pass**: (1) the recommendation-library false-positive bug above, caught here rather than in isolated testing — proof the pass surfaces real issues, not just a formality; (2) a **stale-cookie diagnostic correction** — a "Couldn't send the login link" error appeared in-browser while a direct script call to the same `signInWithOtp()` succeeded moments later; earlier sessions this year attributed similar errors to Supabase's rate limit, but this one was isolated to stale cookies left over from earlier cross-account testing in that specific browser tab (confirmed: a fresh tab succeeded immediately with the identical email). Worth knowing both failure modes exist and look similar — check cookies/tab state before assuming rate-limit.

**One deliberate deviation from pure browser-only testing, disclosed rather than silently smoothed over**: environment flakiness in the browser tool itself (empty `read_page` results, `(0,0)` clicks, scroll timeouts — confirmed via screenshots to be tool-side, not app bugs, and fixed each time by opening a fresh tab) meant two steps used a direct script call instead of a literal click-through: the evidence submission itself (loaded the real draft data the stranger had typed via the real browser session, then called `runAudit()` directly — `submitEvidence()`'s session-scoped client needs real Next.js request context a standalone script can't provide) and `deliverReport()` (no reviewer-workspace "Deliver" button exists yet — a pre-existing, already-documented gap, not new). Every other step, including both logins, both dashboards, both session-request buttons, and the full reviewer decision flow, went through real browser clicks.

**Test data retention decision**: left in the database as real proof, not deleted — same precedent already established for "Nimbus Ledger Test Co" and other genuine end-to-end verification artifacts this session (not synthetic scratch debris). Deleting either disposable account risked cascading through real FK references this proof run created (`reports.reviewed_by`, `lens_findings` dispositions) — safer and more consistent to leave both intact, flagged here explicitly rather than assumed silently either way.

## Hardcoded-values audit + lens benchmarks/governance dimensions migrated to DB (confirmed 2026-08-06)

A full audit of the codebase for values that "would need to change based on real pilot learnings or a business decision, without a code deploy" (same rigor as the earlier full checklist audit) — reported first, migrated only after explicit confirmation, per direction. Findings and disposition:

1. **Financial/Execution/Product lens benchmarks — migrated.** Their own docblocks already said "explicitly a starting point, not permanent" — the clearest case of the three, and the one place a stale hardcoded threshold silently biases every audit.
2. **Recommendation library — confirmed for migration, not yet done.** Queued as the next piece — real complication flagged: `matchRecommendationLibraryEntries()` is called inside a client component on every keystroke, so migrating it needs the same server-fetch-then-prop refactor pattern proven here for `GOVERNANCE_DIMENSIONS`, not a drop-in swap.
3. **AI Reliability self-test prompts + Tender Readiness procurement categories — left hardcoded, confirmed correct.** These should change when the underlying real-world facts change (a new documented failure pattern, a new procurement-standard convergence), not from a business/pilot decision — code is the right place for them, same reasoning as the jurisdiction logic.
4. **`EDIT_WINDOW_HOURS` — left hardcoded, confirmed correct for now.** Real correctness tie to client-facing SLA copy; moving it needs its own deliberate decision, not a side effect of this audit.
5. **`GOVERNANCE_DIMENSIONS` — migrated alongside item 1**, treated as a close cousin (same "explicitly provisional" language, same audit test).

**Test suites written BEFORE migrating, not after** (explicit precondition, closing the exact "claimed tested, never actually committed" gap CLAUDE.md's own Product-lens entry flagged from an earlier pass) — [financial-benchmarks.test-cases.ts](src/lib/lenses/financial-benchmarks.test-cases.ts) (18 cases), [execution-benchmarks.test-cases.ts](src/lib/lenses/execution-benchmarks.test-cases.ts) (25 cases), [product-benchmarks.test-cases.ts](src/lib/lenses/product-benchmarks.test-cases.ts) (37 cases), [ai-governance-framework.test-cases.ts](src/lib/lenses/ai-governance-framework.test-cases.ts) (15 cases) — same committed, runnable-via-`npx tsx`, exit-non-zero-on-failure pattern as `jurisdiction.test-cases.ts`. Every tier boundary explicitly exercised (the value just below, at, and just above each threshold). 95/95 passing. Writing these required first making the comparison/scoring functions pure and parameterized (`compareFinancialMetric(benchmarks, metricKey, value)` instead of reading a module-level const) — a real prerequisite refactor, not a shortcut around it.

**Schema** (`supabase/migrations/20260806120000_lens_benchmarks_and_governance_dimensions.sql`) — the richer per-boundary shape confirmed over a flat key/value copy of `pricing`, since several source objects hold range/tuple boundaries (e.g. `pr_cycle_time_hours.goodRange`) that don't fit one flat value per metric: `lens_benchmarks` (lens, metric_key, metric_label, boundary_key, value, unit — one row per individually-named threshold, 50 rows total) and `governance_dimensions` (dimension_key, label, source, sort_order, 4 level descriptions — 7 rows). Seeded with the exact values already documented in the `DEFAULT_*` constants, which remain in code as both the fallback and the seed data's source of truth. No admin UI built for either table this round (not requested for these — only pricing got one) — same "edit via direct SQL until one exists" precedent already set by `app_settings`, flagged explicitly rather than silently deferred.

**Loaders** ([src/lib/lenses/benchmarks-repository.ts](src/lib/lenses/benchmarks-repository.ts)) — `loadFinancialBenchmarks()`/`loadExecutionBenchmarks()`/`loadProductBenchmarks()`/`loadGovernanceMaturityTierBoundaries()`/`loadGovernanceDimensions()`, explicit (metric_key, boundary_key) → field mapping per lens (not a generic reflection-based reshape) — verbose but auditable, and a typo'd DB row is simply ignored (falls back to the default for that one field) rather than silently corrupting an unrelated one. Every loader falls back to the `DEFAULT_*` constant on any read failure or incomplete row set — a DB hiccup must never take down a lens run. `GovernanceDimensionKey` stays a fixed TS union (structural identity, not tunable content) — only the label/source/level-description text and the maturity tier point-boundaries are DB-loaded; the zod schema for the LLM's per-dimension score output uses the static `GOVERNANCE_DIMENSION_KEYS` list, not the loaded array, so it can stay a module-level const.

**Lens files rewired** (financial.ts/execution.ts/product.ts/ai-governance.ts) — the module-level `SYSTEM_PROMPT` const (which called `format*ForPrompt()` synchronously at import time) became a per-call `buildSystemPrompt(benchmarks)` function, since the prompt can no longer be pre-computed once at import time now that its data is DB-loaded — same "living record, never a cached copy" principle already applied to the company profile. `ai-governance.ts`'s two modes each now `await Promise.all([loadGovernanceDimensions(), loadGovernanceMaturityTierBoundaries()])` once at the top.

**Client-component wiring for `GOVERNANCE_DIMENSIONS`** — it was directly imported into `EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx` (a client component) for the questionnaire-mode maturity dropdowns, which broke once it became an async DB read. Fixed the same way pricing's data flows to a page: `evidence-intake/page.tsx` now calls `loadGovernanceDimensions()` server-side and passes the result down as a `governanceDimensions` prop.

**Verified live, 2026-08-06**: all 4 test suites re-run clean after the full wiring (95/95). A direct regression script confirmed all 5 loaders return data that exactly deep-equals the corresponding `DEFAULT_*` constant (proving the seed data and field-mapping have zero drift, not just that nothing crashes). A live DB-edit-and-revert test (temporarily changed `gross_margin_percent.concerning_below` from 60→55 directly in the DB, confirmed `loadFinancialBenchmarks()` picked it up, reverted, confirmed it reflected the revert) proved the actual "admin-adjustable, not a constant" promise end-to-end, not just the happy path. A real Groq call to `financialLens.runDraft()` with 65% gross margin evidence correctly produced a "Gross Margin Below Healthy Range" finding against the DB-loaded 70% threshold — full proof of the LLM-calling path, not just the deterministic layer. The equivalent live Groq call to `aiGovernanceLens.runDraft()` initially hit Groq's daily token quota (95,995/100,000 used) — disclosed rather than hidden at the time — and was retried successfully once quota reset: 7 real findings correctly generated from a questionnaire scored `[1,0,1,0,1,0,1]`, and `computeOverallMaturity()` correctly summed to 4/21 → "nascent" tier (≤6), confirming both `loadGovernanceDimensions()` and `loadGovernanceMaturityTierBoundaries()` work correctly through a real end-to-end LLM call, not just the regression script. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout.

## Recommendation library migrated to DB (confirmed 2026-08-06)

Second item migrated in the hardcoded-values audit, following the lens benchmarks — same test-first discipline, same defensive-fallback loader pattern, and the same class of client-component refactor already proven for `GOVERNANCE_DIMENSIONS`.

**Test suite written before migrating** ([recommendation-library.test-cases.ts](src/lib/recommendations/recommendation-library.test-cases.ts), 21 assertions) — a positive match per entry (all 16), cross-lens isolation (a keyword match under the wrong lens must not surface), a no-match case, result ordering by match count, and — deliberately included, not left implicit — regression cases for the two real false-positive bugs already caught live in an earlier pass (bare `"margin"` matching a healthy margin finding; the direction-agnostic `"below what we'd expect"` phrase). Two of the initial test cases were themselves wrong on first run (a "no match" fixture that accidentally contained the keyword `"runway"`, and a sort-order fixture where both candidate entries tied at the same match count) — caught by running the suite before wiring anything else, fixed in the test file, not the code, since the code's behavior was correct both times.

**Schema** (`supabase/migrations/20260806130000_recommendation_library.sql`) — flat one-row-per-entry shape (`issue_type_key` PK, label, lens, `keywords text[]`, `recommended_action_template`, rationale), unlike `lens_benchmarks`' per-boundary rows — each library entry is naturally a single unit with no internal sub-boundaries, so pricing's flat-row shape fit better here than the richer benchmark shape. Seeded with the exact 16 entries from `DEFAULT_RECOMMENDATION_LIBRARY`, which remains the fallback + source of truth.

**Refactor** ([recommendation-library.ts](src/lib/recommendations/recommendation-library.ts)) — `matchRecommendationLibraryEntries()` now takes the library as its first parameter instead of reading a module-level const, same pure/parameterized pattern as `compareFinancialMetric()` etc. **Loader** ([repository.ts](src/lib/recommendations/repository.ts)) — `loadRecommendationLibrary()`, same defensive fallback to `DEFAULT_RECOMMENDATION_LIBRARY` on any read failure or incomplete row set.

**Client-component wiring, the real work** — `matchRecommendationLibraryEntries()` is called inside `EditForm`, a client component nested in `ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx`, synchronously on every render as the reviewer types (a hot path, unlike `GOVERNANCE_DIMENSIONS`' one-time form-load read) — this was flagged as the reason this migration wasn't a drop-in swap when first queued, and that held up: `review/[reportId]/page.tsx` now calls `loadRecommendationLibrary()` once server-side and threads it down as a `recommendationLibrary` prop through `ReviewWorkspaceClient` into `EditForm`, so the client-side matching still runs entirely in-memory against the prop array — no live DB calls during typing, just like before.

**Verified live, 2026-08-06**: the test suite passes (21/21) after the full wiring; `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and `npm run build` all clean. A regression script confirmed `loadRecommendationLibrary()` exactly matches `DEFAULT_RECOMMENDATION_LIBRARY` (order-independent, since the DB doesn't guarantee row order). Live in the actual reviewer workspace, on the exact real finding that originally exposed the false-positive bug (`Gross Margin Health`, 76%, report `c442e9c8-...`): clicking Edit showed no suggestion box — the fix holds through the DB path, not just the hardcoded one. Then, in the same live session, edited the diagnosis text to include "margin below... margin compression" and confirmed the "Suggested playbook" box appeared with the correct DB-loaded `thin_margin` content — proving the positive-match path is genuinely wired to the database, not silently still reading the old constant. Edit was cancelled without saving; confirmed via direct DB read that both real findings remained untouched (`draft`, no edited content).
