## Per-tool export instructions in Evidence Intake (confirmed 2026-08-06)

Delivers most of the ease-of-use benefit of native file parsing without needing real sample files to build against — full auto-parsing (reading a client's actual export columns and mapping them automatically) stays a separate, later piece per the Evidence Intake scope decision above; this only guides the client to the right figures for the already-built fill-in template.

**[src/lib/evidence/export-instructions.ts](src/lib/evidence/export-instructions.ts)** — short, tool-specific "where to click" steps for the same 7 known tools as `export_source_signatures` (Xero, QuickBooks, HubSpot, Salesforce, Jira, Intercom, Zendesk), grouped by which lens each tool is relevant to (Financial: Xero/QuickBooks; Commercial: HubSpot/Salesforce; Execution: Jira; Product: Intercom/Zendesk) — a client-facing hint, not the field-mapping data `export_source_signatures` holds for the future parsing engine. **Curated, not AI-generated**, same treatment as `self-test-prompts.ts`/`procurement-categories.ts`/`recommendation-library.ts`. Navigation paths were researched fresh against each tool's own current help docs via live web search (not recalled from training data) — same non-fabrication discipline already applied to the export-signature research, since menu paths go stale the same way export column formats do (confirmed one real asymmetry during this research: QuickBooks Online genuinely cannot export Profit & Loss directly to CSV, only Excel — noted as a caveat rather than glossed over).

**Wired into [EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx](src/app/evidence-intake/EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx)** as a collapsed-by-default `<details>` disclosure ("Using one of these tools? Quick export steps") at the top of each of the four relevant lens sections (Financial/Execution/Product/Commercial) — no extra client-side state needed, and it doesn't add visible length to an already-long form for anyone who doesn't need it. Still pure evidence submission: the client reads the figure off their own export and types it into the existing free-text field themselves — this removes the "where do I even find this" friction, not the typing.

**Verified live (2026-08-06)** against a real client session (the disposable stranger-pass account, company Riverbank Analytics Ltd): all four lens sections render their hint box, expanding the Financial one showed both Xero and QuickBooks with the correct steps and the QuickBooks CSV caveat, confirmed via both `get_page_text` and a screenshot.

## EDIT_WINDOW_HOURS migrated to DB + the real confirmation modal finally built (confirmed 2026-08-06)

Reconsidered from the hardcoded-values audit's original "leave hardcoded for now" call — the earlier concern (client-facing SLA copy could drift out of sync with the enforced value) turned out to be solvable, not a real blocker, and closing it surfaced a bigger, real gap along the way.

**The real finding, surfaced before building anything**: the instruction to "make the confirmation modal's text read the DB value" assumed a confirmation modal already existed in the live app. It didn't. Spec §2.3a's modal (*"Ready to submit? You'll have 24 hours to edit..."*) was only ever built in the demo prototype (explicitly mock data, never connected to Supabase) — the real `src/app/evidence-intake/EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx` submitted directly behind the privacy checkbox with zero SLA disclosure. Confirmed and flagged before proceeding, not silently worked around: **every real client who has submitted evidence through this app so far did so without ever seeing the 24h/72h SLA terms.** Confirmed to build the real modal now, as part of this same task.

**`edit_window_hours` setting** (`supabase/migrations/20260806140000_edit_window_hours_setting.sql`) — reuses the existing `app_settings` table and `getSettingNumber()` helper (same shape as `re_audit_reminder_days`), not a new table — a single scalar value doesn't need `lens_benchmarks`' richer per-boundary machinery. `run-audit.ts`'s `EDIT_WINDOW_HOURS` constant is gone; `editWindowClosesAt` is now computed from `await getSettingNumber("edit_window_hours", 24)`. The reviewer-facing `blockedReason` string in `approveReport()` (`workspace.ts`) — previously a hardcoded "24h" describing a value that's now variable — was fixed the same way, for the same reason: leaving it static would have reintroduced the exact copy/value divergence this migration exists to close, just on the reviewer side instead of the client side.

**The real modal** (`EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx`) — form submit now opens a confirmation modal instead of submitting directly; the actual `submitEvidence()` call moved to a new `handleConfirmSubmit`, fired only from the modal's own Confirm button. Copy: *"You'll have {editWindowHours} hours to edit or add evidence — after that, review begins, and your report will be ready within {editWindowHours + REVIEW_PERIOD_HOURS} hours total."* — both numbers computed from the same `editWindowHours` prop (fetched server-side via `getSettingNumber()` in `evidence-intake/page.tsx`, never a separately hardcoded string), so they cannot diverge from what `run-audit.ts` actually enforces. `REVIEW_PERIOD_HOURS` (48) stays a fixed in-code constant, deliberately not migrated alongside — it has no enforcement mechanism anywhere in code (confirmed narrative-only earlier this file), so DB-backing it now would let the modal promise a number nothing actually holds to; migrating it is its own future decision, paired with building real enforcement.

**Also went beyond the spec's own illustrative modal text, deliberately**: the spec's example line ends "*This will use your free audit.*" as static copy. Given the entire point of this task was eliminating copy that could be wrong, that specific sentence was made a real, computed check instead — `evidence-intake/page.tsx` now queries whether the company has any already-`sent` report (RLS-scoped to the session, the same `sent`-only policy the client report view already relies on) and the modal shows *"This will use your free audit"* or *"This is a paid re-audit"* accordingly, never assuming the happy path.

**Verified live, 2026-08-06**, real client session (the disposable stranger-pass account, company Riverbank Analytics Ltd, which already has a delivered report from the earlier stranger pass): clicked the real checkbox and Submit button through the actual UI — the modal appeared with *"You'll have 24 hours to edit or add evidence — after that, review begins, and your report will be ready within 72 hours total. This is a paid re-audit."* — correctly dynamic and correctly honest (this account is not free-tier eligible). Then, to prove the value is genuinely live and not cached at build time, temporarily changed `edit_window_hours` to 48 directly in the DB, reloaded, and reopened the modal: it correctly read *"48 hours... 96 hours total"* — recomputed from the changed value with no code change. Reverted to 24 afterward, confirmed reflected. Cancelled both times without submitting; no test evidence was actually sent through the pipeline. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout.

**One disclosed browser-tool issue during this verification, not an app bug**: `computer{action:"screenshot"}` returned a blank white image regardless of actual page content in one tab, and scroll actions repeatedly timed out — the same class of tool-side desync documented earlier in this session. Confirmed non-app by cross-checking against a freshly opened tab, where screenshots rendered correctly immediately. Worked around by reading real element refs via `read_page` and clicking by ref (which resolves via the DOM, not stale screen coordinates) rather than relying on visual scroll+coordinate clicking.

## Deliberately flagged, not built this pass — real gaps confirmed from the checklist status check (2026-08-06)

Three honest status checks requested before any further work, answered directly against the actual code, not the docs:

- **Execution Sprint Dashboard** — **correction, 2026-08-06: this status check was wrong, and got corrected the same day.** Zero references to `execution_sprints`/`sprint_tasks` in `src/` was accurate, but "correctly still V2/V3 scope" was not — the founder caught that this had been explicitly moved to V1 early in this build ("needed to actually deliver the paid Execution Sprint, a real revenue offer"), a decision this audit failed to check against before reporting it as settled scope. See "Execution Sprint Dashboard — built, 2026-08-06" below for the full corrected build.
- **Evidence library (what was submitted, re-upload option) on the client report** — **confirmed missing at the time, then built, 2026-08-06.** See "Evidence library on the client report — built, 2026-08-06" below for the full writeup; this line is left in place as the historical record of the gap being flagged before it was closed.
- **The merge-and-complete evidence page** — confirmed correctly dormant, not a gap. `src/lib/evidence/intake.ts` is still the original Phase 2 stub (`createEvidenceSubmission()` throws "not yet implemented") and was never wired to anything; the real live evidence intake (`src/app/evidence-intake/`) is fill-in-template only — confirmed by checking for `<input type="file">` in `EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx` (none exists). Since there's only ever one evidence source right now, there is nothing to merge. This becomes a real, necessary piece the moment native file upload/parsing gets built — which stays confirmed fully deferred, per explicit direction, no further scoping needed at this time.

## Execution Sprint Dashboard — built, 2026-08-06

**The correction that reopened this feature**: this file's own 2026-08-06 checklist status check reported the Execution Sprint Dashboard as "correctly still V2/V3 scope, no code expected yet" — the founder caught that this was wrong: the spec doc's own V2-detail section already said "→ moved to V1 — needed to actually deliver the paid Execution Sprint, a real revenue offer," a decision this session's own audit failed to check before reporting it as settled. With the Execution Sprint now DB-backed-priced at £3,000 and sellable today, the real gap — nothing existed to actually deliver it if someone paid — was real and worth closing. Verified against the spec doc directly before scoping anything further, per the standing "never unilaterally decide a feature is out of scope" rule.

**Final, consolidated design (confirmed 2026-08-06, superseding two earlier scoping rounds in this same conversation)**: not a dev/Agile sprint — the paid implementation engagement itself, a bounded 2–4 week effort to fix ONE specific finding from an approved audit. Not an ongoing management relationship or embedded-CPO arrangement — a defined, time-boxed deliverable.
- **Entry point**: reviewer-triggered, from an approved report's selected finding. No in-app checkout — payment confirmed externally first, same pattern already used for Concierge/F2F Workshop.
- **Task creation**: AI-drafted from the selected finding, reviewer runs the same Accept/Edit/Reject mandatory-gate pass used everywhere else in this app — the sprint isn't client-visible until every task has a disposition.
- **Owner field**: simple free-text role label (Tester, Developer, PM, etc.), not a multi-user/permissions system.
- **Locked plan, once approved**: task description/owner/KPI target/due date are reference, not client-editable directly. A client wanting a change submits a note; it lands in the reviewer queue (reused infrastructure, not a new one) and the reviewer replies by email (existing Resend integration).
- **Status** (not started/in progress/done) is freely client-editable, no review needed — genuinely neutral reporting.
- **KPI actuals** are freely client-enterable, but a significant deviation from target (deterministic threshold check, never AI-judged) automatically surfaces to the reviewer queue via the same mechanism as the plan-change notes, different trigger — "a struggling KPI shouldn't go unnoticed until the end of the sprint." No separate AI-flagging engine, no dedicated audit-trail table — the note/queue item itself is the log.
- **Completion**: client signoff required, no auto-complete. A "gone quiet" reminder reuses the dormant `sprint_progress_checkin` job-type concept; never auto-closes. On signoff, the reviewer adds commentary — that commentary IS the final report, not a separately AI-generated document.
- **Explicitly out of scope**: sub-tasks/dependencies, multi-assignee, file attachments, comment threads beyond the plan-change notes, time-hours logging, drag-drop reordering, templates, in-app payment.
- **Three confirmed implementation details**: KPI-deviation replies send immediately, not on the next cron tick (the one deliberate exception to the "log now, dispatch on cron" pattern used everywhere else — a struggling implementation shouldn't wait up to 15 minutes for a reviewer response); KPI schema is `kpi_description` (text) + `kpi_target_value`/`kpi_actual_value` (numeric) + `kpi_direction` (`higher_is_better`/`lower_is_better`, set by the reviewer at task approval) rather than free text, so the deviation check can be genuinely deterministic; the deviation threshold is `app_settings`-backed, admin-adjustable, seeded at 20%, same pattern as every other benchmark in this build.

**Schema** (`supabase/migrations/20260806150000_execution_sprint_dashboard.sql`, plus three small follow-up migrations below) — `execution_sprints` gains `signed_off_at`/`reviewer_commentary`/`last_client_activity_at`; `sprint_tasks`' original free-text `kpi_target`/`kpi_actual` columns are replaced with the structured `kpi_description`/`kpi_target_value`/`kpi_target_value`/`kpi_direction` triple plus an immutable `ai_draft` jsonb snapshot and a `reviewer_status` (reusing the existing `reviewer_status` enum — draft/edited/approved/rejected, same vocabulary as every other review surface). A new `sprint_queue_items` table is the one shared mechanism for both client change-request notes (`trigger_type: 'client_note'`) and deterministic KPI-deviation alerts (`trigger_type: 'kpi_deviation'`) — deliberately not two separate tables, per the confirmed simplification. RLS on `execution_sprints`/`sprint_tasks` tightened from `for all` to `for select` (matching the earlier `module_requests` RLS-tightening precedent) since every real write goes through the admin client with an explicit field whitelist — Postgres RLS is row-level only and can't enforce "status/kpi_actual_value are editable but task_description/owner/target/due_date are locked," so that lock is enforced at the server-action layer instead. `app_settings` gets `kpi_deviation_threshold_percent` (seeded 20) and, via a later follow-up migration, `sprint_progress_checkin_days` (seeded 7). `notification_event_type` gains `sprint_queue_item`/`sprint_signed_off`/`sprint_reply`, and the original schema's long-dormant `sprint_update` value is activated for the first time for the check-in reminder.

**AI task-drafting** ([src/lib/execution-sprint/draft-tasks.ts](src/lib/execution-sprint/draft-tasks.ts)) — `draftSprintTasks()` takes the selected finding + company/goal context, uses `generateValidatedJson` (mandatory, same as every lens) against a zod schema (`taskDescription`, `ownerRoleLabel`, `kpiDescription`, `kpiTargetValue`, `kpiDirection`, `suggestedDueDaysFromStart` 1–28), curated system prompt rules keeping the output genuinely bounded to a 2–4 week engagement.

**Reviewer workspace** ([src/lib/execution-sprint/workspace.ts](src/lib/execution-sprint/workspace.ts)) — `createSprintFromFinding()` verifies the finding is reviewer-approved/edited and the report is approved/delivered, reconstructs the current company/goal profile (same "living record, never cached" pattern as `rerun-audit.ts`), drafts tasks, persists them in `draft` status. `acceptSprintTask`/`editSprintTask`/`rejectSprintTask` mirror `module-workspace.ts` function-for-function. `approveSprintTasks()` is a real mandatory gate — blocked with a `blockedReason` until every task has a disposition, verified live both ways (blocked with 3 undecided tasks, then succeeded once resolved) — and on success stamps `start_date`/`target_end_date` (28 days out) and computes each surviving task's `due_date` from its own preserved `ai_draft.suggestedDueDaysFromStart`. `createSprintQueueItem()` is the one shared insert-and-notify-every-reviewer helper for both trigger types. `replyToSprintQueueItem()` is the one deliberate immediate-send exception in this codebase — confirmed live at 787ms end-to-end (insert → resolve → real Resend send → `sent_at` stamped), not waiting for the next cron tick. `addSprintReviewerCommentary()` only succeeds once `signed_off_at` is set.

**Entry point wired into the core Reviewer Workspace** ([src/app/(reviewer)/review/[reportId]/actions.ts](<src/app/(reviewer)/review/[reportId]/actions.ts>), [ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review/[reportId]/ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx>)) — a "Start an Execution Sprint" section, visible once the report is approved/sent, lists approved/edited findings with a per-finding button that creates the sprint and navigates straight to its review workspace.

**New reviewer workspace** ([src/app/(reviewer)/review-sprint/[sprintId]/](<src/app/(reviewer)/review-sprint/[sprintId]/>)) — mirrors the module-review workspace's Accept/Edit/Reject/mandatory-gate pattern, flat (one task list, no lens grouping), plus a "Client signed off" panel with the commentary textarea once signoff has happened.

**New client-facing page** ([src/app/(app)/execution-sprint/[sprintId]/](<src/app/(app)/execution-sprint/[sprintId]/>)) — the locked plan fields render read-only; only the status dropdown and KPI-actual input are live inputs, both calling server actions ([actions.ts](<src/app/(app)/execution-sprint/[sprintId]/actions.ts>)) that re-verify company ownership via the session-scoped client before writing an explicitly whitelisted set of columns via the admin client. `updateKpiActualAction()` runs the deterministic `deviationPercent()` check inline and calls `createSprintQueueItem()` when the deviation exceeds the admin-configured threshold. A "Request a change" note form and a "Sign off on this sprint" button (only shown while `in_progress`) round out the page; once `complete`, the page shows the signoff date and the reviewer's final commentary as the "Final report."

**Dashboard tile** ([src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx>)) — an "Active Execution Sprint" section showing the finding title, `done`/total task count, and target end date for the company's current `in_progress` sprint, linking to the full sprint page.

**Reviewer queue integration** ([src/app/(reviewer)/queue/](<src/app/(reviewer)/queue/>)) — a new "Execution Sprint queue" panel lists open `sprint_queue_items` (both trigger types) with an inline reply form calling `replyToSprintQueueItemAction`; sprints still in `scoped` status (drafted but not yet reviewer-approved) also appear in the main unified queue list alongside Core Audit reports and module requests, linking to `/review-sprint/[id]`.

**Check-in reminder** ([src/lib/execution-sprint/progress-checkins.ts](src/lib/execution-sprint/progress-checkins.ts)) — `checkSprintProgressCheckins()` activates the long-dormant `sprint_progress_checkin` job-type concept and the `sprint_update` notification event type (both existed in schema, unused, since the very first migration). "Quiet" is measured from `last_client_activity_at` (touched by every client-facing sprint action), falling back to `start_date` when never touched; recurring and idempotent per cadence window, same pattern as `checkReAuditReminders`. Wired into the single cron entry point ([src/app/api/cron/tick/route.ts](<src/app/api/cron/tick/route.ts>)) as a 6th independent check.

**A real bug found and fixed during live verification, not anticipated upfront**: the reviewer queue's new "Execution Sprint (task review)" panel queried `execution_sprints.created_at` to sort/display newly-scoped sprints — but the original schema never gave `execution_sprints` a `created_at` column at all (unlike almost every other table in this app), a gap `tsc`/`npm run build` couldn't catch since Supabase's generated types aren't statically checked here. Surfaced as a real `"column execution_sprints.created_at does not exist"` error on the actual live `/queue` page, not caught earlier. Fixed with a proper new migration (`20260806170000_execution_sprints_created_at.sql`) — never editing the already-pushed `20260806150000` migration in place, consistent with this codebase's standing rule that an applied migration is immutable and any follow-up fix needs its own file (the same discipline already applied earlier in this same build when `sprint_tasks` was found missing `created_at` too, closed via `20260806150500_sprint_tasks_created_at.sql`).

**Verified live, end-to-end, real browser sessions (2026-08-06)**, reusing the disposable stranger-pass client (`elvanis-test-stranger-pass-2026@gmail.com`, company Riverbank Analytics Ltd) and reviewer (`elvanis-test-stranger-reviewer-2026@gmail.com`) accounts already established this session:
- Seeded two real sprints from real approved findings on the real delivered report (`f3356934-...`) via direct calls to `createSprintFromFinding`/`approveSprintTasks` (the same "reviewer-side proven via direct function call, client-side proven via real browser" split already used for every other reviewer-only backend function in this codebase) — one exercising all three dispositions (accept/edit/reject) plus the mandatory-gate pass, one specifically proving the gate blocks with undecided tasks and then succeeds once resolved.
- **Every client-facing interaction driven through real browser clicks**, not scripted: signed in via the real `/client-login` UI, changed a task's status (confirmed persisted via direct DB read), entered a KPI actual that deviated 44.4% from target — confirmed the exact deterministic note text and the correct reviewer notifications fired — submitted a "request a change" note on a second task, confirmed both queue items rendered correctly with their trigger-type-specific copy on `/queue`, replied to the KPI-deviation item as the reviewer through the real form (confirmed `sent_at` stamped 787ms after `created_at` — genuinely immediate, not cron-dispatched), reloaded the client page and confirmed the reply displayed correctly, confirmed the Dashboard tile rendered the real in-progress sprint with the correct task-completion count, signed off through the real "Sign off on this sprint" button, added final reviewer commentary through the real reviewer-workspace textarea, and confirmed the client page correctly displayed it as "Final report" once `complete`.
- **The mandatory-gate denial branch, confirmed live, not just designed for**: `/review-sprint/[sprintId]` correctly rejected a wrong-role (client) session with "does not have reviewer access," and the client execution-sprint page correctly rejected a wrong-role (reviewer) session with "does not have client access" — both cookie-shared-session denials happened naturally mid-verification (switching between client/reviewer logins in the same browser profile overwrites the session cookie) and both fired correctly rather than leaking content.
- **The check-in reminder, verified via direct function call** (same reasoning as every other cron-check verification in this codebase — this is a scheduled background function, not something a browser click exercises): backdated a real in-progress sprint's `start_date` 8 days, `checkSprintProgressCheckins()` correctly fired once and logged a real `sprint_update` notification, an immediate second run correctly found nothing (idempotent).
- **One disclosed browser-tool desync during this pass, not an app bug**: `read_page` intermittently returned empty trees or `(0,0)` viewports on tabs that had been open for several navigations — the same class of tool-side flakiness already documented earlier in this file, confirmed non-app each time by opening a fresh tab (which always worked immediately) and cross-checking with `get_page_text`/screenshots. One click (the client "Sign off" button) required computing real screen coordinates via `getBoundingClientRect()` through `javascript_tool` (read-only inspection, not used to alter app behavior) after `read_page`/`find` both failed to surface the button's ref, then clicking via real coordinates — confirmed to have actually worked via a DB read immediately after, not assumed.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, both before and after the `created_at` bug fix.

## Evidence library on the client report — built, 2026-08-06

Closes the last remaining item from the "genuinely still deferred" list flagged during the earlier checklist audit ("low priority, but worth building before real pilots") — the client report page had no "what you submitted" view and no re-upload affordance anywhere.

**Design, kept deliberately small per explicit direction**: reads `reports.source_evidence_snapshot` — the exact evidence payload a run was called with, stored verbatim since the basic re-run/refresh button work (2026-08-05) — rather than building any new capture/storage mechanism. "Re-upload" does **not** mean a new submission pathway or a literal file-upload widget (this app has no file upload anywhere yet, per the standing Evidence Intake scope decision) — it's a link to the already-existing, already-verified `/evidence-intake` flow, which already correctly handles "new evidence after a delivered report starts a new paid re-audit cycle" (the confirmation modal there already computes free-vs-paid correctly, see "EDIT_WINDOW_HOURS migrated to DB..." above). Reusing that flow outright, rather than building a second evidence-submission surface, was the actual scope-reduction that kept this small.

**Shared field-label extraction** ([src/lib/evidence/field-sets.ts](src/lib/evidence/field-sets.ts)) — the Financial/Execution/Product field key→label→placeholder list previously lived only as a local const inside `EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx` (a client component). Extracted to a framework-agnostic shared module so the report page (a server component) can render the exact same human-readable labels against the snapshot's `fieldName`s without duplicating the list or reaching across the client/server boundary — `EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx` now imports from here too, so the two can never drift.

**Client report page** ([src/app/(app)/reports/[reportId]/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/reports/[reportId]/page.tsx>)) — a collapsed-by-default `<details>` "Evidence submitted" section (same disclosure pattern already used for the export-instructions hints on the intake form) at the bottom of the page, showing every Financial/Execution/Product field with its label and either the submitted value or an honest "Not provided," the Commercial self-report (named competitors, market/pricing/lost-deals notes), and AI & Governance (live-AI-in-production yes/no, then either the governance-documentation description text or the 7 governance-dimension scores — whichever mode the client used — with real DB-loaded dimension labels via `loadGovernanceDimensions()`, not raw keys). Defensively handles older reports that predate snapshot support (`source_evidence_snapshot` null) by simply omitting the section, same pattern `rerunAudit()` already uses for the identical case. A "Submit new evidence" link sits in the existing session-request button row at the top of the page.

**Verified live (2026-08-06)** against the real delivered Riverbank Analytics Ltd report (`f3356934-...`, the same one used throughout the Execution Sprint verification): the section correctly rendered the real submitted Financial text verbatim — matching, word for word, the evidence the Financial lens findings above it were actually drafted from (68% gross margin, 14 months runway, hosting/support costs creeping up) — and correctly showed "Not provided" for every Execution/Product/Commercial field and AI & Governance dimension this account genuinely left blank. The "Submit new evidence" link was clicked live and correctly landed on the real `/evidence-intake` page (which even correctly resumed a real saved draft left over from earlier testing, proving the existing draft mechanism and this new link compose cleanly). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout.

## Real landing page built + visual design audit (reported, not fixed) + domain timing confirmed, 2026-08-06

Three things requested together: a visual design audit (same "report findings before fixing" discipline as the UX review), a real landing page (genuinely new — nothing built so far served this purpose), and a domain-cutover timing decision explicitly flagged as the founder's call, not something to guess at.

**Visual design audit — reported, deliberately not fixed except where inseparable from the landing page build:**
- **`src/app/page.tsx` was still the untouched `create-next-app` scaffold** — Next.js logo, "Deploy Now"/"Documentation" links to Vercel/Next.js's own sites, "To get started, edit the page.tsx file." Confirmed live in the browser before this change — this was the actual public root of the site.
- **Every page's browser tab said "Create Next App"** — root layout `metadata` was never touched (`title: "Create Next App"`, `description: "Generated by create next app"`). Confirmed live across pages throughout this entire session's history.
- **No logo, wordmark, or brand color anywhere in the codebase** — `globals.css` defines only `--background`/`--foreground`, no accent/brand token exists at all. `public/` still holds the five default Next.js SVGs (`next.svg`, `vercel.svg`, `file.svg`, `globe.svg`, `window.svg`), unused by the real app. No favicon. The client-login screen (screenshotted) has zero visual identification of the product/company.
- **A real, separate bug: the loaded font is never applied.** `layout.tsx` correctly loads Geist Sans/Mono via `next/font/google` and wires the CSS variables — but `globals.css`'s `body` rule hardcodes `font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif` directly instead of referencing `var(--font-sans)`/the `font-sans` utility. Confirmed live via computed styles: the whole app renders in plain Arial, the Geist font is downloaded and never used.
- **What's genuinely good**: the internal app pages (dashboard, reports, evidence intake, reviewer queue) share a real, consistent grayscale system — the same `neutral-200`/`neutral-800` borders, the same rounded/padding rhythm, the same button pattern, correctly repeated across dozens of files. Not randomly inconsistent — a coherent minimal-utilitarian look with zero brand identity layered on top. Professional-adjacent, not distinctly Elvanis, and doing none of the visual trust-building a page asking for real financial data would benefit from.
- **Deliberately not fixed**: the font bug, favicon, brand color, logo, and any internal app page styling all stay open findings pending explicit go-ahead, same discipline as the UX review batch. Fixing the site-wide `<title>`/description was treated as inseparable from the landing page task itself (a landing page can't credibly launch still titled "Create Next App") and done as part of that build, not smuggled in as a separate design fix.

**Real landing page built** ([src/app/page.tsx](src/app/page.tsx)) — replaces the default scaffold entirely. Headline + subhead stating the actual value prop (goal-driven audit, top-3 bottlenecks with financial impact, 30/60/90 roadmap), "Start your free audit" CTA routing to `/client-login` (not directly to `/onboarding` — a cold visitor needs to authenticate first regardless, and `/onboarding` already redirects an unauthenticated visitor to `/client-login` itself, so this is the honest first step, not a shortcut), a five-lens explainer section (Financial/Commercial/Execution/Product/AI & Governance, one real sentence each, drawn from how each lens actually works, not generic marketing copy), a "how it works" 3-step section, a trust section naming the real AI provider (Groq) and the mandatory human-review gate, with links to the real `/privacy` and `/terms` pages, and a second bottom CTA. Deliberately kept in the same grayscale, no-brand-color visual language already used throughout the rest of the app rather than introducing a new color scheme unprompted — that's a real open question from the design audit above, not something to decide unilaterally while building this page. If a brand color gets chosen later, this page should be revisited to match.

**Verified live**: root layout metadata fix confirmed via the actual browser tab title (was "Create Next App," now "Elvanis — Know your top 3 execution priorities"), full page content confirmed via screenshot and page-text read, and the "Start your free audit" CTA clicked through to confirm it correctly lands on the real `/client-login` page (the same real, already-proven magic-link/code sign-in flow, not a stub). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and `npm run build` all clean — `/` now correctly builds as a static route.

**Domain cutover timing — confirmed 2026-08-06, founder's explicit decision, not guessed**: stay on the Vercel URL through pilot testing and the visual design pass; cut over to `app.elvanis.com` only once a real external pilot user has completed the flow and the design pass has landed. Real fact surfaced in this exchange, not assumed: `app.elvanis.com` currently serves the **old** Elvanis app, but has **no real live users on it today** — so the eventual cutover is a low-risk, straightforward DNS repoint whenever the team is ready, not something blocked on migrating real users off the old domain first.

## Honest UX review pass — 10 findings, all fixed and verified live, 2026-08-06

Explicitly requested as a UX quality review, not functional verification — "is the evidence intake template and the 'how do I get my report' flow actually clear and easy to use for a genuine first-time user, not just working correctly." Read the actual rendered pages/screenshots and source copy for six specific questions (field-label jargon, QuickBooks caveat visibility, export-hint noticeability, delivery-email clarity, report scannability, next-step CTA framing), reported a prioritized list of 10 concrete findings — none fabricated, every one grounded in real code/copy/screenshots — then the founder asked for all 10 fixed as one batch, worked through in priority order, each verified live. This is a genuinely different category from every other pass in this file: not "does it work," but "is it clear to someone who's never seen it" — the closest internal proxy available for the still-open no-real-external-pilot-user gap below, though explicitly not a substitute for it.

**Fix immediately — real bugs, not polish:**
1. **72-hour hardcoded/DB divergence on the "report is being reviewed" holding page** — `REVIEW_PERIOD_HOURS` extracted from a local const in `EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx` to a shared [src/lib/reports/sla.ts](src/lib/reports/sla.ts) (`getTotalTurnaroundHours()`), so the holding page (`reports/[reportId]/page.tsx`) now reads the same DB-backed `edit_window_hours`-derived total the confirmation modal already computed, instead of a separately hardcoded "72 hours" that would silently go stale the moment a reviewer changed the setting. Also fixed the copy itself while touching it — "Check back soon" never mentioned the client would get an email, a real gap surfaced by fix #5 below. **Verified live**: `getTotalTurnaroundHours()` called directly, confirmed `{editWindowHours: 24, totalHours: 72}` by default, `{48, 96}` after temporarily changing the DB setting, reverted — same numbers already proven for the confirmation modal, now provably shared, not just presumed shared.
2. **QuickBooks export caveat visibility** — the CSV/Save-As step was a separate `note` rendered in `text-neutral-400` (the single faintest text color on the page) after a step ("Export to Excel") that reads as complete on its own; a real user following it literally would stop there. Folded into one continuous required sentence in [export-instructions.ts](src/lib/evidence/export-instructions.ts) instead. **Verified live via screenshot** — the full sentence now renders in legible `text-neutral-700` inside the restyled tip box (see #10).
3. **"Insufficient evidence" findings visually indistinguishable from real findings** — used the existing `isMissingDataFinding` structural flag (already on every `LensFinding`, no schema change needed) to give these a dashed-border, neutral-background card with a "No evidence submitted" badge instead of a severity badge, on the client report page. **Verified live** against a real report (Nimbus Ledger Test Co) with a genuine mix of both — missing-data findings render visibly distinct from genuine ones.

**Real business gap — needed a design decision, got one, built it:**
4. **Execution Sprint had zero client-facing entry point** — a client reading their #1 priority finding had no way to signal they wanted help implementing it (sprint creation stays reviewer-triggered, no in-app checkout exists). Built [sprint_interest_requests](supabase/migrations/20260806180000_sprint_interest_requests.sql) — deliberately its own small table, not folded into `session_requests` (that table models scheduling a live call; this is interest in a specific finding, not a call). New [SprintInterestButton](src/app/_components/SprintInterestButton.tsx) shown inline on findings with `severity` critical/high and `!isMissingDataFinding` — same "high-priority" threshold `isFixFirstCandidate()` already uses elsewhere, never on missing-data findings (nothing to implement when the gap is "you didn't submit evidence"). New reviewer queue panel ("Execution Sprint interest") lists open requests with a link straight into `/review/[reportId]` and a Dismiss action — doesn't create the sprint itself, the reviewer still uses the existing "Start an Execution Sprint" entry point. **Verified live end-to-end**: signed in as a real client with a genuine `high`-severity, non-missing-data finding (Nimbus Ledger Test Co, "No Risk Classification Awareness"), clicked the real button, confirmed a real `sprint_interest_requests` row + two real reviewer notifications, confirmed the request appeared correctly on `/queue`, dismissed it as the reviewer through the real form, confirmed `status: 'resolved'` with the correct `resolved_by`.

**UX quality fixes:**
5. **Thin notification email templates** — every client-facing template was one generic sentence plus a link to `/reports` (the list page, never the specific report), no company name, no hint at contents, no reminder this app is passwordless. Added `notifications.related_report_id` ([migration](supabase/migrations/20260806190000_notifications_related_report_id.sql), populated by `deliverReport()` and `checkReAuditReminders()`). `report_ready` now links directly to the specific report and pulls the REAL top-3 finding titles (not generic boilerplate — "hint at what's inside" is more honestly satisfied by real content than canned copy, consistent with this codebase's standing non-fabrication discipline); `re_audit_reminder` references the real prior delivery date; both, plus `evidence_incomplete`, get a company-name greeting and a passwordless-login reminder. Reviewer-facing templates left untouched — that wasn't the finding. **Verified live with a real send**: inserted a real `report_ready` notification with `related_report_id` set to the Nimbus Ledger Test Co report, ran `sendPendingNotifications()`, confirmed `sent_at` stamped (the company-name lookup and top-3 title lookup both executed without error against real data). One unrelated pre-existing `@example.com` test-debris notification failed in the same batch (same reserved-domain rejection already documented elsewhere in this file) — deleted, not a regression.
6. **No severity color-coding** — critical/high/medium/low all rendered as identical small gray uppercase text. Added a real `SEVERITY_STYLES` color map (red/orange/amber/neutral) on the client report page. **Verified live** on real findings across both severity tiers present in test data.
7. **Next-step CTAs (Delivery Session, F2F Workshop, Submit new evidence)** — three real problems, not one: shown before any report content, above every finding; zero framing text explaining what a Delivery Session or F2F Workshop actually is (unlike Discovery Session, which already had one); "Submit new evidence" (starts a new paid cycle) carried identical visual weight to "book a call." Fixed by making `SessionRequestButton` self-contained — it now renders its own framed box with a one-line description per session type (and spells out "Face-to-Face (F2F) Workshop" instead of bare "F2F"), removing the now-duplicate bespoke wrapper that used to live only on the evidence-intake page. Moved the whole "Next steps" section to the bottom of the report page, after every finding. "Submit new evidence" is now a plain text link with its own cautionary sentence ("this starts a new, paid re-audit cycle — your free audit has already been used," computed as a logical certainty from the page already showing a `sent` report, no extra query needed), not a bordered button matching the session-request buttons. **Verified live** — screenshot and page-text confirmed the reordering, the framing copy, and the visual distinction.
8. **Top-3/by-lens verbatim repetition** — the Top 3 section showed `recommendedAction`, then the by-lens section repeated the *exact same sentence* under its own "Recommended:" label. Top 3 now shows `diagnosis` (the observation) instead, cleanly separating "what's the headline" from "what should I do," which now only appears once. **Verified live** — real report text confirmed no duplicate sentences between the two sections.
9. **Plain-language field-label fixes** — "runway" now explained inline ("your current spending rate (your \"runway\")") rather than assumed known; "NPS" spelled out on first mention ("Net Promoter Score (NPS)"); "Activation / onboarding notes" (label) and "first value" (placeholder) — jargon stacked on jargon — reworded to "Onboarding notes" / "get set up and start actually using it"; dropped the technical "(comma-separated)" instruction from the competitors field in favor of a natural placeholder example. All in [src/lib/evidence/field-sets.ts](src/lib/evidence/field-sets.ts), shared with the report page's evidence-submitted display so the two can't drift. **Verified live via screenshot.**
10. **Export-hint box indistinguishable from disclaimer boxes** — previously styled identically (same gray border/background) to the two inert notice boxes above it (Discovery Session offer, privacy notice), training the eye to skim past all three as boilerplate. Restyled with a distinct blue accent and a "TIP" badge. **Verified live via screenshot** — visibly distinct from the neutral-gray boxes above it.

**Scope note, disclosed rather than assumed**: this pass improves *clarity and honesty of what's already built* — it is explicitly not, and cannot be, a substitute for the one gap flagged below (no real external pilot user). A founder who already knows what every field means cannot fully evaluate whether the plain-language rewrites actually land for someone who doesn't.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, both incrementally per fix and in one final full pass.

## Final brand identity applied — charcoal + amber, pilot-ready (confirmed 2026-08-07)

Closes the open findings from the visual design audit above (no logo/favicon/brand color anywhere, the font-application bug, the default "Create Next App" title) — the founder made the final color decision and asked for it applied consistently across the whole app, not just the landing page, plus the font/favicon/title fixes done alongside it. Explicitly requested as a review checkpoint, not an auto-approved change — reported back with live screenshots before being called pilot-ready, same discipline as the UX review batch.

**Palette, final, not a placeholder**: primary/dark `#2C2C2A` (charcoal — headers/nav/dark surfaces), accent `#BA7517` (amber — primary buttons/links/key CTAs, paired with `#412402` text for contrast on amber backgrounds), layered on top of the existing `#F1EFE8`-family neutral-gray system rather than a full repaint.

**Architectural approach — recolor the palette once, hand-pick the CTAs** ([src/app/globals.css](src/app/globals.css)): rather than touching every file that already uses `bg-neutral-*`/`text-neutral-*`/`border-neutral-*` (and their `dark:` variants — hundreds of usages across the codebase, per the earlier audit's own observation that internal pages already share "a real, consistent grayscale system"), the entire Tailwind `neutral-*` scale is remapped at the theme level via Tailwind v4's CSS-first `@theme` block — `neutral-100` is now the exact `#F1EFE8` base, `neutral-900` is the exact `#2C2C2A` charcoal. Every existing `bg-neutral-900` nav/dark-surface usage, every `border-neutral-200`, every `dark:text-neutral-400` picked this up automatically, zero per-file risk, single source of truth. New `accent`/`accent-hover`/`accent-ink` tokens were added for the amber CTA color — that pairing (which specific neutral-900 usage is a CTA vs. a nav background) can't be inferred from an existing color name, so those needed real per-file edits, done as a separate, deliberate pass.

**CTA sweep — 24 primary-button instances across 16 files**, swapped from black/white (`bg-neutral-900 ... text-white ... dark:bg-white dark:text-neutral-900`) to amber (`bg-accent ... text-accent-ink hover:bg-accent-hover`, dark: overrides dropped since accent stays constant across themes) via exact (file, line) targeting — pre-verified with `grep -n` before running `sed`, not a blind global substring replace, to avoid corrupting unrelated `bg-neutral-900` usages that should stay neutral (nav bars, card dark-mode backgrounds). Covers: primary submit buttons on Business Profile, Account Settings, client-login, reviewer-login, Evidence Intake, Sprint/Module review workspaces, both module intake forms (Data Protection, Tender Readiness), the Reviewer Queue pricing panel, AI Reliability intake, the core Reviewer Workspace, the Onboarding wizard, and the demo prototype. Session-request buttons ([SessionRequestButton.tsx](src/app/_components/SessionRequestButton.tsx)) got solid amber; the smaller inline Execution Sprint interest prompt ([SprintInterestButton.tsx](src/app/_components/SprintInterestButton.tsx)) deliberately got an amber *outline* style instead — a lighter-weight treatment appropriate for a secondary CTA embedded inside a finding card, not a full-bleed button.

**Deliberate exception, not an oversight**: the "Submit new evidence" link on the client report page ([src/app/(app)/reports/[reportId]/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/reports/[reportId]/page.tsx>)) stays a plain neutral underlined link, not amber — preserving the UX-review batch's own deliberate decision (fix #7) to keep it visually lower-weight than "book a call" buttons, since it triggers a paid re-audit cycle and shouldn't compete visually with genuine CTAs.

**Charcoal nav applied to both authenticated shells** — `(app)/layout.tsx` (all four client-facing pages) and `(reviewer)/layout.tsx` (internal reviewer tooling) both got the same `bg-neutral-900` nav bar with an "Elvanis" wordmark added before the existing nav/email content, plus `hover:text-neutral-50` on nav links and sign-out buttons for legibility against the dark surface — the highest-leverage single change for "consistent across the internal app," since these two layouts wrap every authenticated page in the product.

**Landing page** ([src/app/page.tsx](src/app/page.tsx)) — full-bleed charcoal header (deliberately outside the constrained content column, so it reads as a real site header), amber CTAs (hero + bottom), amber left-border accent on the five-lens cards, amber step numbers in "How it works," amber inline links in the trust section, and a solid charcoal bottom-CTA block replacing the previous plain section.

**Favicon** ([src/app/icon.svg](src/app/icon.svg)) — new file, auto-detected by Next.js App Router (no manual `<link>` needed): a rounded charcoal square with an amber serif "E" monogram, replacing the absent favicon the design audit flagged.

**Font bug fixed** ([src/app/globals.css](src/app/globals.css)) — `body`'s hardcoded `font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif` (the real bug the earlier audit found: Geist Sans/Mono correctly loaded via `next/font/google` and wired as CSS variables, but never actually referenced) now reads `var(--font-sans), Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif` — Geist is the real active font now, Arial stays only as the fallback chain's last resort, not the active choice.

**Title fix** — root layout `metadata` (already corrected during the landing-page build, confirmed again here) reads "Elvanis — Know your top 3 execution priorities," not the default "Create Next App," across every page.

**Verified live with real screenshots, not assumed, across both light and dark color schemes**:
- Landing page (`/`) — hero, five-lens section, and bottom CTA all screenshotted in light mode (charcoal header, cream background, amber CTAs/borders/links, charcoal bottom block) and re-screenshotted in dark mode (dark page background, white heading, amber CTA still legible) — correct in both.
- `/icon.svg` — renders correctly, charcoal square with amber "E."
- `/client-login` — cream background, amber "Send sign-in link" button, correct.
- `/dashboard` (real client session) — charcoal nav bar with wordmark and real data, correct.
- `/reports/[reportId]` (real delivered Nimbus Ledger Test Co report) — charcoal nav, cream content, amber-toned severity badges, dashed "NO EVIDENCE SUBMITTED" cards, solid amber "Request a Delivery Session" button, correctly-neutral (unstyled) "Submit new evidence" link — all correct.
- `/queue` (real reviewer session) — charcoal nav bar with wordmark, amber "Update" buttons on the DB-backed pricing panel, correct.
- `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet` (only 8 pre-existing unrelated errors in privacy.tsx/terms.tsx, not touched by this pass), and `npm run build` all clean throughout.

**One minor, pre-existing, non-blocking observation, not introduced by this pass**: the `(app)` layout's nav bar (`justify-between` flex with no wrap control) has always had nav items/email/sign-out crowd together somewhat at narrow viewport widths — this pass only changed colors and added the wordmark, not the flex layout itself, but the crowding reads slightly more noticeable against the new dark charcoal background than it did against the previous light one. Worth a follow-up pass on responsive nav wrapping, not treated as a defect of this change.

## Lint cleanup + full landing page rebuild (confirmed 2026-08-07)

Two things requested together: fix the last 8 pre-existing lint errors before pilot testing, and rebuild the landing page from a thin hero+lenses+CTA page into a real, full first-impression page — the founder's own assessment was "too thin for a real product launch."

**Lint cleanup**: the 8 errors were all `react/no-unescaped-entities` (raw apostrophes) in [privacy/page.tsx](src/app/privacy/page.tsx) and [terms/page.tsx](src/app/terms/page.tsx) — fixed with `&apos;`. Separately found 2 pre-existing warnings (not part of the named 8) in the Phase-2 stub functions `matchExportSignature()`/`generateReport()` — both use a leading-underscore parameter to signal "deliberately unused," a convention already present in the codebase but never formalized in the eslint config, so every stub like this would warn forever. Fixed at the config level, not per-file: added `argsIgnorePattern: "^_"` to [eslint.config.mjs](eslint.config.mjs) rather than adding inline disables to two files (and every future stub after them). `npx eslint src/ --quiet` and `tsc --noEmit` both fully clean.

**Landing page — full rebuild, not polish** ([src/app/page.tsx](src/app/page.tsx)), 11 sections per explicit spec:
1. **Hero** — headline/subhead is the founder's own exact wording, confirmed verbatim, not paraphrased: "Find the 3 things actually holding your business back." / "Submit your evidence, get a financially-quantified diagnosis and a 90-day plan — not another generic AI report."
2. **The problem** — names the actual pain before the solution (no prioritization, no dollar figures, nothing you can trust blind) — a section type the previous build skipped entirely by jumping straight to "how it works."
3. **How it works** — expanded from 3 to 4 steps (added "Pick your goal" as its own explicit step, since the goal-weighting mechanic is central to how every lens actually works and wasn't mentioned anywhere in the old copy).
4. **Interactive demo** — links to the existing `/demo` prototype (`target="_blank"`), reused as-is per explicit instruction, not rebuilt. Framed honestly: "mock data and compressed timers... no signup, no real evidence needed."
5. **Five-lens breakdown** — expanded from one-liners to fuller, more specific descriptions (e.g. Financial now mentions the deterministic benchmark-comparison discipline; Commercial mentions the origin-tagging split).
6. **Modules section** — Tender Readiness / AI Reliability Audit / Data Protection Compliance, one real use-case sentence each, with live price pulled from the same DB-backed pricing table as section 7 (not restated as a separate literal).
7. **Pricing** — renders directly from `listPricing()` ([src/lib/pricing.ts](src/lib/pricing.ts)), the same live source of truth the reviewer-facing `/queue` pricing panel edits — Standard Core Audit gets its own highlighted "Free" card, every other row (including the `is_placeholder` Monthly Execution Office, correctly flagged) renders from the DB directly. A real copy bug was caught and fixed during verification: the placeholder note originally stuttered ("Placeholder — not yet finalized: Placeholder — cannot be finalized...") by concatenating a hardcoded prefix in front of `item.notes`, which already started with the same word — fixed to just render `item.notes` directly when present.
8. **Why us** — three real, honest differentiators (financially quantified, always human-reviewed, source-agnostic evidence), replacing the old page's "Your data, handled honestly" section, whose content was folded into the new FAQ's data-safety answer instead of being kept as a near-duplicate section.
9. **FAQ** — 5 questions (data safety, turnaround time, tool-connection requirement, sparse-data handling, one-off-vs-ongoing), `<details>` disclosures — same collapsed-by-default pattern already used elsewhere in this app (export-instruction hints), not a new UI pattern.
10. **Book a Discovery Session** — a real design decision, not a literal booking form: `requestSession()` ([src/lib/service-layer/session-requests.ts](src/lib/service-layer/session-requests.ts)) requires an authenticated session and an existing `companies` row, so an anonymous landing-page visitor cannot call it directly. Per the explicit instruction to reuse the existing mechanism rather than build a new booking flow, this CTA is honestly framed as a sign-up step: "sign up and you can request one directly from your evidence intake page... no separate booking form" — routes to `/client-login`, same as every other primary CTA, rather than fabricating a no-account request path that doesn't exist anywhere else in this codebase.
11. **Final CTA + footer** — unchanged in substance from the prior build, carried forward.

**Nav also expanded** — added `#how-it-works`/`#modules`/`#pricing`/`#faq` anchor links to the header nav, since a single-screen "Sign in" link stopped being sufficient wayfinding once the page grew to 11 sections.

**A real, separate gap caught during verification, not requested but fixed**: `npm run build` showed `/` as a fully static route (`○`). Since the page has no auth check, Next would otherwise prerender it once at build time and bake in whatever `pricing` read at that moment — silently defeating the entire "admin-adjustable without a redeploy" point of the pricing table for the one page most new visitors actually see. Fixed with `export const revalidate = 60` — confirmed via the build output's own `Revalidate: 1m` column, keeping the page's static-generation performance while guaranteeing a reviewer's price edit on `/queue` shows up here within a minute, not only on the next deploy.

**Verified live, full section-by-section walkthrough, both light and dark mode, 2026-08-07**: hit real browser-tool rendering flakiness this pass (blank/stale screenshots on scroll — the same class of tool-side desync already documented repeatedly elsewhere in this file) — worked around by resizing the viewport to the page's true `document.documentElement.scrollHeight` and capturing the entire page in one screenshot per pass, rather than scrolling, which reliably avoided the desync. Confirmed via real screenshots: all 11 sections render correctly in order with the exact requested copy, hero/problem/how-it-works verified at both narrow and 1280px-desktop width, the DB-loaded pricing panel renders correctly (including the post-fix placeholder note), all 5 FAQ items expand and read correctly (verified via a full-page capture with `details.open` set programmatically, equivalent to a user clicking each one), the Discovery Session CTA and final CTA/footer render correctly, `/demo` confirmed still loading the real unmodified prototype, and a full dark-mode pass across the entire rebuilt page confirmed every section legible and correctly styled. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet` (zero errors, zero warnings — the 8 errors and 2 warnings both closed in this same pass), and `npm run build` all clean, with the new `/` revalidate window confirmed in the build output.

## Landing page product-clarity pass, part 1 of 2 (confirmed 2026-08-07)

The founder reviewed the full rebuild above and flagged 7 real product-clarity issues, explicitly "not polish." Two (a Calendly/booking embed, and rebuilding the interactive demo around a real seeded test company instead of the compressed mock simulation) are genuine open design/architecture decisions this session can't unilaterally resolve — flagged back to the founder rather than guessed at or half-built, per the standing "surface it clearly and ask" rule. Removed it. `/reviewer-login` itself is completely untouched — confirmed still resolving (`curl` 200) and still fully functional — it's simply no longer linked from anywhere a visitor could stumble onto it, reachable only by a direct, bookmarked URL, exactly as requested.
5. **Positioning ambiguity fixed** — the subhead already named both halves ("a financially-quantified diagnosis and a 90-day plan"), but nothing on the page foregrounded "you get a concrete plan, not just a diagnosis" as early or unmissably as it deserved. Fixed in three places: the hero eyebrow line now reads "A diagnosis, plus a 90-day action plan — for founder-led B2B teams" (was "Goal-driven execution audit..."); a new pair of checkmark badges ("✓ Financially-quantified diagnosis" / "✓ Concrete 30/60/90 action plan") sits directly under the subhead, above the fold, impossible to miss; and "How it works" step 4 was retitled from "Get your report" to "Get your diagnosis + action plan" with body copy now saying "a concrete 30/60/90 day roadmap you can hand straight to your team," not just "a roadmap."
7. **Pricing section restructured, not just restyled** — real sticker-shock problem, not a cosmetic one: the previous version listed every module price flat in the pricing section (Tender Readiness, AI Reliability Audit, Data Protection Compliance, Execution Sprint, F2F Workshop, Monthly Execution Office), on top of already showing each module's price, with real context, in its own card in the Modules section above — pure repetition that visually summed to £11,000+ even though no real buyer purchases everything at once. The flat module-price list is gone from this section entirely; module pricing still lives exactly where it has real context, in the Modules section's own cards, unchanged. Monthly Execution Office is not shown anywhere on the public page anymore, not even as "Coming soon" — it's still an unvalidated placeholder number (`is_placeholder: true` in the `pricing` table) with no dedicated section of its own on this page either, so there was nothing honest to say about it publicly yet; removal was the cleaner of the two options the founder offered, not a default.

**Still open, flagged back to the founder rather than built on a guess** (items 4 and 6 of the original 7):
- **"Book a demo" / Calendly CTA** — this app has no calendar/scheduling integration anywhere (a deliberate, previously-documented choice — the entire Service Layer request mechanism was built specifically because "no calendar integration exists to automate" and the founder didn't want to fake one). Adding a real Calendly (or similar) embed/link needs an actual account URL to point at; fabricating a placeholder link that goes nowhere would be the exact kind of fake functionality this codebase has consistently refused to ship. Needs the real scheduling URL before this can be built.

Replacing it with a real company walking through the actual live dashboard/report/findings requires either (a) a new unauthenticated public route that reads one specific real seeded company via the admin client, bypassing auth entirely — a genuinely new public, no-login surface, the same category of decision already flagged with its own caution for Digital Presence Scan ("the only unauthenticated, public-facing surface... a different security/abuse posture that hasn't been designed for anywhere in this codebase"); or (b) handing out real magic-link/shared-session access to a demo account, a different and arguably riskier posture than (a). Neither is a small addition to the existing `/demo` route — this is a real architecture decision, not a copy change, and is flagged here rather than picked unilaterally.
