## Real auth bug found, root-caused, and fixed — /auth/callback's hardcoded reviewer-login fallback (confirmed 2026-08-07)

A real, reported bug: a first-time client using "Get started" received a confirmation email and, after clicking it, landed on `/reviewer-login` instead of client onboarding. Explicitly investigated by reproducing the exact flow before touching any code — not fixed blind.

**Root cause, confirmed by direct reproduction, not guessed**: [src/app/auth/callback/route.ts](src/app/auth/callback/route.ts) is shared by every auth flow (client and reviewer), and its failure-path fallback was hardcoded to `/reviewer-login` unconditionally — a leftover from when the reviewer flow was the only one that existed (the original comment literally said "reviewer login today"). This has nothing to do with client/reviewer auth "colliding"; it's one unconditional fallback that was never updated when the client flow was added on top.

**Why the exchange actually fails, confirmed by replicating the exact `signInWithOtp()` call this app makes**: this project uses `@supabase/ssr`'s default PKCE flow (no `flowType` override anywhere in the codebase, confirmed by grep) — `signInWithOtp()` sets a `code_verifier` cookie in the browser that requests the link, and redeeming the emailed `code` requires that same cookie still be present. This fails whenever the link is opened in a different browser/device than the one that submitted the form (checking email on your phone after submitting from your laptop, for instance) or visited first by an email provider's link-prescanning bot (the same class of failure this codebase already knew about for the reviewer flow, but had only ever worked around with the OTP-code fallback, never fully root-caused). This class of failure is expected to happen sometimes — the actual bug was always landing everyone on the same wrong page when it does.

**Fix, verified against the real chain, not assumed**: rather than have the callback guess the right fallback from `next`'s path shape, each login flow now passes its own explicit `loginPath` alongside `next` — [src/app/client-login/actions.ts](src/app/client-login/actions.ts) passes `loginPath=/client-login`, [src/app/reviewer-login/actions.ts](src/app/reviewer-login/actions.ts) passes `loginPath=/reviewer-login`, and the callback route falls back to whichever was named (defaulting to `/client-login` if absent, not `/reviewer-login` — a safer default than the bug itself). Traced the complete real redirect chain end-to-end with a genuinely fresh Supabase-generated signup link: Supabase's own `/verify` endpoint redirects to the app with the tokens in the URL fragment (`#access_token=...`, confirmed — fragments are never transmitted to a server, by design), so the actual request the app server receives carries no `code` at all; before the fix this landed on `/reviewer-login` regardless of which flow it was, after the fix it correctly lands on `/client-login`. Verified via direct HTTP tracing (`curl`, since the browser tool blocks navigation to external auth domains) rather than assumed from reading the diff. Real synthetic test users created during reproduction were deleted afterward.

**"Confirm password" email content — investigated, closed, no separate bug**: the founder pasted the actual "Confirm signup" template content from the dashboard, and separately confirmed it's word-for-word what the real email received said: "Confirm your email address... Follow the link below to confirm this email address and finish signing up... Confirm email address." No mention of "password" anywhere, in the template or the real received email — ruling out any old password-based template firing. The original "confirm password" description in the bug report was an imprecise recollection of this exact "confirm your email... finish signing up" copy, not a distinct issue. **Confirmed: the redirect fix above was the complete fix — there was only ever one real bug here, not two.**

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean.

**Not yet committed/pushed** — same standing pattern, held for explicit go-ahead.

## Second real auth bug found and fixed: first-time signup was three steps, not one (confirmed 2026-08-07)

Reported live: a first-time email got a "confirm your email" message, clicking it didn't sign the person in, and they had to re-enter their email a second time before getting a working code.

**Root cause, confirmed by reading this codebase's own existing code, not guessed**: [scripts/grant-reviewer.ts](scripts/grant-reviewer.ts) already admin-creates reviewer accounts with `email_confirm: true`, with its own comment explaining why — "unconfirmed email flow not needed... since the person proves ownership via the magic-link they'll request themselves." That comment is direct evidence this project's Supabase "Confirm email" setting requires a separate confirmation step before a plain `signInWithOtp()` on a brand-new user will actually establish a session — and that bypass was only ever built for the reviewer path. The self-serve client path had no equivalent, so a first-time client hit the full two-email round trip: a "Confirm signup" email that only flips `email_confirmed_at` without logging anyone in, then a second, real "Magic Link" email that actually works.

**Fixed the same way reviewers already are, not by touching the Supabase dashboard**: [src/app/client-login/actions.ts](src/app/client-login/actions.ts)'s `requestClientMagicLink()` now explicitly pre-confirms a brand-new email via `admin.createUser({ email_confirm: true })` before requesting the real sign-in OTP (`shouldCreateUser: false`), so Supabase treats every signup as an already-confirmed returning user from the very first request — one email, one working link/code, no separate confirmation round-trip. `email_exists` (confirmed via direct testing — Supabase's real error code and HTTP status for a duplicate `createUser` call) is treated as success, not failure, matching the same "atomic conflict handling over check-then-write" pattern already used by `ensureClientUserRow`.

**Verified, not assumed**: replicated the exact new code path (pre-confirm, then `signInWithOtp` with `shouldCreateUser: false`) against a real, brand-new synthetic email, then confirmed via `admin.generateLink()` that a follow-up request for that same email now returns `verification_type: "magiclink"`, not `"signup"` — direct proof the account is genuinely treated as an existing, confirmed user from the first request onward, not just that the code compiles. Test user cleaned up afterward. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean.

## Pricing card CTAs (confirmed 2026-08-07)

The Standard/Concierge comparison cards had price and copy but no actual call to action. Fixed in [src/app/page.tsx](src/app/page.tsx): Standard gets a direct "Get started" link into the real signup flow (solid amber, matching the page's own convention for direct self-serve actions). Concierge gets "Request a demo," routed to the same real Calendly link already used as the page's primary "talk to us now" path — not a bare payment button (no in-app checkout exists anywhere in this codebase), and deliberately not the Discovery Session request flow, which is the slower sign-up-first/human-follow-up path already offered its own section further down the page. Outline style, matching the page's established convention for the consultative half of every other CTA pair on this page (hero: "Start your free audit" solid / "Book a demo" outline; the dedicated demo section: "Book a demo now" solid / "Request a Discovery Session" outline). Verified live: both links confirmed via the accessibility tree (`href="/client-login"` and `href="https://calendly.com/elvanis-app/30min"` respectively) and via full-page screenshot showing correct button placement and styling on both cards.

## Onboarding wizard stuck on "Creating…" — root-caused and fixed (confirmed 2026-08-07)

Reported live: submitting the wizard's final Review step stayed stuck on "Creating…" forever, no redirect, no error.

**Reproduced end-to-end through the real UI**, not assumed: signed up with a genuinely fresh email through the real, now-one-step signup flow (see the signup fix above), filled all 5 wizard steps, and hit the exact same hang on submit. Confirmed via direct DB read that `createCompanyAndGoal()` itself had succeeded — the company and goal rows were genuinely written — proving the server action was never the problem. The network log showed the `/evidence-intake` RSC fetch had even completed with a 200, yet `window.location.href` (checked live, not assumed from stale UI) never actually advanced past `/onboarding`.

**Root cause**: [src/app/onboarding/OnboardingWizard.tsx](src/app/onboarding/OnboardingWizard.tsx)'s `handleSubmit()` called `router.push("/evidence-intake")` immediately followed by `router.refresh()` — a known Next.js App Router footgun where `refresh()` fired right after `push()` races the push's own in-flight navigation and can leave the client stuck mid-transition, never committing to the new route. This is the first place in the app where that pattern lands on a page doing several sequential awaited Server Component calls (`/evidence-intake` does six), giving the race enough of a window to actually manifest — the same pattern elsewhere (e.g. client-login → business-profile, which redirects onward almost immediately) apparently didn't have enough surface area to trigger it visibly.

**Fixed by removing the redundant `refresh()` call**, not by working around the race some other way — every target page (`/evidence-intake`, `/business-profile`, `/queue`, `/reports/[id]`) is already fully dynamic (session/cookie-dependent, confirmed via the build output's own `ƒ` markers), so `push()` alone already forces a fresh server render; `refresh()` was pure redundant risk in every one of these forward-navigation cases, not a real safeguard. Applied consistently to all three other call sites of the identical pattern found via a full grep: [src/app/client-login/page.tsx](src/app/client-login/page.tsx), [src/app/reviewer-login/page.tsx](src/app/reviewer-login/page.tsx), and [src/app/evidence-intake/EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx](src/app/evidence-intake/EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx) — closing the same latent bug before it surfaced elsewhere, rather than only patching the one that got reported. **Deliberately left unchanged**: the sign-out buttons ([src/app/(app)/sign-out-button.tsx](<src/app/(app)/sign-out-button.tsx>), [src/app/(reviewer)/sign-out-button.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/sign-out-button.tsx>)), which use the same two calls for a genuinely different reason — invalidating the Router Cache on the way *out* of an authenticated page so the browser's back button can't surface stale authenticated content post-logout, not "make the target page's own data fresh." That's a real, load-bearing use of `refresh()`, not the same redundant pattern.

**Verified live end-to-end after the fix, with a second fresh account**: full 5-step wizard, submit, confirmed via direct `window.location.href` check (not the UI display, which the tool's own known rendering desync makes an unreliable signal on its own) that the browser genuinely landed on `/evidence-intake`, and confirmed the real Evidence Intake form rendered with real content. Synthetic test accounts/companies from both the reproduction and the verification pass deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean.

**Not yet committed/pushed** — held for explicit go-ahead, same standing pattern.

## The one thing that cannot be verified internally: no real external pilot user yet (flagged 2026-08-05)

Every "verified live" claim in this file so far — the full client chain, all three modules, reviewer auth, real email, the reviewer-denial branch — was driven by the same person building the system, using real infrastructure but disposable or founder-controlled test accounts. That is genuine, real verification of *whether the system works as built*. It is not, and cannot be, verification of *whether the system works for someone who didn't build it*: unclear copy, a confusing intake question, an edge case in how a real company's messy real data doesn't fit the fill-in-template fields, a genuinely ambiguous goal-selection moment — none of these surface when the tester already knows what answer the system expects.

This is explicitly not something to "build" — there is no code fix for it. It is a gap in *proof*, not in the product, and it is the one V1 completeness claim that stays open no matter how much further internal testing happens. **V1 should not be considered fully proven until at least one real external user — someone other than the founder, with a real company and real evidence, with no foreknowledge of how the system is supposed to behave — has gone through the full flow (sign up → business profile → evidence submission → reviewer review → delivered report) and their experience has been observed, not assumed.** Flagging this now, explicitly, rather than letting continued internal verification quietly stand in for it.

## Design-system primitives + Business Profile reference fix (confirmed 2026-08-07)

First step of the final, app-wide visual design pass the founder requested ("this needs to be genuinely finished, pilot-ready, not another partial pass... every page, not just the landing page and nav"). Per the founder's own explicit two-message sequencing ("fix it here first [Business Profile]... Report back with a screenshot" before the full page list), this phase built the real design-system primitives and fully fixed Business Profile as the reference implementation — the broader rollout across the rest of the app (Evidence Intake's five lens forms, Dashboard, Reports & History, Account Settings, the client report view, all three module intake/report pages, the Reviewer Workspace, the Execution Sprint pages, the reviewer queue, client-login/onboarding) is the confirmed next phase, not yet started.

**Real gap the founder's own diagnosis correctly named**: "the bones were never styled, only the accent color was added on top" — the earlier brand-identity pass (see "Final brand identity applied" above) recolored the existing `neutral-*` Tailwind scale and swept CTA buttons to amber, but every input/textarea/select in the app was still a bare, per-page-duplicated `className="w-full rounded border px-3 py-2 text-sm"` with no border color specified, rendering as the browser's unstyled default. No shared component existed anywhere — every page hand-rolled its own form markup.

**Design-system primitives built** ([src/app/_components/ui/](src/app/_components/ui/)) — real React components, not just shared CSS classes, so every consuming page gets label/hint/error handling for free instead of re-implementing it:
- **`Input.tsx`** / **`Textarea.tsx`** / **`Select.tsx`** — shared `label`/`hint`/`error` props, consistent `neutral-300`/`neutral-700` border, `accent`-colored focus ring (`focus:ring-2 focus:ring-accent/20`), red error state, dark-mode variants throughout.
- **`TagInput.tsx`** — real tag-input UI for every field previously labeled "(comma-separated)," relying on the client to already know to type commas. Enter or comma commits the current draft as a pill; Backspace on an empty draft removes the last tag; blur commits any in-progress draft rather than silently discarding it. **Component logic verified correct via direct, isolated `KeyboardEvent`/native-setter dispatch** — a real distinction surfaced during verification: the browser automation tool's own higher-level `type`+`key` actions were intermittently unreliable against this specific controlled-input pattern (merged two entries into one pill, or silently dropped a commit) purely as a tool-side synthetic-event artifact, not a code bug — confirmed by dispatching a genuine `KeyboardEvent` directly via `el.dispatchEvent()`, which committed correctly and cleanly every time. Same class of tool desync already documented elsewhere in this file, just manifesting through a different tool call this time (`computer{type/key}` instead of `screenshot`/`scroll`).
- **`Card.tsx`** — shared `title`/`subtitle` container, replacing every page's own hand-rolled `rounded-lg border border-neutral-200 bg-white p-5...` one-off (padding/spacing had drifted page to page since nothing was actually shared).
- **`Button.tsx`** — `primary`/`secondary` variants (`VARIANTS` map), formalizing the amber-primary/neutral-outline-secondary convention already established ad hoc on the landing page during the brand-identity pass, now a real reusable component instead of a hand-copied className string per call site.

**Business Profile fully rebuilt as the reference fix** ([src/app/(app)/business-profile/BusinessProfileForm.tsx](<src/app/(app)/business-profile/BusinessProfileForm.tsx>), [DesiredFutureStateField.tsx](<src/app/(app)/business-profile/DesiredFutureStateField.tsx>), [page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/business-profile/page.tsx>)) — the founder's four numbered asks, all closed:
1. **Revenue range band is now a real dropdown** of 7 fixed bands (Pre-revenue through £20m+ ARR), not a free-text field where "£1m-£5m ARR" / "1-5m ARR" / "£1,000,000-£5,000,000" were all equally valid-looking answers. The DB column stays plain text (no schema change needed) — the dropdown just constrains what gets written. Any pre-existing value that doesn't match one of the fixed bands is dynamically prepended as its own selectable option (`revenueBandOptions`), so a legacy free-text answer is never silently destroyed by this becoming a dropdown.
2. **Every previously-bare field now has a real placeholder or hint** showing what a good answer looks like: Industry ("e.g. B2B SaaS — marketing analytics"), Employee count ("e.g. 45"), Stage ("e.g. seed, Series A, bootstrapped"), Website URL ("https://acme.com"), Customer type ("e.g. SMEs, enterprise, consumers"), Team structure summary (a full worked example), Social links and Main tools/stack (tag-input hints explaining what to paste and why).
3. **Social/review links and Main tools/stack — the two fields literally labeled "(comma-separated)" — are now real `TagInput` fields**, not text relying on the client already knowing to type commas.
4. **Every field now uses the shared Input/Select/Textarea/TagInput/Button primitives**, and both `Card` sections (Company details, Primary/Secondary goal) use the shared `Card` component — closing the real gap the founder named: the form's underlying bones were never styled, only nav/buttons had the accent color applied on top.

**Verified live, end-to-end, real session (2026-08-07)** — a fresh disposable test account (`design-pass-verify@gmail.com`, admin-created pre-confirmed, driven through the real onboarding wizard to get a genuine company row) confirmed via a full page reload (not just in-session state) that every fix persisted correctly through a real save: both Social/review link tags rendered as distinct, correctly-labeled pills (not merged), Revenue range band correctly showed "£1m – £5m ARR" selected, Customer type showed "SMEs, mid-market," Main tools/stack showed the "Xero" tag, and Team structure summary showed the full entered text — all through the new component styling (subtle borders, amber focus rings, consistent card padding, typography hierarchy). Screenshot sent to the founder as the requested checkpoint. Test account and its company/goal rows deleted afterward (a UI-verification-only account, not a substantive end-to-end proof run worth retaining, unlike the Nimbus Ledger/Riverbank Analytics/stranger-pass accounts kept elsewhere in this file).

**Not yet done — the rest of message 1's page list remains open, per the founder's own explicit sequencing**: landing page forms (nav/CTAs already done in the brand-identity pass, but no form inputs there), client-login/onboarding, Evidence Intake's five lens forms, Dashboard, Reports & History, Account Settings, the client report view, all three module intake/report pages, the Reviewer Workspace, the Execution Sprint pages, and the reviewer queue all still need the same Input/Select/Textarea/TagInput/Card/Button primitives applied, plus the same helper-text/placeholder audit message 1 asked for app-wide. Held for explicit go-ahead now that the reference fix is confirmed, not assumed.

## Next-step guidance on Business Profile + Dashboard, "no next step" UX gap closed (confirmed 2026-08-07)

Real feedback after the design-system checkpoint: after saving Business Profile there was no indication of what to do next and no link into Evidence Intake, and the Dashboard's empty state ("No delivered report yet") existed in code but was easy to miss — plain unstyled text with no visual weight.

**Shared, deterministic status** ([src/lib/reports/journey-status.ts](src/lib/reports/journey-status.ts)) — `computeJourneyStatus()` returns one of three stages (`no_evidence`/`in_review`/`has_report`) from the company's most recent `reports` row, read by both pages so they can't describe the client's own state differently to each other — same "single source of truth" discipline as `deriveRoadmap()`/`getTotalTurnaroundHours()`. **Must be called with the admin client, not session-scoped** — `reports`' client-facing RLS only allows `status = 'sent'` through (the same real mismatch already documented and worked around on the client Report view), so a session-scoped query would silently see zero rows for any `pending_review`/`approved` report and misreport a genuinely in-review company as `no_evidence` — worse than showing nothing, since it would prompt a client to re-submit evidence they already sent. Always called with a companyId already ownership-verified via the session client first.

**`NextStepBanner`** ([src/app/_components/NextStepBanner.tsx](src/app/_components/NextStepBanner.tsx)) — one shared, amber-accented callout (deliberately distinct from the neutral form Cards around it, since it's the one action prompt on the page) with per-stage copy and a real CTA: `no_evidence` → "Submit evidence" linking to `/evidence-intake`; `in_review` → "Check status" linking to the specific report's holding page (which already renders "Your report is being reviewed"); `has_report` → "View report" linking to the same page, which renders the real content once `sent`. Built alongside a `LinkButton` ([src/app/_components/ui/LinkButton.tsx](src/app/_components/ui/LinkButton.tsx)) — Button.tsx's `VARIANTS` map was exported so a real `next/link`-based CTA (navigation, not a form action) can share the exact same visual style without duplicating the className string.

Wired into both **Business Profile** (banner always shown, above the form Cards) and **Dashboard** (banner replaces the old plain-text empty state; only rendered when there's no `sent` report — the existing full stats section is unchanged and untouched once one exists).

**Verified live end-to-end, all three states, real data (2026-08-07)** — a fresh test account was created and driven through a real session (the actual `requestClientMagicLink` send path was transiently failing during this verification; bypassed using the same `admin.generateLink()` technique already established earlier this session, via a temporary local-only debug route that called `supabase.auth.setSession()` directly, deleted immediately after use — never committed). Confirmed via real page loads on both Business Profile and Dashboard: `no_evidence` state (fresh company, no reports) showed the correct copy and a `/evidence-intake`-linked CTA; `in_review` state (a real `pending_review` report inserted) showed the correct copy and a CTA linking to that exact report's ID, which correctly rendered the pre-existing "Your report is being reviewed" holding page; `has_report` state (the same report flipped to `sent` with `reviewed_by` set, satisfying the DB's mandatory-review check constraint) showed the correct copy on Business Profile, and Dashboard correctly stopped showing the banner and rendered its normal stats sections instead. Test account, company, and report deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, both before and after the debug route was removed.

**Broader "user tour guide" ask — resolved, progress stepper built (2026-08-07).** Offered three real options with meaningfully different scope (a persistent progress stepper / a full interactive tooltip-driven walkthrough / reviving the spec's originally-designed but never-built "in-app Guide"); founder chose the stepper.

**`ProgressStepper`** ([src/app/_components/ProgressStepper.tsx](src/app/_components/ProgressStepper.tsx)) — 4 fixed named steps (Profile → Evidence → Review → Report), reusing the same `computeJourneyStatus()` signal `NextStepBanner` already reads (no separate state to drift). "Profile" is always shown done: every page this renders on already requires a real `companies` row (enforced by the `(app)` layout), so by construction the visitor has always completed at least that step to be there. Wired into all three pages most relevant to a first-timer's journey: Business Profile, Dashboard (now always computes `journeyStatus`, not only when there's no `sent` report, since the stepper needs it in every state including `has_report`), and Evidence Intake (a top-level route outside the `(app)` group, so it computes its own `journeyStatus` via the admin client the same way). Deliberately not added to Account Settings (unrelated to this journey) or Reports & History (a historical list, not "current position").

**Verified live, all 4 stages, real data (2026-08-07)** — reused the same fresh-account + `admin.generateLink()` + temporary local debug-route technique from the `NextStepBanner` verification above (deleted immediately after use again, never committed) to drive a real session through all three pages at each of the three underlying `journeyStatus` states (a 4th on-screen step, "Report," is just the visual endpoint of the `has_report` stage, not a distinct stored state): `no_evidence` correctly showed Profile done / Evidence current / Review+Report grayed out on both Business Profile and Evidence Intake; `in_review` (a real `pending_review` report inserted) correctly showed Profile+Evidence done / Review current on Dashboard; `has_report` (the same report flipped to `sent` with `reviewed_by` set) correctly showed all of Profile/Evidence/Review done and Report as the current/final step, with the stepper rendering above Dashboard's normal stats section rather than the `NextStepBanner`. Test account, company, and report deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, both before and after the debug route was removed a second time.

## Real numeric metric fields added to Evidence Intake (confirmed 2026-08-07)

A real gap flagged directly from a screenshot: the live fill-in-template Evidence Intake form had zero numeric input fields anywhere — every field was free-text/qualitative — while `compareFinancialMetric()`/`compareExecutionMetric()`/`compareProductMetric()` (the deterministic benchmark-comparison machinery, built and unit-tested earlier this build) require a typed `metrics: MetricInput[]` array to run on. Confirmed by reading the code directly, not assumed: `submitEvidence()` hardcoded `metrics: NO_METRICS` (an empty array) on every real submission — a real, previously-flagged, deliberate deferral from 2026-08-03 that had gone unmentioned since, exactly the kind of thing the standing "flag it, don't let it silently not happen" rule exists to catch.

**Confirmed this is not the KPI-actuals failure mode** — the LLM never extracts a number from free text and feeds it into the deterministic comparison; the comparison only ever runs on the separate `metrics` array. When `metrics` is empty, `formatComputedComparisonsForPrompt()` already explicitly instructs the model: "discuss any numbers in the evidence qualitatively, but do not assert a specific benchmark tier/comparison for them." Flagged as a real, smaller residual risk: unlike similar prompt-only rules elsewhere in this codebase (trace-logs, missing-documentation duplicates), this one had never been stress-tested live before this fix — it just happened to never matter since `metrics` was always empty.

**Real numeric fields added** ([src/lib/evidence/field-sets.ts](src/lib/evidence/field-sets.ts)) — a new `EvidenceMetricDefinition[]` per lens, `metricKey`s copied verbatim from `FinancialMetricKey`/`ExecutionMetricKey`/`ProductMetricKey` (not re-derived, to avoid a silent mismatch falling back to qualitative-only): Financial (gross margin %, cash runway months, customer concentration %), Execution (delivery lead time days, PR cycle time hours, PR review pickup time hours, decision/approval latency hours, weekly meeting hours per manager), Product (annual logo churn %, NPS, core feature adoption %, activation rate %, support CSAT %) — 11 fields total, all optional, same "blank is meaningful too" principle as every other field.

**Wired end-to-end**: [EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx](src/app/evidence-intake/EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx) renders a "Key metrics (optional)" number-input grid above each lens's free-text fields, parses only at submit time (blank/non-numeric entries silently skipped, not coerced), and saves into the same debounced draft mechanism as everything else. [actions.ts](src/app/evidence-intake/actions.ts)'s `submitEvidence()` now passes the real `metrics` through to `runAudit()` instead of `NO_METRICS`. [rerun-audit.ts](src/lib/audit/rerun-audit.ts) reads `metrics` from the stored snapshot when re-running (falling back to `NO_METRICS` only for pre-2026-08-07 snapshots that never had it). The client report page and demo-live page's "Evidence submitted" sections now also display submitted metric values, same treatment as the qualitative fields.

**Verified live, real submission, real Groq call (2026-08-07)**: a fresh test account submitted `gross_margin_percent: 55` through the actual UI — confirmed via direct DB read that `reports.source_evidence_snapshot.financial.metrics` correctly stored `[{"metricKey":"gross_margin_percent","value":55}]`, and the resulting real Financial lens finding used the exact deterministic comparison text verbatim: *"Gross margin is 55%, which is below the 60% concerning threshold (healthy range is 70-80%)"* — proof the full path (UI → draft → submission → snapshot → `runAudit()` → `financialLens.runDraft()` → `compareFinancialMetric()` → real LLM call) now works, not just that the code compiles. Draft persistence for the new fields also independently confirmed via a real full-page reload. One separate, minor pre-existing observation surfaced during this same live run, not caused by this fix: a second finding on the same report claimed "no monthly revenue, margin... figures were submitted" despite the margin figure being present and correctly used in the adjacent finding — a prompt-quality nuance in how the missing-evidence framing reacts to empty free-text fields, flagged rather than silently ignored, not blocking since a reviewer sees both findings together. Test account and data cleaned up afterward. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout.

## Adversarial test for the "no benchmark comparison for narrative-only numbers" rule — confirmed proven, 2026-08-07

Closes the self-flagged residual risk from the numeric-metric-fields fix above — explicit direction: don't leave it as "reads as solid," construct a real adversarial test case and confirm before considering it closed.

**Test design** ([financial.ts](src/lib/lenses/financial.ts) rule 6): synthetic evidence deliberately built to tempt the model into violating the rule, same discipline as every other test this session (no real production data exists yet). One real submitted metric (`gross_margin_percent: 55`, correctly benchmark-comparable) alongside two untracked numbers in free text: a customer-concentration figure (40%) in the *same domain* as a real benchmarked metric — the model has the actual threshold numbers sitting in its "REFERENCE BENCHMARKS" context — and an unrelated hosting-cost figure (62% of opex) chosen to superficially resemble the margin benchmark's own numbers. Ran `financialLens.runDraft()` directly against real Groq, 5 times.

**Result: 0/10 hard violations across two rounds (10 total real runs)** — no run ever attributed a specific threshold number or the system's own real tier value to either untracked figure. The actual failure mode rule 6 exists to prevent (a client shown a false specific benchmark claim) never occurred.

**Real, honest secondary finding**: 8/10 runs used generic risk-tier-adjacent language ("elevated risk," "watch item," "concerning") for the 40% figure — notably never even the *correct* tier if computed for real (would be "critical" at 40%, confirming the model isn't attempting real arithmetic, just writing plausible prose). Tightened rule 6 with an explicit negative example forbidding tier-style words on untracked numbers, applied identically to `execution.ts` and `product.ts` (same rule text existed verbatim in both) — **the prompt tightening measurably did not eliminate the pattern**, just shuffled which generic word got used, the same "prompt-only instruction isn't fully reliable" signature this codebase has hit before (trace-logs, missing-documentation duplicates).

**Confirmed decision, not silently resolved**: given zero financial/factual harm in 10 real trials and that a deterministic text-classifier for "tier-adjacent language" carries real false-positive risk (would also flag legitimately-earned strong language elsewhere), the founder chose to accept this as proven against the failure mode it actually exists to prevent, rather than build a fuzzy backstop — the mandatory reviewer gate already covers this level of stylistic softness. The prompt tightening stays in place as a real, if partial, improvement (same "measurably improved but did not eliminate" treatment already applied elsewhere in this codebase), not reverted. Test script was a real-Groq adversarial probe, not a deterministic unit test — run live, findings recorded here, then deleted, same treatment as every other live-Groq verification in this codebase (never committed as a repeatable test since it costs real API calls and isn't deterministic).

## Design-system pass extended to Evidence Intake (confirmed 2026-08-07)

Status check requested directly ("is the design-system pass still in progress, or does Evidence Intake specifically need another look?") — confirmed by reading the code, not assumed: the pass was genuinely still only applied to Business Profile as the reference implementation. Every field on Evidence Intake — all four lens sections' free-text and the brand-new numeric metric fields, Commercial, AI & Governance — was still a raw `className="w-full rounded border px-3 py-2 text-sm"` with no border color specified, the exact "default browser styling" gap the design system exists to close.

**[EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx](src/app/evidence-intake/EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx) rewritten with the shared primitives** — every `<input>`/`<textarea>`/`<select>` replaced with `Input`/`Textarea`/`Select` (Business Profile's exact same components, no new primitives needed), each of the six sections (Financial/Execution/Product/Commercial/AI & Governance) now wrapped in a `Card`, the submit and modal buttons now use `Button` (`variant="secondary"` for Cancel). Checkboxes (no shared checkbox component exists yet) got a consistent `accent-accent` Tailwind utility instead of browser-default styling — a small, low-risk addition, not a new component. `label` prop is a plain string on these primitives (no per-word styling), so the numeric fields' previously two-toned label+unit rendering simplified to one string (`"Gross margin (%)"`) — a deliberate small simplification for consistency with how Business Profile's own primitives are used everywhere else, not a regression.

**Verified live (2026-08-07)** — a fresh test session confirmed via full-page screenshots that every Card/field renders with real borders, consistent spacing, and correct typography hierarchy across the entire page (previously invisible when unstyled), and confirmed interactively that the amber focus ring genuinely fires on click (not just present in the CSS) — the exact same proof standard already used for Business Profile. Test account and data cleaned up afterward. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint`, and `npm run build` all clean.

**Still open — the rest of the original design-system rollout list**: client-login/onboarding, Dashboard, Reports & History, Account Settings, the client report view, all three module intake/report pages, the Reviewer Workspace, the Execution Sprint pages, and the reviewer queue still need the same primitives applied. Business Profile and Evidence Intake are now both done as reference implementations proving the pattern works on both a settings-style form and a longer multi-section intake form.

## Real bug list from live testing — all 7 worked through individually (confirmed 2026-08-10)

Direct founder bug report from live testing, explicitly requested worked through one at a time with individual status, not bundled — "especially #8" in the request referred to what's numbered #7 below (a likely off-by-one in the founder's own count), the one flagged as possibly blocking review.

**1. App-wide message/notification design sweep — done.** Real gap: most error/success/info messages across the app were bare `<p className="text-sm text-red-600">` strings with no background/border/icon, reading as raw/unstyled text (the literal complaint: "look like raw browser validation"). Built a real shared [Alert.tsx](src/app/_components/ui/Alert.tsx) component (error/success/warning/info variants, icon + colored background + border, matching the boxed treatment a few places had already hand-rolled ad hoc) and swept it across client-login, reviewer-login, onboarding, Evidence Intake, all three reviewer workspace clients (Review/Module/Sprint — blocked-reason banners, deliver/sprint/rerun errors), the Execution Sprint client page, and the shared SessionRequestButton/SprintInterestButton components. Left Input.tsx/Textarea.tsx's own per-field error text alone — small red text directly under a field is a standard, already-reasonable inline-validation pattern, distinct from the page-level "message" surfaces this fix targets.

**2. OTP/login screen copy + Verify button — done, confirmed live end-to-end.** [client-login](src/app/client-login/page.tsx)/[reviewer-login](src/app/reviewer-login/page.tsx) rewritten: wrapped in a real `Card` with a wordmark badge above it (previously bare text floating on an empty page — the actual "cheap and untrustworthy" root cause), copy tightened to sound like a real product rather than a technical debug message, the "sent" state now uses the new `Alert` component instead of a hand-rolled box, and a "Use a different email" escape hatch was added. **Verified live, not just visually**: drove a real signup through the actual browser — email submitted, real magic-link+code sent, code verified via a real Supabase-generated OTP, and the Verify code button genuinely authenticated and redirected into the real onboarding wizard. Confirms the button was already correctly wired; the fix here was purely the surrounding UX.

**3. Logo churn field hint — done, confirmed live.** Added an optional `hint` field to `EvidenceMetricDefinition` ([field-sets.ts](src/lib/evidence/field-sets.ts)) and populated it only for `annual_logo_churn_percent`: "The % of customers (by count) who cancelled in the last 12 months — not revenue lost, just how many left." Term kept (it's real, correct SaaS terminology, not a typo), jargon explained inline via `Input`'s existing `hint` prop. Verified live via `get_page_text` — renders directly under the field label.

**4. Loading indicator during evidence submission — done, confirmed live against a real Groq run.** Evidence submission runs all 5 lenses server-side (staggered, each a real Groq call) with no prior feedback beyond a disabled button — easy to miss entirely, reading as a frozen page. Added a real full-screen overlay (spinner + "Analyzing your evidence… We're running your Financial, Execution, Product, Commercial, and AI & Governance analysis. This usually takes under a minute — please don't close this tab.") shown for the whole duration of `submitEvidence()`. **Verified live**: `get_page_text` captured the overlay's exact text mid-submission, and the run correctly completed ~45s later.

**5 & 6. Holding page copy + edit path + design polish — done, confirmed live, together (they're the same page).** Real, previously-nonexistent gap confirmed by reading the actual holding page (`reports/[reportId]/page.tsx` when `status !== "sent"`): it was two lines of bare text with **zero** link anywhere back to `/evidence-intake`, despite the confirmation modal explicitly promising "you'll have N hours to edit or add evidence" — a client had no way to act on that promise from this page. Fixed: the page now loads `edit_window_closes_at`, computes whether the window is genuinely still open, and shows real distinct copy + a real amber CTA ("Add or revise evidence") only while it's open, wrapped in a proper `Card`. **A deeper, real architecture gap surfaced and disclosed, not silently patched**: this codebase has no "edit an existing report in place" mechanism at all — every `/evidence-intake` submission runs a brand-new audit and creates a brand-new report, full stop. So "editing" during the window today literally means submitting a second, separate report, not updating the first. Rather than build a bigger edit-in-place feature unasked, or silently block resubmission, `/evidence-intake` now shows a real `Alert` warning (`inProgressReportId`, computed from the already-existing `computeJourneyStatus()`) telling the client exactly that before they resubmit. **Flagged, not fixed this pass**: whether real edit-in-place should be built, given the confirmation modal's own "edit or add evidence" wording currently overpromises what happens today. **Verified live end-to-end**: a real submission's holding page correctly showed "Evidence received — still open for changes... about 24 more hours" with a working, correctly-styled CTA.

**7. Reviewer queue — root-caused (confirmed NOT a bug) and restructured, both confirmed live.** Investigated with a direct DB query before touching any code: the newest report (company "Sally," submitted 2026-08-10T05:38, `edit_window_closes_at: 2026-08-11T05:38`) simply hadn't reached its 24h window yet — the queue's `.lte("edit_window_closes_at", now)` filter is working exactly as designed and documented (spec §2.3a: still-editable reports are the client's business, not the reviewer's). Not a bug — but a real, confirmed UX gap: nothing on `/queue` distinguished "empty because nothing's happened" from "empty because a real submission exists and just isn't ready yet." Fixed with a new "Still with client" section (real query, `edit_window_closes_at > now`, purely informational, no review action) so a reviewer can see a real submission exists even before it's actionable. Separately restructured the "Ready for review" list from one flat, undifferentiated list into groups by company — each item still links out to its own real workspace (`/review`, `/review-module`, `/review-sprint`) for full details/actions, not a duplicated new UI. **Verified live end-to-end** as the disposable reviewer test account: the grouped list correctly showed "Nimbus Ledger Ltd" with 6 items grouped under one heading, and "Still with client" correctly showed both "Sally" and the newly-submitted "UX Check Test Co" with accurate future open-for-review timestamps — directly confirming the original report was never a functional bug.

**Real, unrelated bug found and fixed during live verification of #5/#6, not one of the 7 reported items**: `LinkButton.tsx` and its consumers ([NextStepBanner.tsx](src/app/_components/NextStepBanner.tsx), the reviewer queue page, the report holding page) have no `"use client"` directive and are Server Components — but they were importing `VARIANTS` from `Button.tsx`, which does have `"use client"`. A Server Component importing a named, non-component export from a `"use client"` module gets `undefined` at render time, not the real value (Next.js's client-boundary transform only preserves the actual component reference across that boundary). This silently rendered every server-rendered `LinkButton` with a literal `"undefined"` class fragment instead of real amber/outline styling — confirmed via `getComputedStyle()` showing a fully transparent background, reproduced after a full hard navigation (not an HMR artifact). Fixed by extracting `VARIANTS` into its own plain module ([button-variants.ts](src/app/_components/ui/button-variants.ts), no `"use client"`) that both `Button.tsx` and `LinkButton.tsx` import from — confirmed live afterward via `getComputedStyle()` showing the real accent color (`rgb(186, 117, 23)`).

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, checked at every checkpoint. Test data (a disposable `elvanis-test-uxcheck-2026@gmail.com` account and its company/report, created purely to drive this verification) deleted afterward — a UI-verification pass, not a first-of-its-kind proof run worth retaining.

**Follow-up, confirmed 2026-08-10 — item 5's flagged decision resolved**: founder confirmed "resubmit creates a new report" is the accepted real behavior for pilot — real edit-in-place is NOT being built now. The actual fix needed was making the confirmation modal (and the holding page, which carried the same implication) honestly describe that, not imply true in-place editing. Both now say plainly: "There's no in-place editing — if you need to change or add anything during that window, submitting again creates a separate, new report rather than updating this one." The holding page's CTA was also relabeled from "Add or revise evidence" to "Submit new evidence" to match.

**A second, subtler real bug found and fixed while re-verifying this wording live**: a literal space immediately after a JSX expression (e.g. `{editWindowHours} hours`) is silently dropped by JSX's whitespace-collapse rule whenever that specific text node spans multiple source lines — confirmed by inspecting live `childNodes`, which showed `"24"` immediately followed by `"hours before review begins..."` with no leading space, despite a real space in the source. A single-line text node right after the same kind of expression (e.g. `hours total.` on its own line) was unaffected. This is very likely the actual mechanism behind the original bug report's "typo — missing space in '72hours'" observation — a real, reproducible gotcha that a plain source grep for the literal string could never have caught, which is why the earlier pass wrongly concluded that specific typo "doesn't currently exist in code." Fixed in both the confirmation modal and the holding page by using an explicit `{" "}` after every expression whose following text could wrap to a new line, instead of relying on a plain space surviving JSX's line-based trimming. **Flagged, not swept app-wide**: this is a newly-discovered class of risk that could exist anywhere else in the app with a similar multi-line paragraph + interpolated-number pattern — fixed everywhere touched by this pass, not audited exhaustively across the whole codebase (a bounded scope decision, not an oversight).

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean after both fixes. Verified live a second time post-fix: the confirmation modal correctly reads "You'll have 24 hours before review begins... ready within 72 hours total" (both spaces present) against the real, kept Riverbank Analytics Ltd proof account. Disposable test accounts created purely to drive this verification round deleted afterward.

Committed and pushed, per explicit go-ahead.

## Delayed-execution architecture: evidence storage separated from audit execution (confirmed 2026-08-10)

Real architecture fix, not a wording patch — direct founder request following the honest "does the audit run immediately or after the window closes?" question. Answer, confirmed by reading the code directly: the audit ran **immediately and synchronously** inside `submitEvidence()`, on every submission including every resubmission during the supposed 24h "edit window" — meaning every "edit" was actually a full, real, costly 5-lens Groq run and a brand-new duplicate report, never an in-place update. This was the real bug behind the earlier "missing space" investigation's tangent, and the founder correctly identified it as a real cost problem (unnecessary repeat Groq calls), not just a UX one.

**New model, built and verified live end-to-end**:
1. Submitting evidence now only stores/updates a `pending_evidence_submissions` row ([migration](supabase/migrations/20260810133218_pending_evidence_submissions.sql)) — no `runAudit()` call at all.
2. Resubmitting during the window updates the SAME row in place (`submitted_at`/`edit_window_closes_at` anchored once, never reset) — no new report, no new Groq call.
3. A new cron check ([run-pending-audits.ts](src/lib/audit/run-pending-audits.ts)) finds rows whose window has closed and runs the audit **exactly once**, using whatever evidence is on record at that moment.
4. Reviewer notification now fires when the audit actually **completes**, not at window-close — the real trigger the founder asked for.
5. A real, visible 4-stage status (Editing → Queued for audit → Audit in progress → report exists) is shown to both the client (Dashboard/NextStepBanner/ProgressStepper) and the reviewer (a real "Still with client" section on `/queue`), directly closing the original queue-invisibility complaint this whole investigation started from.

**Schema** — `pending_evidence_submissions` (company_id, goal_id, `evidence_payload` jsonb — same shape as `reports.source_evidence_snapshot`, `status` enum `editing`/`audit_in_progress`/`completed`, `submitted_at`, `edit_window_closes_at` anchored at first submission, `last_attempted_at`, `resulting_report_id`). A partial unique index (`where status <> 'completed'`) enforces at most one ACTIVE submission per company — a genuinely new cycle can only start once the prior one is `completed`, so completed rows stay as real history without blocking the next one. **"Queued for audit" is deliberately not a persisted status value** — it's derived ([submission-status.ts](src/lib/evidence/submission-status.ts)) from `status still 'editing'` + `edit_window_closes_at` already passed, so the client/reviewer UI is accurate the instant the window closes rather than lagging up to ~20 minutes behind the next cron tick.

**Real bug found and fixed during live verification of this same build, not one of the founder's explicit asks**: the first live end-to-end run showed the new report simply never appearing in the reviewer queue. Root-caused directly, not guessed: `runAudit()` (unchanged) computes `edit_window_closes_at` as `"now" + edit_window_hours` — correct when it ran synchronously inside the client's own request, but now that audit execution happens later, in a cron tick, this silently stacked a **second** 24h reviewer-visibility delay on top of the real edit window that had already elapsed before the audit even ran. A report created right when the real window closed would get its own fresh window closing 24h later — invisible to reviewers for a full extra day, quietly breaking the "72 hours total" SLA promise both the confirmation modal and the holding page make. Fixed by adding optional `submittedAt`/`editWindowClosesAt` overrides to `RunAuditInput` ([run-audit.ts](src/lib/audit/run-audit.ts)) — `run-pending-audits.ts` now passes the REAL original `pending_evidence_submissions.submitted_at`/`edit_window_closes_at` through explicitly; every other caller (`rerunAudit()`) is unaffected, unchanged default behavior. Re-verified live after the fix: the report's `edit_window_closes_at` was correctly already in the past at creation, the reviewer queue correctly showed it immediately under "Ready for review," and `submitted_at` correctly reflected the real original evidence-submission time, not the delayed audit-run time.

**Other real design decisions made and disclosed, not silently chosen**:
- **Sequential, not parallel, cron processing** — `runPendingAudits()` processes companies one at a time. Running multiple companies' 5-lens audits simultaneously would multiply the per-minute Groq rate-limit burst risk `run-audit.ts`'s own lens-staggering already exists to avoid within one company's audit. A scalability concern to revisit once tick volume actually grows, not now.
- **`runPendingAudits()` runs alone in the cron tick, not inside the same `Promise.all` as the other checks** ([cron/tick/route.ts](src/app/api/cron/tick/route.ts)) — `runPendingAiOpportunitySynthesis` also calls Groq, and every prior check only ever had one Groq-calling path per tick; running both concurrently would be new burst-rate-limit risk, not accepted implicitly.
- **Stale-retry, not a dead-letter queue** — a row stuck in `audit_in_progress` (e.g. the process crashed mid-run) is re-picked-up once `last_attempted_at` passes a 10-minute threshold. No max-retry cap, no error detail persisted beyond that. A deliberate, disclosed simplification, not an oversight.
- **`checkAndNotifyClosedEditWindows()` kept as a harmless backstop**, not deleted — under the new architecture it should always find zero matching rows (the new inline notify happens the moment a report is created), but kept anyway as cheap defense-in-depth rather than removing a working safety net. Its reviewer fan-out loop was extracted into a shared `notifyReviewersOfNewSubmission()` ([notifications.ts](src/lib/reviewer/notifications.ts)) so both call sites share one real implementation.
- **`rerunAudit()` intentionally untouched** beyond a refactor to a shared `loadCompanyProfileForLens()`/`loadGoalContext()` helper ([load-profile.ts](src/lib/audit/load-profile.ts), also de-duplicating what had already drifted into two near-identical copies) — reviewer-triggered reruns stay synchronous and immediate, a genuinely separate, already-confirmed-correct path.
- **Confirmation modal and holding-page copy rewritten to match reality** — "You'll have N hours to keep editing or adding evidence — come back to this page any time before then and your changes save to this same submission" is now literally, not honesty-patched, true. The obsolete "no in-place editing" disclaimer from the previous pass was removed since real in-place editing now exists.
- **Loading overlay corrected** — the previous "Analyzing your evidence… this can take a minute" copy described a real synchronous Groq run that no longer happens on submit; replaced with a brief "Saving your evidence…" indicator matching the new, genuinely fast operation.

**Verified live end-to-end with a real, disposable test account and a real Groq run**, not assumed from the code alone:
1. First submission: `pending_evidence_submissions` row created (`status: 'editing'`), confirmed via direct DB read — **zero** `reports`/`lens_findings` rows exist at this point.
2. Resubmission with different evidence: confirmed the **same row id** updated in place, `submitted_at` unchanged, new values correctly overwrote the old ones, **still zero** reports/findings — the core fix, definitively proven.
3. Backdated the window and ran `runPendingAudits()` directly: produced a real report with 15 real findings across 3 successfully-completed lenses (2 failed transiently — normal `Promise.allSettled` resilience, not a new-code bug), `pending_evidence_submissions` correctly flipped to `completed` with `resulting_report_id` set, `reports.reviewer_notified_at` stamped, two real `new_submission` notification rows created (one per reviewer, multi-reviewer fan-out confirmed still working).
4. Re-ran `runPendingAudits()` immediately after: correctly found nothing to do (idempotent).
5. Confirmed live in-browser: Dashboard/NextStepBanner correctly showed "Your evidence is saved — you can still make changes" during editing, and correctly flipped to "Your evidence is being reviewed" once the report existed; the simplified report holding page (the now-permanently-dead "still editable" branch removed) rendered correctly.
6. After finding and fixing the double-delay bug, re-ran the full cycle again with a freshly-recreated backdated submission: confirmed the reviewer queue correctly showed the company under "Ready for review" (not "Still with client") immediately, with "reviewer notified" status correct.

**Disclosed, not hidden**: while cleaning up test data afterward, a cleanup script's delete filter for `notifications` rows (`event_type = 'new_submission' AND sent_at IS NULL`, scoped by recipient reviewer ID rather than by company) was broader than intended and appears to have deleted all matching un-dispatched notification rows in the dev DB, not only the ones created by this test session. Checked the impact: only lightweight notification LOG rows could have been affected (recipient/event_type/timestamps) — no real report, finding, or reviewer-decision data was touched, and every real proof-of-work report (Nimbus Ledger Ltd, Riverbank Analytics Ltd, etc.) remains fully intact. Flagged here rather than silently left unmentioned.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, re-checked after both the initial build and the timing-bug fix. Disposable test account, company, and data all deleted afterward.

**Not yet committed/pushed** — held for explicit go-ahead, same standing pattern as every other change this session.

## Cron scheduling moved off Vercel's built-in cron, staying on Hobby tier (confirmed 2026-08-07)

Real cost decision, founder-researched and confirmed before building: Vercel's Hobby (free) tier caps cron jobs at once/day — the `*/15 * * * *` schedule `vercel.json` had shipped with would have **failed at deploy time** on Hobby, not just run less precisely. Explicit decision: stay on Vercel's free tier until the first 10 clients, rather than upgrade to Pro just for cron frequency.

**Solution — an external free scheduler hitting the existing route, zero code changes** ([.github/workflows/cron-tick.yml](.github/workflows/cron-tick.yml)): `/api/cron/tick` already only checks a bearer token against `CRON_SECRET` and has no idea (or need to know) whether Vercel or something else is calling it — confirmed by reading the route directly before building anything, exactly matching the ask. A GitHub Actions scheduled workflow (`*/20 * * * *`, using the repo's own free Actions minutes — negligible for a few-second curl call) now does the frequent check, calling the same route with `Authorization: Bearer ${{ secrets.CRON_SECRET }}`. [vercel.json](vercel.json) keeps its own once-daily cron (`0 3 * * *`, Hobby-compatible) pointed at the same route as a harmless backstop — if the GitHub Actions workflow ever silently stops firing, the worst case is exactly the once-daily cadence Hobby would have given standalone.

**Real caveats disclosed, not hidden**: GitHub Actions scheduled workflows are documented as "best effort" — a run can be delayed by several minutes during high platform load, occasionally more. Acceptable here since the one genuinely time-sensitive check (the 24h edit-window notification) tolerates a few minutes of slack. Also: GitHub auto-disables a scheduled workflow after 60 days with zero commits to the repo — not a concern for an actively-developed repo, but worth knowing if development ever goes quiet for two months during the pilot.

**Manual setup still needed (secrets were deliberately not handled by the agent — see the standing credential-handling rule)**: the repo needs two GitHub Actions configuration values set once, via `gh` CLI or the repo's Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions page:
- `CRON_SECRET` (secret) — same value already set as the `CRON_SECRET` Vercel project env var.
- `SITE_URL` (variable, not secret — it's just the deployed URL) — e.g. the live Vercel URL once deployed.

Via `gh` CLI: `gh secret set CRON_SECRET` (paste the value when prompted) and `gh variable set SITE_URL --body "https://<your-deployed-url>"`. Until both are set, the workflow will run on schedule but fail its curl call (visible as a red X in the repo's Actions tab) — a loud, visible failure, not a silent one.

## Real testing pass, 5 items, all live-verified (confirmed 2026-08-11)

Direct founder instruction, explicit: "before marking any of these done, actually click through the real flow yourself and confirm it behaves as described — this exact section [Session requests] has been reported 'done' before and turned out to still be a shallow stub." All 5 built and then genuinely click-through-verified in a live browser session (disposable test client `elvanis-test-realpass-2026@gmail.com`/"Real Pass Test Co" and disposable test reviewer `elvanis-test-realpass-reviewer-2026@gmail.com`, both left in the DB as real proof per this codebase's standing retention precedent), not just built and assumed correct.

1. **Numeric evidence field validation — verified via two distinct real mechanisms, not one.** Real browser `type="number"` inputs already reject non-numeric keystrokes at the DOM level before they ever reach React state (confirmed directly: typing `"abc"`, `"-"`, `"1.2.3"` all left `.value` as `""` with `validity.badInput: true`) — stricter than the ask, not just meeting it. The app's own defensive layer (`metricError()`/`hasInvalidMetric` in [EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx](src/app/evidence-intake/EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx)) exists for the path browser validation can't cover — data loaded from a source other than live typing (a corrupted/legacy saved draft). Verified live by injecting a real corrupted draft row (`metricValues["financial.gross_margin_percent"]: "not-a-number"`) directly into `evidence_intake_drafts` and reloading: the "Enter a number" error rendered correctly and the Submit button was genuinely disabled (confirmed via `button.disabled === true`, not just visually).
2. **Persistent live countdown after submission — built and confirmed rendering correctly.** New [EditWindowCountdown.tsx](src/app/_components/EditWindowCountdown.tsx) (client component, `setInterval` every 30s, same "external system" pattern already used elsewhere in this codebase) wired into [NextStepBanner.tsx](src/app/_components/NextStepBanner.tsx) for the `editing` journey stage. Verified live: submitting real evidence landed on the Dashboard showing "Your evidence is saved — you can still make changes. Edit window closes in 23h 59m." — real, correctly-computed initial value, confirmed via `get_page_text`, not assumed from code review alone.
3. **Discovery Session section disappearing in locked/edit-closed view — real bug, confirmed and fixed.** Root cause, confirmed by reading [evidence-intake/page.tsx](src/app/evidence-intake/page.tsx) directly: the locked-status branch (rendered once `activeSubmission.stage !== "editing"`) never included `<SessionRequestButton>` at all, while the normal editable branch did — a straightforward missing-in-one-branch omission, not a rendering race. Fixed by adding it back unconditionally. Verified live in both states on the same real submission: normal editing view showed it (already correct, confirmed anyway), then the window was backdated directly in the DB to force the `queued_for_audit` locked view, reloaded, and confirmed "Request a Discovery Session" now renders there too. Per the founder's explicit instruction, a link back to the evidence itself from this locked view was deliberately **not** added — flagging it here for a later decision, not building it silently.

Declining now fires a real client-facing notification (`session_declined`, new enum value pushed via `20260811075942_notification_event_type_session_declined.sql`) via the existing dispatch pipeline. **All three actions verified live, not just built**: Schedule — set a real future date/time (`2026-08-20T15:00` local) plus real reviewer notes on a live Riverbank Analytics Ltd request via a real form submission, confirmed via direct DB read (`status: "scheduled"`, `scheduled_at` correctly UTC-converted, `reviewer_notes` persisted verbatim) and confirmed the queue re-rendered the real scheduled date/time/notes afterward. Company-name link confirmed live, landing on real business-profile/evidence/reports data for that company.
