## "Submit now" fast-track + countdown-copy clarity pass (confirmed 2026-08-11)

Two additions requested together, both live-verified before being marked done, same discipline as the real testing pass above.

**1. "Submit now, I'm done editing" fast-track.** A deliberate, separate action from the normal 24h wait — closes the edit window immediately and runs the real audit right then, exactly once, with its own explicit warning-copy confirmation modal ("This closes your edit window right now and starts the analysis immediately — you won't be able to make further changes after this").

- **Race-safety is the whole point, not a side effect** — the founder's explicit ask was "make sure this doesn't reopen the accidental-duplicate-Groq-call problem we just fixed." [claimPendingEvidenceSubmissionForImmediateAudit()](src/lib/evidence/pending-submission.ts) is a single conditional UPDATE (`status = 'editing' -> 'audit_in_progress' WHERE status = 'editing'`), the same atomic-claim pattern already used for the cron path, so a client's click and a cron tick that happen to land on the same row at the same instant can only ever have one winner — Postgres's own row-level UPDATE atomicity is the entire guarantee, no separate lock table. **The cron's OWN claim step had a real, previously-unguarded gap this surfaced**: `run-pending-audits.ts`'s claim UPDATE was unconditional-by-id, not conditioned on the row's prior status — before this fix, a "Submit now" click racing a cron tick on the exact same row could have caused the cron to blindly re-claim and re-process a row someone else already grabbed, reintroducing the duplicate-Groq-call bug through a new second caller. Fixed by conditioning the cron's claim on the row's actual previous status too (`.eq("status", row.previousStatus)`), checking the returned row count, and backing off cleanly (added to `stillPending`) if it lost the race — the exact same discipline applied to both callers, not two different mechanisms.
- **`runAuditForClaimedSubmission()`** ([run-pending-audits.ts](src/lib/audit/run-pending-audits.ts)) is the shared code path both the cron and "Submit now" call once a row is claimed — extracted from the former private `processRow()`, exported so there's exactly one place that runs the actual audit, never two drifting copies.
- **`edit_window_closes_at` is set to the real moment of the click, not the original future value** — same reasoning already established for the cron's own "don't stack a second reviewer-visibility delay" fix: the client explicitly closed their own window early, so that's genuinely when it closed, and the resulting report is immediately reviewer-visible by construction. `submitted_at` stays the real original submission time, untouched.
- **`submitEvidenceNow()`** ([evidence-intake/actions.ts](src/app/evidence-intake/actions.ts)) reuses `submitEvidence()` for its first step (same upsert, same ownership check, same privacy-acknowledgment gate) so nothing typed since the last save is silently lost, then claims and runs. A genuinely synchronous, real five-lens Groq call inside this one request (same actual duration the pre-delayed-execution architecture used to show a loading state for) — given its own honest "Analyzing your evidence…" overlay, distinct from the fast "Saving your evidence…" copy the normal (now store-only) submit uses.
- **Real bug found and fixed during live verification, not anticipated**: the new "Submit now" button wasn't gated on `!privacyAcknowledged` the way the normal submit button already is — clicking it before checking the privacy box correctly failed server-side (the shared `submitEvidence()` ownership/consent check caught it) but the resulting error rendered only near the bottom-of-form privacy section, easy to miss when the action that caused it was a button near the top of the page. Fixed by rendering the same shared error state a second time, directly below the "Submit now" button itself.
- **Verified live end-to-end**, real disposable test account, real Groq run: submitted evidence normally (creates the 'editing' row), returned to `/evidence-intake`, confirmed Cancel on the fast-track modal genuinely does nothing (DB unchanged), then confirmed for real — DB read mid-flight showed `status: audit_in_progress`, `edit_window_closes_at` correctly set to the real claim moment (not the original future value), `submitted_at` unchanged; the loading overlay's "Analyzing your evidence…" copy was confirmed actually present via direct DOM query, not assumed from a screenshot; ~40s later the client was redirected straight to `/reports/[reportId]`, which correctly rendered the real holding page; a DB read confirmed exactly one `reports` row existed, `pending_evidence_submissions.status: completed` with the matching `resulting_report_id`, `reviewer_notified_at` stamped, and real `new_submission` notifications created for all three reviewers on file.
- **Race-safety proven directly against real Postgres concurrency, not just reasoned about, two ways**: (a) two truly concurrent calls to `claimPendingEvidenceSubmissionForImmediateAudit()` on the same row via `Promise.all` — exactly one returned `claimed: true`, the other correctly returned the "already queued or being analyzed" error, zero duplicate processing; (b) the more literal scenario the founder actually described — `runPendingAudits()` (the cron) raced directly against `claimPendingEvidenceSubmissionForImmediateAudit()` (the "Submit now" path) on the same due row via `Promise.all` — the submit-now claim won, the cron's own result correctly showed `processedReportIds: []` with the company in `stillPending` (backed off cleanly, no double-run), and a DB read confirmed the company still had exactly one `reports` row total, not two.

**2. Countdown-copy clarity pass.** Both real live-countdown surfaces rewritten with explicit paired language, not a bare timer:
- [NextStepBanner.tsx](src/app/_components/NextStepBanner.tsx) (Dashboard/Business Profile/Reports): "You can still make changes. Edit window closes in {timer}." → "You still have {timer} to review and change anything in your submission — after that, review begins automatically."
- [EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx](src/app/evidence-intake/EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx): a genuinely new countdown, previously absent from this page entirely (the only live countdown lived on Dashboard, away from the actual fields it applies to) — "You have {timer} to review and change anything below," directly above the editable form, doubling as the home for the new "Submit now" button since both belong together conceptually.
- **Verified live**: submitted real evidence, confirmed the Dashboard/NextStepBanner rendered the new explicit copy with a real live timer value (not a placeholder), then confirmed the new on-page countdown banner on `/evidence-intake` itself rendered correctly with the same real value and the "Submit now, I'm done editing →" link directly beneath it.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, re-checked after the privacy-gating bug fix. Disposable test account, company, goal, report, and findings created purely to drive this verification deleted afterward (a verification pass, not a first-of-its-kind proof run worth retaining — same precedent as the prior real-testing-pass batch).

Committed and pushed, per explicit go-ahead.

## Real bug list from live testing, round 2 — root cause on the "invisible report" bug, plus two new reviewer-workflow features (confirmed 2026-08-12)

**Priority item — "delivered report not visible to client" — root-caused, confirmed NOT a code bug.** Extensive reproduction (direct RLS query with a real session token, real browser navigation including a deliberately-engineered stale-Router-Cache scenario, a check of every `sent` report in the DB for structural corruption, notification-content check) all came back clean — the report correctly displayed via every real path tested. The actual explanation, confirmed directly with the founder: the report that had been reviewed/delivered (twice — hence the two `report_ready` notifications originally flagged as a real anomaly) belonged to a **different** client account (`elvanis-test-realpass-2026@gmail.com` / "Real Pass Test Co") than the one being checked on the frontend (`sallym.abbas@gmail.com` / company "Sally") — RLS correctly scoped reports to the owning company, so "Sally" showing nothing was exactly correct, since that company genuinely had no delivered report. A real, separate thing this surfaced along the way: "Sally"'s own evidence submission had been sitting overdue for ~2 hours with zero report ever generated, because nothing runs `runPendingAudits()` automatically in local dev (only the deployed site's GitHub Actions job does) — triggered it manually to give the founder a real report to review end-to-end (`617f930e-…`, 18 real findings across 4 lenses).

**48h review-SLA — real overdue tracking built (direct founder request, confirmed).** [sla.ts](src/lib/reports/sla.ts)'s own earlier docblock had already flagged this exact tradeoff — "migrating it to app_settings now would let the copy promise a number nothing actually holds to... only promote it alongside building real enforcement for it." That's what this is: `reports.review_due_at` (`edit_window_closes_at + review_period_hours`, stamped once at report-creation time in [run-audit.ts](src/lib/audit/run-audit.ts), same "compute it now, don't recompute against a possibly-later setting" principle already used for `edit_window_closes_at` itself) plus a real red "Overdue" badge on the reviewer queue's "Ready for review" section once it passes (`app_settings.review_period_hours`, DB-backed, replacing the hardcoded `REVIEW_PERIOD_HOURS` constant — same pattern as `edit_window_hours`). Scoped to Core Audit reports only for now, not modules/sprints, which have no equivalent deadline concept yet — flagged in-code rather than silently guessed at. **Verified live**: the real, years-old Nimbus Ledger Ltd reports (genuinely `pending_review` since 2026-08-01) correctly show "OVERDUE," while a `Tender Readiness` module item in the same list correctly does not.

**Conflict Detection — two real problems, both fixed, direct founder request.**
1. **Raw finding UUIDs leaking into conflict descriptions** ("Finding 46ec1ae8-b4bd-49ce-83f1-141ee3813111 indicates…") — root cause confirmed by reading the prompt directly: rule 2 told the LLM to "cite the EXACT findingId values," and the model was doing exactly that inside the free-text description meant for a human reviewer. Fixed in [conflict-detection.ts](src/lib/synthesis/conflict-detection.ts): `findingAId`/`findingBId` (the structured, machine-linking fields) still require exact IDs; a new explicit rule tells the model `conflictDescription` must describe findings by their actual content, in plain language, never by ID.
2. **The example conflict the founder flagged was a real false positive, not a genuine contradiction** — "live AI in production with no governance documentation" vs. "no risk classification awareness" aren't contradictory; the second is an expected *consequence* of the first, not a competing claim. Added this exact pair as a concrete counter-example directly in the prompt's hard rules. **Verified live**: re-ran Conflict Detection against the exact same finding set that originally produced this false positive — the pair did not reappear; the model instead found two more genuinely defensible tensions (with zero raw UUIDs in either description).

**AI-suggested conflict resolutions — new feature, deliberately reversing this module's original "never resolves anything itself" design (confirmed 2026-08-12, direct founder request).** Conflict Detection's prompt now requires a `suggestedResolution` per conflict — a real, concrete recommendation, not a hedge — persisted to a new `finding_conflicts.ai_suggested_resolution` column. Same "AI drafts, reviewer decides" pattern as every finding in this app: shown as its own distinct box in the Reviewer Workspace (visually separate from the conflict description itself, so it reads as a suggestion to evaluate, not a stated fact), and the resolution form is now prefilled with it — the reviewer can save as-is, edit first, or clear it and write their own. **Verified live end-to-end**: inserted a real Groq-generated conflict+suggestion pair into a real report, confirmed the "Suggested resolution" box rendered correctly in the browser, clicked "Resolve Conflict," confirmed the form's input was genuinely prefilled with the exact suggested text.

Migration: [20260812100000_conflict_ai_suggestion_and_review_due_at.sql](supabase/migrations/20260812100000_conflict_ai_suggestion_and_review_due_at.sql) — `reports.review_due_at` (backfilled for all existing reports), `finding_conflicts.ai_suggested_resolution`, `app_settings.review_period_hours` seeded 48. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout. Test conflict and backdated timestamp used for live verification were cleaned up / restored to correct values afterward — no synthetic data left on the real "Sally" report.

**Still open, per the founder's own explicit sequencing** — items #1 (`/evidence-intake` missing site nav), #2 (unstyled empty states), #4 (no client-visible evidence history), and the full honest page-by-page re-test of the entire client journey, all deferred until this priority item and its follow-ups were closed.

## Issues #1 and #2 from the round-2 bug list closed (confirmed 2026-08-12)

**#1 — `/evidence-intake` missing site header/nav, confirmed isolated, not systemic.** Root cause: this page had lived at `src/app/evidence-intake/` (a top-level route) since it was first built, with no documented reason for living outside `(app)` — unlike `onboarding`/`client-login`/`reviewer-login`, which are deliberately outside `(app)` for real, stated reasons (e.g. onboarding must be reachable before a `companies` row exists, so `(app)/layout.tsx`'s own redirect-there logic can't be circular). Every other genuinely authenticated, core-product client page already lived correctly inside `(app)` and inherited its shared charcoal nav — this was a real, one-off oversight, confirmed by checking every other top-level route had its own actual reason. Fixed with a clean `git mv` of all 3 files (`page.tsx`/`actions.ts`/`EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx`) into `src/app/(app)/evidence-intake/` — route groups don't affect the URL, so `/evidence-intake` is unchanged, and no import paths needed edits (every file already used `@/lib/...` absolute or same-directory relative imports). **Verified live**: a fresh disposable test session loading `/evidence-intake` now correctly renders the full charcoal nav bar — "Elvanis" wordmark, Dashboard/Business Profile/Reports & History/Account Settings, account email, Sign out — confirmed via `get_page_text`, matching every other client-facing page.

**#2 — unstyled empty states, audited app-wide, two real gaps found and fixed.** Grepped the whole client-facing app tree for empty-state text patterns first, not assumed from the one flagged example. Found the reviewer-only surfaces (`/queue`'s "No sprints yet.", `/company/[companyId]`'s "No goal set yet."/"No reports yet."/"No module requests yet.") were **not** the same gap — each already sits inside a real, styled `Card` alongside other populated Cards, a normal, acceptable "no items in this data field" pattern for internal tooling, not a bare page-body placeholder. Two real client-facing gaps matching the founder's actual complaint:
1. **Reports & History's "Nothing delivered yet."** — a single bare, unstyled line with zero next-step guidance, the exact flagged example. Fixed by reusing the existing `NextStepBanner`/`computeJourneyStatus()`/`ProgressStepper` machinery already built for Dashboard/Business Profile/Evidence Intake (2026-08-07) instead of inventing a new empty-state component — same single source of truth, so this page now correctly distinguishes "never submitted" from "in review" from every other real journey stage, with a real actionable CTA, instead of one generic sentence. **Verified live**: a fresh test account with zero evidence submitted saw the real amber `NextStepBanner` ("Next: submit your evidence" + a working "Submit evidence" button) and the `ProgressStepper` correctly at step 2, screenshotted to confirm real visual weight (border, padding, amber accent), not a bare line.
2. **The client report page's "No findings to show yet."** — a rare edge case (every finding on an otherwise-delivered report was rejected during review) that was still a bare `<p>` with no visual weight, easy to misread as a rendering error. Wrapped in the shared `Alert` component (`variant="info"`, the same message-design primitive built during the earlier app-wide message sweep) with real copy explaining what happened, instead of a plain sentence.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout both fixes. Disposable test account/company/goal used to verify #2 deleted afterward (a UI-verification pass, not a first-of-its-kind proof run worth retaining). Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route created and deleted twice during this pass (once per fix), never committed — confirmed via `git status` before each writeup.

**Still open, per the founder's original sequencing**: #4 (no client-visible evidence intake history) and the full honest page-by-page re-test of signup-through-delivery.

## Issue #4 from the round-2 bug list closed — client-visible evidence intake history (confirmed 2026-08-12)

**Real gap confirmed before building**: "submission date, edit date... not retained/viewable anywhere on their side" was true in two distinct ways, checked separately rather than assumed as one bug:
1. **No edit-date tracking existed at all.** `pending_evidence_submissions.submitted_at` is deliberately anchored once at first submission and never reset (a real SLA-integrity guarantee, not an oversight — see that table's own migration docblock) — but nothing else on the row tracked when a later in-window resubmit actually happened. Every edit was a plain `UPDATE` with no timestamp column to bump.
2. **A client could only ever see their own submitted answers AFTER a report was fully reviewed and delivered.** The 2026-08-06 "Evidence library on client report" work put a real `EVIDENCE_FIELD_SETS`-driven disclosure on the delivered report page, reading `reports.source_evidence_snapshot` — but the moment evidence left the editable `editing` stage (queued for audit / being analyzed), the locked view on `/evidence-intake` showed only a status label, with zero way to review what was actually submitted, for however long that lock lasted.

**Fixed both, not just one:**
- **`pending_evidence_submissions.updated_at`** (new column, `supabase/migrations/20260812110000_pending_evidence_submissions_updated_at.sql`, backfilled to `submitted_at` for pre-existing rows) — [pending-submission.ts](src/lib/evidence/pending-submission.ts)'s `upsertPendingEvidenceSubmission()` now bumps it on every real in-window edit; `loadActivePendingEvidenceSubmission()` returns both `submittedAt`/`updatedAt` for the UI to render honestly (a "last edited" line only shown when it genuinely differs from `submittedAt` by more than a minute, so a never-edited submission doesn't show the same moment twice).
- **Extracted the evidence-rendering block into a real shared component** ([EvidenceSubmittedDisclosure.tsx](src/app/_components/EvidenceSubmittedDisclosure.tsx)) instead of duplicating it — the ~90-line block previously lived only inline in the delivered client Report page; both that page and the `/evidence-intake` locked view now render from the one implementation, same "shared logic, can't drift" discipline already used for `deriveRoadmap()`/`getTotalTurnaroundHours()`/`computeJourneyStatus()`. The locked view on [evidence-intake/page.tsx](src/app/(app)/evidence-intake/page.tsx) now shows real `Submitted {date}` / `last edited {date}` text plus the actual submitted content via this shared component (`activeSubmission.evidencePayload` is the same shape as `source_evidence_snapshot`, confirmed structurally compatible) — a client whose evidence is locked/queued/analyzing can now see exactly what they submitted, not just a status label. The editing view on [EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx](src/app/(app)/evidence-intake/EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx) got the same real submitted/edited dates, placed directly in the existing countdown banner.
- **Deliberately not touched**: `/demo-live` has its own separate, un-extracted inline copy of this same rendering block — left alone since it's a fixed, already-verified, read-only public demo page, and touching it wasn't necessary to close this gap. Flagged here as a known, accepted duplication, not a silent oversight.

**Verified live, real data, not assumed from code review**: a fresh disposable test account (`elvanis-test-evidencehistory-2026@gmail.com`) had real evidence inserted directly (gross margin 62%, a named competitor "Acme Rival Co," market-change notes, etc.) — the editing view correctly showed "Submitted 8/12/2026, 11:18:59 AM" with no edit-date line (since none had happened yet); after bumping `updated_at` forward 5 minutes to simulate a real edit, reloading correctly showed "Submitted 8/12/2026, 11:18:59 AM · last edited 8/12/2026, 11:24:23 AM"; after backdating `edit_window_closes_at` to force the locked "Queued for audit" view, reloading correctly showed both dates AND a full "What you submitted" disclosure rendering the exact real content entered — gross margin 62, the margin-trend sentence, the named competitor, the market-change note — confirmed via `get_page_text` and a screenshot. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout. Disposable test account/company/data and the temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route all deleted afterward.

**Still open**: the full honest page-by-page re-test of signup-through-delivery, per the founder's original explicit request ("I want a genuine, comprehensive re-test of the entire client journey... checked page by page, not assumed working because individual pieces were tested in isolation before").

## Full honest page-by-page re-test, signup through report delivery (confirmed 2026-08-12)

The re-test the founder explicitly asked for, closing out the round-2 bug list. Driven through real browser clicks with a genuinely fresh disposable client account (`elvanis-test-fullretest-2026@gmail.com`, company "Fulltest Analytics Ltd") and the existing disposable stranger-pass reviewer, page by page, not assumed working from prior isolated tests. Kept in the DB as real proof, same retention precedent as Nimbus Ledger Test Co / Riverbank Analytics Ltd — this is a first-of-its-kind full run, not routine debris.

**Path walked, every step through real UI interaction (not scripted DB writes), unless noted**: landing page → "Get started" → real `/client-login` signup (real magic-link send + real OTP verification) → real 5-step onboarding wizard (company, goal, refine, details, review — every field filled, secondary-goal dropdown correctly excluded the already-selected primary) → redirected correctly to `/evidence-intake` (confirming the earlier "stuck on Creating…" router-refresh-race fix still holds) → real evidence typed into all lens sections (financial metrics deliberately set to trigger real findings: 58% gross margin against the 60% threshold, 9-month runway against 6/12-month thresholds, 30% customer concentration; AI & Governance deliberately set to live-AI-in-production + all 7 governance dimensions at 0, to exercise the deterministic guaranteed finding) → real "Submit for review" with the real confirmation modal (correct copy, correctly free-tier) → real "Submit now, I'm done editing" fast-track with its own real warning-copy modal → real 5-lens Groq audit ran end-to-end (all 5 lenses succeeded, 20 real findings, zero failures) → real reviewer login (hit the known Supabase Auth per-email rate-limit failure class on the UI send — not an app bug, confirmed via the same fallback OTP-verification technique already established this session) → real Reviewer Workspace pass (18 Accept, 1 Edit with a genuine appended reviewer note verified persisted verbatim, 1 Reject, zero conflicts detected this run) → real "Approve report" (correctly blocked-then-succeeded pattern already proven, `approved_at`/`reviewed_by` stamped) → real "Deliver report" (`status: sent`, `delivered_at` stamped, a real `report_ready` notification row created for the correct recipient) → back to the client session → real Dashboard, Reports & History, the delivered Report page (top-3, 30/60/90 roadmap, severity badges, the "NO EVIDENCE SUBMITTED" distinct treatment, sprint-interest buttons, and the item-#4 evidence-submitted disclosure all rendering the exact real submitted content) → Business Profile.

**What this confirmed working, matching every documented behavior, not just "looked fine":**
- All three round-2 fixes (#1 nav, #2 empty states, #4 evidence history) held under a completely independent run, not just their own original verification.
- The deterministic benchmark-comparison sentences rendered exactly as designed ("Gross margin is 58%, which is below the 60% concerning threshold (healthy range is 70-80%)").
- The deterministic guaranteed finding ("Live AI in production with no governance documentation") fired correctly and became the #1 top-3 priority.
- The already-documented "insufficient evidence" prompt-quality nuance (a finding claiming no financial figures were submitted despite real metrics being present in an adjacent finding) reproduced exactly as previously described — confirming it's a real, known, low-severity LLM-wording quirk, not a regression.
- The onboarding-wizard router-refresh fix, the Submit-now race-safety architecture, the reviewer mandatory-decision gate, the Approve/Deliver pipeline, and the notification-on-delivery logic all held under fresh, independent exercise.

**One real, self-inflicted process error caught and corrected mid-run, not an app bug**: the first "Submit now" click appeared to silently do nothing — no error, no navigation, no DB change. Investigated properly rather than assumed: network logs showed the server action *was* called and returned 200, but `pending_evidence_submissions.status` never advanced. Root cause, confirmed directly: the privacy-acknowledgment checkbox resets on every fresh page load (documented, existing behavior), and after navigating to a fresh `/evidence-intake` load I had filled every field except re-checking that box — the server correctly rejected the submission, and the already-fixed "show the error near the Submit-now button too" UX fix (from the earlier round-2 pass) rendered the rejection message correctly, twice, exactly as designed. Confirmed by reading the actual error alert in the DOM, not assumed. Re-checked the box and resubmitted cleanly.

**One real test-tooling lesson, not an app bug**: an initial attempt to accept all 20 findings via a scripted loop clicking "the first Accept button repeatedly" only produced 1 real acceptance despite 19 reported clicks — because Accept/Edit/Reject buttons deliberately stay visible after a decision (so a reviewer can change their mind), so "click the first Accept button" kept re-submitting the same already-decided finding. Not a bug — a reasonable design choice the naive script didn't account for. Fixed by targeting each finding's own "needs decision" badge specifically; all 20 findings then decided correctly and verified via direct DB reads after each batch.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean before and after. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route created and deleted three times during this pass (client signup, reviewer login, refreshed client session), never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup. No new app bugs found in this pass — every finding was either a confirmation that an existing fix holds, or a self-inflicted test-process error, both disclosed above rather than glossed over.

## Real document upload (PDF/DOCX) for AI & Governance, Tender Readiness, Data Protection Compliance (confirmed 2026-08-12)

Direct founder question before this was built: "does the client-facing intake for Tender Readiness and Data Protection Compliance support real document upload for the AI to actually read and audit — same 'document-review mode' concept already built into AI & Governance?" Investigated by reading the actual code, not trusting this file's own prose — **no module supported it, not even AI & Governance's own "document-review mode,"** which turned out to be a free-text description textarea, not real document ingestion. Reported honestly, then built for real per explicit direction: "I want to fix this now, not defer it with CSV parsing — it's a different, smaller problem: real document upload (PDF/DOCX) with text extraction, feeding the extracted content into the same prompts that currently only receive typed descriptions... No column-mapping complexity like the CSV case — just extract text, replace the typed-summary input with real document content." Deliberately the smaller problem, not the still-deferred native-export/CSV pipeline (see the Evidence Intake scope decision above) — extract-only, no persistence of the raw file (explicit founder decision, confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: "extract only, discard the file... consistent with how evidence is already stored elsewhere in the app").

**Shared extraction primitive** ([src/lib/documents/extract-text.ts](src/lib/documents/extract-text.ts)) — `extractTextFromDocument(file: File)` using `pdf-parse` v2 (PDF) and `mammoth` (DOCX), `.pdf`/`.docx` only (`.doc` gets an explicit, specific rejection message rather than silently mis-parsing), 10MB ceiling (`MAX_DOCUMENT_SIZE_BYTES`, kept in sync with `next.config.ts`'s `serverActions.bodySizeLimit`), and a near-empty-extraction floor (<50 chars treated as a scanned/image-only PDF with no text layer, not a false-positive success) — same "missing evidence is itself a finding" discipline applied here at the point of ingestion. [src/lib/documents/actions.ts](src/lib/documents/actions.ts)'s `extractDocumentTextAction()` is the one shared `"use server"` Server Action all three surfaces call. [src/app/_components/ui/DocumentUploadField.tsx](src/app/_components/ui/DocumentUploadField.tsx) is the one shared client component (upload/extract/error UI only — the parent decides what to do with the extracted text via `onExtracted`).

**A real, genuinely hard bug found, root-caused, and fixed — not worked around.** The first live upload attempt failed with `Setting up fake worker failed: Cannot find module '.../.next/dev/server/chunks/ssr/pdf.worker.mjs'`. Root cause, confirmed by reading `pdfjs-dist`'s own source directly (`node_modules/pdfjs-dist/legacy/build/pdf.mjs`, `PDFWorker#_setupFakeWorkerGlobal`), not guessed: pdf-parse's Node-side text extraction runs via a "fake worker" (in-process, no real worker thread — this is the *correct*, expected code path for a server environment, not itself a bug) that loads via a fully-dynamic `import(this.workerSrc)` call, marked with `/*webpackIgnore*/`/`/*@vite-ignore*/` comments telling webpack and vite not to try to statically bundle it. **There is no Turbopack equivalent of that hint** — Turbopack still intercepts the dynamic import and mis-resolves it to a `.next/dev/server/chunks/ssr/...` path that was never written to disk, regardless of what `GlobalWorkerOptions.workerSrc` is set to at runtime. Two real, documented false starts before the actual fix, both left as commit-history/docblock record rather than silently smoothed over: (1) tried `PDFParse.setWorker()` with a path resolved via `require.resolve("pdf-parse")` — confirmed via debug logging that Turbopack's SSR bundle intercepts `require.resolve` itself, returning a non-real path (`path.dirname()` of `"."`); (2) tried resolving the path from `process.cwd()` instead (which genuinely did resolve to a real, existing file on disk) — had **zero effect on the actual error**, which is what correctly triggered reading pdfjs-dist's own source rather than continuing to guess at path values, since a verified-correct path value making no difference is direct evidence the lever being pulled isn't the one that matters. **Real fix**: `next.config.ts`'s `serverExternalPackages: ["pdf-parse", "pdfjs-dist"]` — keeps the dependency out of the Turbopack/webpack server bundle entirely, so Node's own native ESM resolution loads and executes it untouched, and pdfjs-dist's own default relative worker path (`./pdf.worker.mjs`, resolved against its own module location) just works, since that file genuinely sits right next to it on disk. This let `extract-text.ts` get simpler, not more complex, than either false start — no custom worker-path resolution code needed at all once unbundled. **Verified live against the real, previously-failing PDF** (not just re-reasoned about): real extracted text populated correctly, confirmed via direct DOM read matching the source document's actual content verbatim; confirmed clean via server logs (no error); confirmed via `npm run build` that the fix holds in the production/Turbopack build path too, not only `next dev`.

**A second, smaller real gap found and fixed during the same live pass, not anticipated upfront**: Data Protection Compliance's `DocumentUploadField` instance had its own success message promising "review it below and edit if needed before submitting" (true for AI & Governance and Tender Readiness, which both feed the extraction straight into an existing visible, editable textarea) — but DPC's shared-document design (one upload covering up to five category fields, per the founder's own confirmed decision) meant the extracted text was captured into `existingDocumentationText` state and **never rendered anywhere**, contradicting the widget's own promise. Fixed by giving it its own visible, editable `Textarea`, distinct from the five category fields, once text has been extracted.

**AI & Governance wiring** ([EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx](<src/app/(app)/evidence-intake/EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx>)) — a new `governanceEvidenceSource` state (`"manual" | "parsed"`) tracks provenance using the `EvidenceFieldInput.source` field that already existed in the type, unused, anticipating exactly this. Uploading sets both the textarea content and `source: "parsed"`; typing directly (including editing after an upload) resets it back to `"manual"`.

**Data Protection Compliance wiring** ([index.ts](src/lib/modules/data-protection-compliance/index.ts)) — the harder of the three, since one shared document can cover some but not all of the five category fields, and the module's existing deterministic "guaranteed missing-evidence finding per blank category" logic (`dropDuplicateMissingEvidenceFindings`, pre-existing) assumed a blank category always meant no evidence at all. Rewritten as `reconcileBlankCategoryFindings(llmFindings, categoriesWithoutEvidence, hasSharedDocument)`: when no shared document exists, behavior is unchanged (blank category → guaranteed finding, any LLM finding for that category dropped). When a shared document exists, a blank category is no longer automatically "no evidence" — the LLM is prompted to assess the shared document against every blank category, and the guaranteed missing-evidence finding for that category is only kept if the LLM's own response for it is *itself* `isMissingDataFinding: true` (i.e., the LLM concluded the document doesn't actually address that category either); otherwise the LLM's real finding survives and the guaranteed placeholder is dropped. `buildPrompt()` gained a `sharedDocumentSection` and a conditional `noEvidenceRule` reflecting this. **Verified live end-to-end** against a real DOCX (consent-flow, retention-policy, and data-subject-rights content; deliberately silent on breach-response and cross-border-transfer) with all five category text fields left blank: exactly 5 findings came back — `breach_response`/`cross_border_transfer` correctly got the deterministic guaranteed missing-evidence finding (`isMissingDataFinding: true`), and `consent_flow`/`data_subject_rights`/`retention_policy` correctly got real, non-duplicate LLM findings (`isMissingDataFinding: false`) — confirmed via a direct DB read of `module_findings`, not just the UI.

**Tender Readiness wiring** ([TenderReadinessIntakeForm.tsx](<src/app/(modules)/tender-readiness/TenderReadinessIntakeForm.tsx>)) — simpler, since this module already had exactly one free-text "existing compliance documentation" field; the upload just feeds the same visible textarea the client could otherwise type into. **Verified live**: uploaded PDF's extracted text correctly appeared in the textarea alongside a separately-typed AI-use-case description, correctly persisted into `module_requests.intake_data.existingDocumentation` on submit (confirmed via direct DB read).

**Also verified live**: DOCX extraction (untested until this pass — PDF hit the blocking bug first) works correctly through the real app; the corrupted-file and `.doc`-rejection failure paths still produce their correct, specific error messages after the `serverExternalPackages` fix (re-verified, not assumed unaffected by the bundler-config change). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout.

**Genuinely still deferred, unchanged by this pass**: native CSV/PDF financial-export parsing with per-source column mapping (Xero, QuickBooks, etc.) — a different, larger problem, per the founder's own explicit framing at the start of this task, not silently expanded into.

**Validation coverage, confirmed then tightened before committing (direct founder question)**: extension and size validation both already existed server-side in `extractTextFromDocument()` — `.pdf`/`.docx` only (`.doc` gets its own specific rejection message, anything else a generic one), `MAX_DOCUMENT_SIZE_BYTES` (10MB) plus a zero-byte check. **A real, honest gap found while confirming this, not just a rubber-stamp yes**: `MAX_DOCUMENT_SIZE_BYTES` was set to exactly the same value as `next.config.ts`'s `serverActions.bodySizeLimit` (10MB) — meaning a genuinely oversized file would hit Next's own generic body-size-limit rejection *before* our code ever ran, so the friendly "This file is too large" message could never actually fire for the exact case it exists to explain. Fixed by extracting both constants into a new [src/lib/documents/constants.ts](src/lib/documents/constants.ts) (no Node-only imports, safe to import from a client component — unlike `extract-text.ts`, which imports `pdf-parse`/`mammoth` at module scope) and adding a real client-side pre-check in [DocumentUploadField.tsx](src/app/_components/ui/DocumentUploadField.tsx): an oversized file is now rejected immediately, with the same friendly message and the same ceiling value, before any network call. **Verified live**: dispatched a real 11MB file through the actual upload widget — rejected instantly with "too large (11.0MB) — the limit is 10MB," confirmed via server logs that zero `extractDocumentTextAction` request was ever made (a true client-side catch, not a slow round-trip). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all re-confirmed clean after this fix.

Committed and pushed, per explicit go-ahead.

## Dashboard rebuilt into a genuine unified home page + real /services page (confirmed 2026-08-12)

Direct founder request, explicitly scoped as "a real product redesign, treat it with the same weight as a module build" — the previous Dashboard was a status-only stub (top-3 + roadmap + one Execution Sprint tile, confirmed by reading the old file directly before touching anything). Design principle, the founder's own framing, checked against Vanta/Drata: findings, status, and next actions live in ONE place, not scattered across pages a client has to remember to check separately.

**1. AI Opportunity & Readiness — a genuinely new client-facing surface, not a resurfacing of something hidden.** Grepped for `do_now`/`fix_groundwork_first` across `src/` before starting — confirmed zero client-facing (or reviewer-facing) UI had ever rendered this data; only the synthesis module itself referenced these values. Now its own headline `Card`, equal visual weight to Top 3 (two-column grid, neither subordinate), reading `ai_opportunity_synthesis`/`readiness_scores` for the client's latest `sent` report — RLS scopes both tables to the owning company only (not `sent`-gated the way `reports` itself is), safe here since both queries are always scoped to an already-confirmed-`sent` report.

**A real bug found and fixed during live verification, not anticipated upfront**: the first draft's empty-state check was `opportunities.length === 0`, which conflated two genuinely different states — "synthesis hasn't run yet" (no `readiness_scores` row exists) and "synthesis ran and genuinely found nothing safe to pursue right now" (a real, legitimate outcome — both tables are always written together by the same persist call). Caught this live: ran real synthesis against Nimbus Ledger Ltd's actual current report and got exactly this — real readiness scores, zero opportunities, because governance/data-quality were both 0. The original check would have shown "Not generated yet" for a report that genuinely HAD been synthesized, which is dishonest. Fixed by checking `readiness === null` instead (the real "has synthesis run" signal) and giving the zero-opportunities case its own honest message ("No AI opportunities were identified as safe to pursue right now... that's a genuine assessment, not a missing feature").

**A second real, pre-existing gap surfaced doing this, not fixed here**: confirmed live that no report created after 2026-08-04 has ever had synthesis run — `runPendingAiOpportunitySynthesis()` only picks up reports with `status = 'approved'` (a report that's already progressed to `sent`, as every real report in this session's later test accounts has, falls permanently outside that filter, since nothing re-queues it). This is the same "cron doesn't run automatically in local dev" gap already documented elsewhere in this file, but with a sharper edge here — it means synthesis has silently never run for any of this session's more recent real client accounts (Fulltest Analytics Ltd, Riverbank Analytics Ltd, Sally, Real Pass Test Co) until this pass manually triggered it for two of them to verify the UI. Flagged here, not fixed — closing it properly means either widening the cron query to also catch `sent` reports with `ai_opportunity_synthesized_at is null`, or ensuring the check fires before the status transition happens, and that's a real design decision for the founder, not one to make silently while rebuilding a UI page.

**2. Top 3 priorities** — unchanged rendering, moved to sit alongside AI Opportunity in the same two-column grid rather than full-width above everything else.

**3. Live status tiles**, genuinely new — `Active status` section, only rendered when something real exists (empty is not an error state, Services is where a client starts something new):
- **Module requests** (Tender Readiness/AI Reliability/Data Protection) — real status per request (`Submitted — under review` / `Reviewed — awaiting delivery` / `Delivered`), read via the admin client for the same reason `computeJourneyStatus()` already does: `module_requests`' own RLS only allows a client to SELECT `sent` rows (tightened 2026-08-06 to mirror `reports`), but a client should still see "under review" as a real status rather than silence — only `status`/`module_type`/`created_at` are read this way, never `intake_data` or findings content.
- **Session requests** (Discovery/Delivery/F2F) — real status per request (`Requested — awaiting scheduling` / `Scheduled` / `Completed` / `Declined`), session-scoped read (this table's RLS is `for all`, not `sent`-gated).
- **Execution Sprint** — same tile the old Dashboard had, kept, now grouped under the shared "Active status" heading instead of standing alone.

**4. Services and support — closes the "clients have no path to the paid modules" gap for real this time**, as its own dedicated page ([src/app/(app)/services/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/services/page.tsx>)), not a section bolted onto the bottom of one report page (the previous batch's fix). Lists every real offer with a DB-backed price ([listPricing()](src/lib/pricing.ts), never a hardcoded literal): Core Audit (free), Concierge tier (honest "no self-serve upgrade exists" note, since no in-app checkout exists anywhere in this codebase), all three standalone modules with real "Request this" links, Execution Sprint (honestly framed as reviewer-scoped, not self-serve, linking to the report to express interest), and all three session types via the existing `SessionRequestButton` — Delivery/F2F correctly gated behind having a real delivered report, same reasoning already established on the report page and evidence-intake page. Added a real nav link (`/services`, [layout.tsx](<src/app/(app)/layout.tsx>)) so it's reachable from anywhere in the authenticated app, not just from Dashboard.

**Shared infrastructure built alongside, avoiding new duplication**: [src/lib/modules/module-meta.ts](src/lib/modules/module-meta.ts) — one source of truth mapping `module_type` (the DB enum) to its display label, its genuinely different-spelled route path (`ai_reliability` → `/ai-reliability-audit`, `data_protection` → `/data-protection-compliance`), and its genuinely different-spelled `pricing.item_key`, consolidating what the reviewer queue page had already been duplicating ad hoc. Extracted `formatPrice()` out of the landing page into [src/lib/pricing.ts](src/lib/pricing.ts) itself, so the new Services page and the landing page can't drift on price-display formatting.

- **Nimbus Ledger Ltd** — ran real synthesis against its actual current report (13 real approved findings) → genuinely zero opportunities (the real "fix identified" case that caught the bug above) — confirmed the honest zero-opportunities message renders instead of "not generated yet." Same account's real `Active status` section correctly showed all 4 of its real module requests (3 `approved`, 1 `pending_review`) with correct per-status labels.
- **Riverbank Analytics Ltd** — real, never-synthesized report correctly showed the honest "Not generated yet" message (proving that branch still works correctly, not just the two "has run" branches). Its real Execution Sprint (`in_progress`, 0 of 3 tasks) and two real session requests (`Requested — awaiting scheduling`, `Scheduled` with a real scheduled date/time from an earlier session's live testing) all rendered correctly in the same `Active status` grid.
- **Services page** — verified live against Fulltest Analytics Ltd's real session: all pricing pulled live from the DB, all three module "Request this" links carry the correct real company ID and land on the correct real intake pages (re-confirmed the exact href pattern already proven in the previous batch).

The real synthesis data generated for Nimbus Ledger Ltd and Fulltest Analytics Ltd during verification was left in the database, not deleted — genuine output of the real production synthesis code run against real approved findings (same retention precedent as every other first-of-its-kind live proof in this file), not fabricated data.
