### Cron gap closed same-day — every real approved-then-delivered report had silently skipped synthesis forever

Direct founder follow-up, prioritized immediately: "this means the headline feature we just built the whole Dashboard redesign around has been silently non-functional for every real report so far." Confirmed and fixed before anything else, per explicit instruction.

**Root cause, confirmed by reading the query directly**: `runPendingAiOpportunitySynthesis()` ([src/lib/synthesis/run-pending-synthesis.ts](src/lib/synthesis/run-pending-synthesis.ts)) only ever matched `status = 'approved'`. A report that reaches `approved` and then progresses to `sent` (`deliverReport()`, a separate step) before this check ever ticks falls **permanently** out of that filter — nothing re-queues it, since `sent` was never a matched status. In production this narrow window is normally closed by the ~20-minute GitHub Actions cron cadence, but in local dev — where the cron never runs automatically at all — every real report this session created after 2026-08-04 progressed straight from `approved` to `sent` with zero chance for the check to ever catch it while `approved`. Confirmed live: queried for `status in ('approved','sent') and ai_opportunity_synthesized_at is null` and found exactly 4 real reports affected (Nimbus Ledger Test Co, Riverbank Analytics Ltd, Real Pass Test Co, Sally) — every one of this session's real accounts except the two (Nimbus Ledger Ltd, Fulltest Analytics Ltd) manually synthesized during the previous pass's live verification.

**Fixed**: widened the query to `.in("status", ["approved", "sent"])`. Synthesis's own inputs (approved/edited findings, the report's own persisted `evidence_sufficiency_by_lens`/`governance_maturity_tier`) are equally valid whether the report is `approved` or already `sent` — nothing about delivery invalidates them. Still fully idempotent via `ai_opportunity_synthesized_at is null`, so a report that already got synthesized while briefly `approved` is correctly skipped, not double-run.

**Backfill run against the real fixed function, not a workaround script** — called the actual exported `runPendingAiOpportunitySynthesis()` directly (not the manual replica used in the previous pass's verification) via a one-off `tsx` runner, deleted after. Processed all 4 real pending reports: Nimbus Ledger Test Co (0 opportunities — another genuine "groundwork first" verdict, not a bug), Riverbank Analytics Ltd (0 opportunities), Real Pass Test Co (0 opportunities), and Sally (2 real opportunities: "Implement AI-powered onboarding process for self-serve micro-businesses," "Develop AI-driven customer segmentation for targeted marketing," both correctly `fix_groundwork_first` given the account's real low data-governance/process-maturity scores). Re-ran immediately after — correctly found 0 reports remaining, confirming idempotency holds through the widened filter too.

**Verified live, not just via a DB read**: signed in as Sally's real account (`sallym.abbas@gmail.com`, the founder's own real testing identity — used the existing `admin.generateLink()` technique, which mints a session token server-side and sends no real email, same as every other live-session verification this session) and loaded the real Dashboard. The AI Opportunity & Readiness section correctly rendered the two real backfilled opportunities with real readiness scores, through the exact same code path a real client hitting this page would use — closing the loop the founder asked for.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean. Temporary debug-session route and scratch runner scripts created and deleted again during this pass, never committed.

## Old-Elvanis-inspired batch, items 3/4/1/5 (confirmed 2026-08-13)

After the old Elvanis app (app.elvanis.com) was reviewed for portable ideas (GitHub clone, `git@github.com:sallyabas/elvanis.git`, since logging into the live app itself would have meant entering a password — the standing credential-handling rule this session's own boundary correctly refused), the founder confirmed 7 items to pull forward, in a specific build order: **3 → 4 → 1 → 5 → 2 → 6 → 7**. Items 3, 4, 1, and 5 are done and live-verified; 2, 6, 7 remain.

**Item 3 — Commercial/Market auto-trigger, closed.** `runCompetitorResearch()` ([src/lib/lenses/commercial-research.ts](src/lib/lenses/commercial-research.ts)) was fully built and tested since 2026-07-31 but never had a real caller — both real audit-execution paths hardcoded `independentResearch: []`. New `runCompetitorResearchSafely()` wraps it in try/catch (research is enrichment, never a hard requirement — a transient Tavily failure must never fail the whole 5-lens audit the way an uncaught throw would), wired into [run-pending-audits.ts](src/lib/audit/run-pending-audits.ts) (the primary audit trigger) and [rerun-audit.ts](src/lib/audit/rerun-audit.ts) (re-run fresh, not from the snapshot — real-time web search, not evidence the client submitted, same "living record" principle already applied to company/goal profile there). **Real, disclosed behavior/cost change**: every real audit now makes 2-5 real Tavily calls, not zero. **Verified live**: a direct call to `runCompetitorResearchSafely()` against real evidence made real Tavily calls and returned real results with real source URLs (Xero pricing-change articles).

**Item 4 — Sprint-completion bridge, closed.** [src/lib/execution-sprint/next-priority.ts](src/lib/execution-sprint/next-priority.ts)'s `findNextPriorityFinding()` — once a client signs off an Execution Sprint, the client page now queries the same report's other reviewer-approved/edited findings, excludes the finding this sprint already addressed and any `isMissingDataFinding` rows (nothing to "implement" when the gap is missing evidence), and reuses `isFixFirstCandidate()`/severity ranking (never a new AI judgment) to surface a real "what's next" prompt linking back to the full report — a genuine, deterministic gap this closes: previously nothing pointed a client back to the roadmap after their one sprint finished. **Verified live** against the real, already-complete Riverbank Analytics Ltd sprint: correctly excluded its own selected finding, correctly filtered the report's missing-evidence Product finding, correctly surfaced the next real candidate by severity.

**Item 1 — Signal cascades, closed.** Ported the old app's `SIGNAL_CASCADES` concept, reusing the recommendation library's existing 16 curated `IssueTypeKey`s as the cascade vocabulary rather than inventing a new taxonomy (confirmed approach) — an honest, accepted limitation: a finding outside those 16 types shows no cascade signal, same as it already shows no recommendation suggestion. Each `RecommendationLibraryEntry` gained a hand-authored `cascadesTo: IssueTypeKey[]` field ([recommendation-library.ts](src/lib/recommendations/recommendation-library.ts), migrated via [20260813090000_recommendation_library_cascades_to.sql](supabase/migrations/20260813090000_recommendation_library_cascades_to.sql) — a new migration, never editing the already-applied 20260806130000 one in place) — cross-lens by design (e.g. `no_financial_visibility → thin_margin, short_runway, customer_concentration, no_operating_reporting`), with a couple of deliberate mutual-reinforcement pairs allowed (`decision_latency ↔ meeting_overload`) rather than forcing a strict acyclic shape, matching the old app's own precedent. New [src/lib/recommendations/cascade.ts](src/lib/recommendations/cascade.ts)'s `computeCascadeSignals()` matches each finding to its best library entry (the same deterministic keyword-matching function already used for recommendation suggestions — never a second, drifting implementation) and counts how many OTHER real findings on the same report match something it cascades to. `isFixFirstCandidate()` ([prioritization.ts](src/lib/reviewer/prioritization.ts)) gained an optional `cascadeCount` parameter (default 0, so every existing single-argument call site is unchanged) — a finding upstream of 2+ other real findings is now flagged fix-first regardless of its own severity, a deliberate, disclosed threshold judgment call, not derived from data. Wired into the Reviewer Workspace's "Suggested fix-first" section ([ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review/[reportId]/ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx>)), showing "upstream of N other findings" with the real downstream titles on hover. **Test-first, 9/9 passing** ([cascade.test-cases.ts](src/lib/recommendations/cascade.test-cases.ts)) — cross-lens cascade correctness, terminal issue types, per-report scoping (not a global count), unmatched findings, self-exclusion, irrelevant-downstream isolation. **Verified live** against a real Fulltest Analytics Ltd report (20 findings): correctly counted a finding as cascading to 3 downstream findings when TWO separate real findings both independently matched the same issue type (`customer_concentration`) plus one matching `short_runway` — proof the counter counts real findings, not just distinct issue types.

**Item 5 — Roadmap cascade reasoning, closed, depends on item 1.** [src/lib/reports/roadmap.ts](src/lib/reports/roadmap.ts)'s `deriveRoadmap()` now also takes the report's full finding set and the recommendation library — a real, disclosed design decision: the 30/60/90 **bucket** a finding lands in still comes from severity alone, unchanged (severity is already "business impact if left unaddressed," a genuinely different concept from cascade count, and a critical finding still needs 30-day action regardless of how many other findings it cascades to) — what cascade reasoning changes is that **within** each bucket, findings upstream of others now sort first and carry a real "fix this first, unlocks N other findings" annotation. Wired into all three callers: [Dashboard](<src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx>) (which needed a new query for the report's full finding set — it previously only ever fetched the top-3 rows by id), the [client Report page](<src/app/(app)/reports/[reportId]/page.tsx>), and [/demo-live](src/app/demo-live/page.tsx) (so the real public demo shows the same real cascade logic, not a simplified stand-in).

**A real, pre-existing bug found and fixed while building item 5, not anticipated upfront**: `LensFinding.findingId` is only re-tagged with the real `lens_findings` DB row id **in-memory**, during the original `run-audit.ts` call (for conflict detection and default top-3 selection) — it is **never written back into the persisted `ai_draft` jsonb column**. Every page that later reads a finding back out of the DB gets the LLM's own stale, draft-scoped `findingId`, not the real row id. This was harmless everywhere it was only ever used as a React list key (collisions are unlikely in practice) — but the new cascade-lookup-by-id in `deriveRoadmap()` needs the REAL id to match against `allReportFindings`, and using the stale `findingId` silently returned `cascadeCount: 0` for every finding, every time (confirmed by first reproducing the bug live, then fixing it). Fixed by introducing `Top3FindingWithId` (`{ id: string; finding: LensFinding }`, the real DB id paired explicitly) as `deriveRoadmap()`'s real input shape instead of trusting `LensFinding.findingId` — all three callers already have the real DB id in scope from their own findings query, so this was a real, surgical, no-schema-change fix. The deeper root cause (persisting the real id back into `ai_draft`, or backfilling historical rows) is a separate, larger, disclosed follow-on — not fixed as a side effect of this roadmap change. **Re-verified live after the fix**, same real Fulltest Analytics Ltd report: the AI & Governance finding (cascading to 2 real present findings) correctly sorted before the Financial margin finding (cascading to 1), both correctly annotated with their real downstream titles — confirming the fix, not just the design, works end-to-end.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, checked after every item. Scratch verification scripts deleted after each use, never committed.

**Item 2 — Goal trend-tracking + structured metric capture, closed.** Two sub-parts, confirmed to build together (same "capture now, feature that reads it can wait for real volume" precedent as `case_library`): structured capture at onboarding, and a trend display reading it.

*Structured capture*: `goals.target_metric_key`/`target_metric_value` (nullable, migration [20260813100000_goals_target_metric_structured.sql](supabase/migrations/20260813100000_goals_target_metric_structured.sql)) — a real (metric key + target number) pairing alongside the existing free-text `target_metric`, not a replacement. New [src/lib/lenses/metric-direction.ts](src/lib/lenses/metric-direction.ts) is a small, curated 13-entry direction lookup (`higher_is_better`/`lower_is_better`), deliberately derived FROM `EVIDENCE_FIELD_SETS` (the existing single source of truth for the 13 known metric keys, itself copied verbatim from `FinancialMetricKey`/`ExecutionMetricKey`/`ProductMetricKey`) rather than a fourth hand-typed copy. Onboarding wizard's "Refine your goal" step gained a metric `Select` (grouped by lens via `optgroup`) + target-value `Input`, both client- and server-validated (a lone key with no value, or vice versa, is rejected).

*Trend display*: [src/lib/goals/metric-trend.ts](src/lib/goals/metric-trend.ts)'s `loadGoalMetricTrend()` reads real numbers straight from `reports.source_evidence_snapshot` (never derived from a finding's prose, never LLM-summarized) across a company's real `sent` reports, returning chronological points for whichever metric the client chose to track. Deliberately the smaller, honest scope confirmed over the heavier version: shows real numeric progression (e.g. "58% → 62%") with a direction cue, never a fabricated achieved/missed verdict against the stated target — that needs real repeat-audit volume to be worth building and stays deferred. Wired into a new "Goal metric trend" section on the [Dashboard](<src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx>), only rendered once both a tracked metric AND at least one real report containing it exist — both honest "nothing to show yet" states, not errors.

**Verified live, both halves, not assumed from code review**: structured capture was driven through a real disposable browser session, the full 5-step wizard, submission, and a direct DB read confirming `target_metric_key: "gross_margin_percent"`, `target_metric_value: 75` persisted exactly as entered. One real, self-inflicted test-harness snag along the way, not an app bug: navigating directly to `/onboarding` (bypassing `(app)/layout.tsx`) skipped `ensureClientUserRow()`, producing a real `companies_user_id_fkey` violation — confirmed this is impossible in the actual product flow (client-login's own `next=/business-profile` redirect target is inside `(app)`, so `ensureClientUserRow()` always runs first for a real signup) by re-driving the same session through `/business-profile` first, which correctly bootstrapped the row and redirected to `/onboarding` exactly as designed; the wizard then submitted cleanly. Trend-display logic was verified two ways: the real single-report-with-metric case was confirmed directly against real DB data (Real Pass Test Co, `gross_margin_percent: 62`, correctly returned as a single point with no fabricated trend claim); no real company yet has 2+ real `sent` reports both containing numeric metrics (the feature only launched 2026-08-07, after most real test accounts' reports already existed) — confirmed honestly by querying live data rather than assumed — so the 2+-point chronological-ordering path was verified structurally against a fake Supabase client instead (same "synthetic data for structural proof, real data for the real case" precedent already used for `case_library`/similar-patterns). Disposable test account, company, and goal deleted afterward.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, checked after every item. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route created and deleted once during this pass, never committed.

**Item 6 — Surface real strengths, per-lens chart, closed.** The client report page (and [/demo-live](src/app/demo-live/page.tsx), for consistency with the real product surface) was previously entirely deficit-framed — every finding, healthy or not, rendered in the same "here's what's wrong" visual language. Reuses `goalRelevance === "directly_supports"` as the real, deterministic strength signal (already the exact "genuinely healthy and directly relevant to the goal" value every lens's prompt already produces — see its own extensive docblock in `lenses/types.ts`) rather than inventing a new judgment.

A new "Strengths by lens" section, per-lens horizontal bar (pure CSS, no chart library — green strength segment vs. neutral "to address" segment, proportional width) plus a real "What's working" list of the actual strength finding titles. `isMissingDataFinding` rows are excluded from BOTH counts — an evidence gap is neither a strength nor a weakness, a third category this report already visually distinguishes elsewhere (the dashed "NO EVIDENCE SUBMITTED" card) — confirmed correct in the live proof below (Product/Customer showed "0 strengths · 0 to address" for a report whose only Product finding was the missing-evidence placeholder, not miscounted as a weakness). AI & Governance's own prompt rule 4 forbids ever producing a "things are healthy" finding by design, so it will structurally show 0 strengths here — accepted as correct, not a bug, per explicit confirmation before building.

**Verified live against real DB data** (Riverbank Analytics Ltd, the same account `/demo-live` serves): the section correctly rendered "Financial — 1 strength · 2 to address" and "Product / Customer — 0 strengths · 0 to address," with "What's working" correctly listing only "Cash Flow Runway" (the one real `directly_supports` finding). Confirmed the visual bar itself, not just the surrounding text, since the browser tool's screenshot capability was flaky this pass (same known desync documented elsewhere in this file): `getComputedStyle()` on the real rendered DOM confirmed exactly one green bar segment at `33.3333%` width (1 of 3 real Financial findings) with a genuine green background color — the proportional math and the color both hold, not just the labels around them.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean.

**Item 7 — Digital Presence check (signed-in client scope), closed. The 7-item batch is now complete.** Deliberately narrower than the original spec's still-deferred "Digital Presence Scan" feature (unauthenticated public entry point, B2B/B2C-aware scoring, real ToS scraping concerns — none of which this needed to solve): a real-time, plain-fetch technical-SEO check on the client's own already-registered `website_url`, plus a presence check (existence/linkage only, never content scraping) of known social/review channels linked from the homepage. Never persisted — a live, on-demand check, not a new feature layered onto the still-unused `digital_presence_scans` table (which belongs to the bigger, genuinely different deferred feature).

[src/lib/digital-presence/check.ts](src/lib/digital-presence/check.ts) — `runDigitalPresenceCheck()`: 8 technical checks (HTTPS, title, meta description, viewport, canonical, H1, robots.txt/sitemap.xml reachability) via lightweight regex against the raw HTML response (no DOM/headless browser — the disclosed limitation confirmed before building, surfaced directly in the UI copy: a JS-rendered SPA with no server-rendered `<head>` will show false negatives here). Social-channel detection matches homepage `href`s against 8 known domains (LinkedIn/X/Facebook/Instagram/Trustpilot/G2/Capterra/Glassdoor), then issues a bounded HEAD-with-GET-fallback reachability check against each match — never fetches or reads the target page's actual content, matching the confirmed "existence/linkage only" scope. A real, disclosed SSRF guard (`isBlockedHost()`) rejects localhost/private-IP-range literals before ever issuing a request, since this fetches a URL the client themselves supplied via their own profile — explicitly flagged as a partial mitigation (doesn't cover DNS rebinding, which `fetch()` gives no hook to guard against without a custom agent), not silently assumed complete. Wired into a new "Digital presence check" card on [Business Profile](<src/app/(app)/business-profile/page.tsx>) ([DigitalPresenceCheck.tsx](<src/app/(app)/business-profile/DigitalPresenceCheck.tsx>), a new `runDigitalPresenceCheckAction()` in that page's `actions.ts`) — the natural home, since `website_url` already lives there as part of the living company record.

**Verified live and comprehensively, both the backend function directly and the real UI click-through**: `runDigitalPresenceCheck()` tested against real sites — Stripe (correct HTTPS/title/meta/viewport/canonical/H1/robots.txt, correctly `sitemapXmlReachable: false`, zero social links found — an honest result for a page whose footer nav is JS-rendered, not a bug), Y Combinator (all 8 technical checks correct, 4 real social channels found with real correct URLs, 3 correctly reachable and LinkedIn correctly flagged unreachable — a real, honest result consistent with LinkedIn's own known bot-blocking behavior, not a false negative), and Basecamp (all correct, zero social links — same honest JS-rendered-nav case as Stripe). SSRF guard confirmed against `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, and the AWS metadata endpoint `169.254.169.254` — all three correctly rejected before any request was issued. Invalid-URL and nonexistent-domain error paths both confirmed. **Then verified the real UI end-to-end**, not just the backend function: a disposable test account with a real company (`website_url: https://www.ycombinator.com`) was driven through a real browser session, clicked the actual "Run digital presence check" button, and the rendered results matched the direct backend call exactly — every checkmark, the real page title, and all 4 real social channels with correct reachability. Disposable test account, company, and goal deleted afterward; temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route created and deleted twice across the two live-verification passes this batch, never committed.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean.

**All 7 items of the old-Elvanis-inspired batch are now built, live-verified, and committed/pushed to `main`** (commit `742ab2d`), in the confirmed order (3 → 4 → 1 → 5 → 2 → 6 → 7), per explicit go-ahead 2026-08-14.

## Three real bugs from live testing of Tender Readiness, checked and fixed in all three modules (confirmed 2026-08-15)

**1. "Stuck on loading" — root-caused by live reproduction, not guessed.** Filled and submitted the real intake form against a real jurisdiction-applicable company (Nimbus Ledger Ltd) and watched server logs directly: the completion state itself was always correct (`status === "done"` renders correctly, confirmed via a clean run — 3.3s total, 2.58s inside the real Groq call). The actual gap: `submitTenderReadinessAudit()` runs a real, synchronous Groq call in the same request with **zero loading feedback beyond a button-text change to "Submitting…"** — the exact same class of bug already found and fixed for Evidence Intake ("no prior feedback beyond a disabled button — easy to miss entirely, reading as a frozen page"), and this codebase has extensively documented real Groq rate-limit/slowness events elsewhere that could stretch this same call to tens of seconds, long enough for an unadorned button to read as genuinely frozen. Fixed the same way Evidence Intake was: a real full-screen overlay ("Analyzing your submission…", spinner, "please don't close this tab") shown for the duration of the real call.

**2 & 3. Document field labels said "optional" in a way that read as "uploading is optional" rather than the real meaning ("you may not have this, and that's fine"), and didn't clearly connect the review textarea to the upload above it.** Fixed in [TenderReadinessIntakeForm.tsx](<src/app/(modules)/tender-readiness/TenderReadinessIntakeForm.tsx>): the upload label dropped "(optional)" entirely, and the textarea below was relabeled "Documentation text — from your upload above, or typed directly" so it's unambiguous the two fields are connected. A real confirm-before-submit gate was added — same "missing evidence is itself a finding" principle already used in Financial: if `existingDocumentation` is blank at submit time, the button no longer submits directly; it shows "Confirming: you don't have existing documentation for this?" with an explicit "Yes, continue without documentation" / "Go back and add it" choice, so a blank field never silently passes through unconfirmed. The backend's existing deterministic "no documentation" guaranteed-finding logic was already correct — this is a front-end honesty fix, not a backend behavior change.

**Checked against AI Reliability Audit and Data Protection Compliance, per explicit instruction, not assumed clean:**
- **AI Reliability Audit** ([AiReliabilityIntakeForm.tsx](<src/app/(modules)/ai-reliability-audit/AiReliabilityIntakeForm.tsx>)) — same architecture, same issue 1 (fixed with the identical overlay pattern, module-appropriate copy). This module has no document-upload field at all, so issues 2 and 3 genuinely don't apply here — confirmed by reading the file, not assumed.
- **Data Protection Compliance** ([DataProtectionIntakeForm.tsx](<src/app/(modules)/data-protection-compliance/DataProtectionIntakeForm.tsx>)) — issue 1 applied and was fixed the same way. Issues 2/3 applied differently given this module's real, deliberate design difference from Tender Readiness (one shared document upload covering up to five categories, per explicit founder direction, with no free-text-only path — a client can only get text into the field by uploading first): the upload label already said "Upload…" clearly (item 2 was already satisfied), so only "optional" needed removing, and the confirm gate was added scoped specifically to the shared document field — not to the five category fields below it, which already carry their own "blank is meaningful too" framing message and would make a second confirm gate per category redundant, unrequested, and worse UX on a 5-field form.

**Verified live end-to-end for all three modules, not just code-reviewed**: Tender Readiness — confirmed the confirm gate correctly withholds the network call (checked server logs showed no new POST until explicit confirmation), confirmed the overlay renders immediately on click (caught synchronously in the same script execution, before the click handler's `await` could resolve — a real, direct proof rather than inferring it from timing), confirmed the submission completes correctly afterward, and confirmed via a direct DB read that the confirm-gated submission persisted `existingDocumentation` exactly as typed. AI Reliability Audit — confirmed the overlay renders and the submission completes correctly against a real conversational-mode submission. Data Protection Compliance — confirmed the "optional" wording is gone, the confirm gate fires and correctly withholds submission, the overlay renders on confirmation, the submission completes correctly, and a direct DB read confirmed `existingDocumentationText: null` while the one filled category field (`consentFlow`) persisted correctly — proving the confirm-gate scoping (document field only, not the five categories) works exactly as designed.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout. Disposable module requests created during this verification pass were left in the database (real proof of the fix working, same retention precedent as every other first-of-its-kind live verification in this file) — no synthetic test accounts or scratch files were created this pass; all testing ran against real, already-existing companies (Nimbus Ledger Ltd, Doc Upload Test Co).

## The loading overlay above didn't fully fix the "stuck" bug — the real root cause, found in production, 2026-08-15

The founder reported the exact symptom the previous fix was supposed to close still happening on the live Vercel deployment, with a real console error attached: `POST /tender-readiness?companyId=... 500 (Internal Server Error)`, followed by `Uncaught (in promise) Error: An error occurred in the Server Components render`. Investigated directly rather than assumed fixed by the earlier pass.

**Root cause, confirmed by reading the actual code path, not guessed**: the earlier loading-overlay fix addressed the WRONG half of the problem. It correctly gave the client visual feedback while a submission is in flight, but every one of `doSubmit()`/`handleSubmit()` in all three module intake forms — and, found while investigating the same class of bug, `handleConfirmSubmit()`/`handleConfirmSubmitNow()` in Evidence Intake — called its Server Action with a bare `await`, no `try/catch`. `submitTenderReadinessAudit()`'s own try/catch only covers errors it can construct a real `{success: false, error}` response FOR — it cannot catch a genuine RPC-level failure, where the underlying fetch itself rejects instead of resolving to any value at all. A raw 500 from the platform (most plausibly Vercel's default serverless function execution timeout killing the request mid-flight during a slow or rate-limited Groq call — this codebase has extensively and repeatedly documented real Groq slowness/rate-limit events elsewhere) is exactly that: a rejected promise the client-side code was never listening for. With no `try/catch`, that rejection propagated as an uncaught exception, `setStatus("error")` never ran, and the loading overlay — now correctly rendering — span forever with no way out. The earlier fix wasn't wrong, it was incomplete: it fixed "no feedback while waiting" without fixing "no recovery if the wait never ends."

**Two real fixes, not one, addressing both halves**:
1. **Client-side guarantee, all four affected forms**: every `await submit*(...)` call is now wrapped in a real `try/catch` — [TenderReadinessIntakeForm.tsx](<src/app/(modules)/tender-readiness/TenderReadinessIntakeForm.tsx>), [AiReliabilityIntakeForm.tsx](<src/app/(modules)/ai-reliability-audit/AiReliabilityIntakeForm.tsx>), [DataProtectionIntakeForm.tsx](<src/app/(modules)/data-protection-compliance/DataProtectionIntakeForm.tsx>), and — found during this same investigation, not requested but the identical class of gap — [EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx](<src/app/(app)/evidence-intake/EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx>)'s `handleConfirmSubmit()` and `handleConfirmSubmitNow()` (the latter is the single highest-risk site in the whole app for this exact failure mode: a genuinely synchronous five-lens Groq call, the longest real synchronous AI call left anywhere in this codebase). Any genuine RPC-level failure now correctly transitions to a real, visible error state ("Something went wrong reaching the server — please try again.") with the submit button re-enabled, instead of leaving the client staring at a spinner with no way to know anything went wrong or to retry.
2. **Root-cause mitigation, not just the symptom**: none of the four affected routes had `maxDuration` configured, so each fell back to Vercel's default serverless function timeout — genuinely too short for a real-world Groq call under rate-limiting or retries. `export const maxDuration = 60;` added to all four page files ([tender-readiness/page.tsx](<src/app/(modules)/tender-readiness/page.tsx>), [ai-reliability-audit/page.tsx](<src/app/(modules)/ai-reliability-audit/page.tsx>), [data-protection-compliance/page.tsx](<src/app/(modules)/data-protection-compliance/page.tsx>), [evidence-intake/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/evidence-intake/page.tsx>)) — the practical ceiling for this, not a guarantee it eliminates every possible timeout on every plan, disclosed as such rather than overclaimed.

**Verified live, not just reasoned about** — the actual production failure (a genuinely rejected fetch) can't be manufactured from local dev, so the exact failure mode was reproduced directly instead of assumed fixed: `window.fetch` was monkey-patched in a real browser session to reject any request to `/tender-readiness` with a `TypeError`, simulating precisely what a killed serverless function or dropped connection looks like from the client's perspective. Confirmed the loading overlay appeared immediately on submit (as designed), then — after the patched rejection — confirmed it correctly disappeared and the real error message rendered with the submit button re-enabled, not stuck. `fetch` was then restored to its real implementation and a genuine submission was run through the same session to confirm the fix didn't break the working path — it completed and rendered "Submitted for review" exactly as before.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout. No scratch files or test accounts left behind — this pass used a real, already-existing company (Nimbus Ledger Ltd) and a real disposable module request it created, left in the database per the same retention precedent as every other first-of-its-kind live verification in this file.

Committed and pushed (commit `49fa9c0`), per explicit go-ahead.

## A second, real, distinct production 500 — document upload itself, confirmed still failing after the fix above (2026-08-15)

The founder reported the exact same symptom (raw 500, digest-only error) again after the fix above deployed — but this time triggered specifically by uploading a document, not by the final submit. Investigated as a genuinely separate bug rather than assumed to be the same one recurring: the submit-handler fix above closes a client-side gap in the SUBMIT path; document upload goes through an entirely different Server Action, `extractDocumentTextAction()`, which had never been touched by that fix.

**Root cause, the strongest available hypothesis — disclosed as a hypothesis, not a confirmed fix**: `extractDocumentTextAction()` had no `try/catch` around its call to `extractTextFromDocument()`, the same class of gap just fixed in the submit handlers, and got the same defensive fix. But there's a real, distinct SECOND candidate specific to document upload: `extractTextFromDocument()`'s own docblock already documents a genuine Turbopack-specific bug fixed earlier this session (`serverExternalPackages: ["pdf-parse", "pdfjs-dist"]` in `next.config.ts`) — pdf-parse loads its text-extraction worker via a fully-dynamic `import(this.workerSrc)` call from WITHIN its own code, not a static import this app's code controls. That earlier fix was verified against local `next dev` and local `npm run build` — but neither of those exercises Vercel's own deployment-time file tracing, which determines which files actually ship inside the deployed serverless function. A fully-dynamic import with a runtime-computed path is exactly the kind of import a static tracer can miss, meaning the worker file that works locally may genuinely not be present in the deployed function's filesystem — a failure that can ONLY manifest on the real platform, never locally, which is consistent with why this specific fix couldn't be confirmed in dev alone.

**Two fixes applied, one guaranteed, one a disclosed best-effort hypothesis**:
1. **Guaranteed fix**: [extractDocumentTextAction()](src/lib/documents/actions.ts) now wraps `extractTextFromDocument()` in a real `try/catch` — the exact same defensive guarantee just applied to every submit handler. Whatever the underlying cause, the client now gets an honest, actionable error message ("Couldn't process this document...") instead of a raw platform crash. This closes the actual reported symptom regardless of whether the hypothesis below is correct.
2. **Disclosed best-effort mitigation**: [next.config.ts](next.config.ts) now sets `outputFileTracingIncludes` — the real, documented Next.js mechanism for forcing a file a tracer missed into a specific route's deployed bundle — for the three routes that use document upload (Tender Readiness, Data Protection Compliance, and Evidence Intake, which carries AI & Governance's document-review mode), globbing in `pdf-parse`'s and `pdfjs-dist`'s `.mjs` files. **This is explicitly the strongest available hypothesis, not a confirmed fix** — it cannot be verified from local dev or a local build, only from an actual Vercel deployment, and this session has no access to Vercel's function logs to see the real underlying error (the browser only ever shows a digest-only, detail-omitted production error).

**What was and wasn't verified, disclosed honestly rather than overclaimed**: confirmed real PDF text extraction still succeeds correctly after the `try/catch` change (a hand-constructed real PDF with genuine text content, extracted successfully via a direct call to `extractTextFromDocument()`) — proving the fix introduces no regression to the working local path. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean, confirming the new config doesn't break the build. **What could NOT be verified**: whether `outputFileTracingIncludes` actually resolves the real Vercel-specific failure — that needs either a real redeploy-and-retest, or the actual Vercel function log detail (the "digest" identifier, or the full server-side error visible in the Vercel dashboard's Functions/Logs tab, which the browser console deliberately omits in production). If document upload still fails after this deploys, the `try/catch` fix guarantees the client will at least see a real, specific error message instead of a raw crash — and that message should be the next real clue to the underlying cause, worth reporting back verbatim.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout. No scratch files left behind.

## Third attempt superseded the first two — pdf-parse removed entirely, replaced with unpdf (confirmed 2026-08-15)

The founder reported the exact same symptom (raw 500, digest-only "Server Components render" error) a THIRD time, on the same document-upload path, after the `outputFileTracingIncludes` fix above had already deployed — meaning that fix, disclosed at the time as "the strongest available hypothesis, not a confirmed fix," did not resolve it. Investigated properly this time rather than attempting a fourth variation on the same Vercel-config guess.

**Real, additional gap found and closed first**: [DocumentUploadField.tsx](src/app/_components/ui/DocumentUploadField.tsx)'s own `handleFileChange()` — the actual client-side caller of `extractDocumentTextAction()` — had never been given the try/catch fix applied to every submit handler earlier the same day. This is a real, independent gap (a genuinely uncaught client-side rejection would itself explain a stuck/crashed UI regardless of the server-side cause) and was fixed the same way as everywhere else, but on its own it would not explain a server-side 500 with a Next.js-generated digest, which is what was actually reported.

**Real root cause, this time confirmed via credible, specific, published external sources rather than reasoned from first principles a third time**: searched directly for this exact failure class instead of continuing to iterate on file-tracing config. Found it is a well-documented, recurring problem: pdf-parse is built on pdfjs-dist, which has an OPTIONAL native dependency on `canvas` — a module requiring Python, node-gyp, and a C++ toolchain to compile, none of which exist in Vercel's serverless build/runtime environment. This is unrelated to the dynamic-worker-import theory the previous two fixes were built around (a real, correctly-diagnosed, but different problem — the Turbopack dev-mode bug it explains was real and stays fixed) — this is a native-compilation gap, not a file-tracing gap, and no amount of `outputFileTracingIncludes` tuning could ever resolve it, which is exactly why that fix didn't work. Sources: [pdf-parse vs. unpdf on Vercel](https://dev.to/chudi_nnorukam/serverless-pdf-processing-why-unpdf-beats-pdf-parse-2jji), [unpdf documentation](https://unjs.io/packages/unpdf/).

**Fixed by removing the dependency, not configuring around it**: `pdf-parse` uninstalled from `package.json` entirely (pulling `pdfjs-dist`/`canvas` out as transitive dependencies along with it). [extract-text.ts](src/lib/documents/extract-text.ts)'s PDF path rewritten to use [unpdf](https://github.com/unjs/unpdf) (`getDocumentProxy()` + `extractText(pdf, { mergePages: true })`) — a purpose-built alternative that ships its own zero-native-dependency, serverless-compiled build of PDF.js by default, specifically built to "work on Vercel out of the box" with no bundler configuration required. [next.config.ts](next.config.ts)'s now-inapplicable `serverExternalPackages`/`outputFileTracingIncludes` entries for pdf-parse/pdfjs-dist were removed, since nothing in this codebase imports either anymore — both prior fix attempts' docblocks were kept as an honest record of what was tried and why it didn't fully resolve the issue, rather than silently deleted. `mammoth` (DOCX) is untouched — a much simpler pure-JS library with no worker/native-compilation mechanism, and no evidence anywhere of an equivalent Vercel-specific problem.

**Verified, same discipline as both prior attempts — what WAS and WASN'T confirmed, stated plainly**: a hand-constructed real PDF with genuine text content, a genuinely corrupted PDF, and a `.doc` file were all run directly through `extractTextFromDocument()` — all three produced the exact same correct results as before the swap (successful extraction, the specific "Invalid PDF structure" error, and the specific .doc-rejection message respectively), confirming no regression in the working paths or the error paths. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean, including a full production build with Turbopack (the exact build mode Vercel uses) completing with no pdf-parse/pdfjs-dist errors anywhere. **What still cannot be verified from this environment, disclosed honestly rather than promised as fixed**: whether this fully resolves the live Vercel 500 — that requires an actual deploy-and-retest, which this session has no way to trigger or observe directly. The material difference from the two prior attempts is that this fix removes a dependency with a specific, credible, externally-documented history of exactly this failure class on this exact platform, rather than adjusting configuration around a dependency that keeps failing in ways this session can't fully reproduce or observe. If the live Vercel error persists after this deploys, the next actionable step is getting the real server-side error detail from Vercel's dashboard (Deployments → the failing deployment → Functions/Runtime Logs, searchable by the digest ID already visible in the browser console) — that's the one piece of ground truth this environment cannot obtain on its own, and continuing to guess a fourth time without it would not be a good use of anyone's time.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout. No scratch files left behind.

**Not yet committed/pushed** — held for explicit go-ahead, same standing pattern as every other change this session.

## Six real gaps in the module intake/service flow, checked and fixed (confirmed 2026-08-15)

Direct founder bug report, six items, worked through individually with a real root-cause check before fixing item 6 (a genuine "is this a bug or correct behavior" question the founder explicitly asked to be answered before touching anything).

**1. All three module intake pages missing the site nav, fixed by moving them into `(app)` — and a real, latent security gap closed in the same pass.** Root cause: Tender Readiness, AI Reliability Audit, and Data Protection Compliance's standalone entry pages had lived outside `(app)` since first built, trusting an unauthenticated `?companyId=` query param with zero session check of their own — the same class of gap already found and fixed for Evidence Intake on 2026-08-12, but never swept to these three. Confirmed real client auth has existed app-wide since 2026-08-03, and both real call sites into these pages ([reports/[reportId]/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/reports/[reportId]/page.tsx>), [services/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/services/page.tsx>)) already live inside `(app)` and already pass the session's own company id — confirming the move was safe before making it, not assumed. All three moved via `git mv` into `(app)`, each page rewritten to derive its company from the session (`(app)/layout.tsx`'s own auth+company gate, then a session-scoped `companies` query) instead of trusting the query param — closing a real, previously-latent gap where any signed-in client could have submitted a module request against ANY company's id by editing the URL, not just adding a nav bar around the old behavior. The now-redundant `?companyId=` was dropped from both real link sites.

**2. Module requests fired zero notification anywhere in their lifecycle — confirmed, then fixed on both ends.** Grepped every module `persist.ts` and `module-workspace.ts` directly: no notification call existed at submission (unlike a core-audit report's `new_submission`) or at delivery (unlike `report_ready`) — "Submitted for review" was purely an on-screen message. Two new `notification_event_type` values added ([migration](supabase/migrations/20260815104610_notification_event_type_module_lifecycle.sql)): `module_new_submission` (reviewer-facing, fired on real submission via a new [notifyReviewersOfNewModuleRequest()](src/lib/reviewer/notifications.ts), wired into all three modules' `persist.ts`) and `module_ready` (client-facing, fired by [deliverModuleRequest()](src/lib/reviewer/module-workspace.ts) mirroring `deliverReport()`'s exact pattern — recipient resolved via `companies.user_id`, links to `/reports` since no client-facing module detail view exists yet). Real templates added to [dispatch.ts](src/lib/notifications/dispatch.ts). **Verified live end-to-end, not just built**: a real Tender Readiness submission correctly created exactly 3 `module_new_submission` rows (one per reviewer on file); running the full accept → approve → deliver cycle against that same real request correctly created exactly 1 `module_ready` row targeted at the right client.

**3, 4, 5 — shared [ModuleSubmittedNotice](src/app/_components/ModuleSubmittedNotice.tsx) component, used by all three intake forms' "done" state.** Closes three real gaps together: no back/next-step link (now links to `/services`), no time estimate (reuses the existing `review_period_hours` app_setting — the same reviewers, a similar process — framed honestly as "typically," not a guaranteed deadline, since modules have no enforced SLA equivalent to `reports.review_due_at`), and no visible contact option (`mailto:info@app.elvanis.com`, the address already verified and sending real transactional email in this codebase — flagged as a default, not assumed to be the intended inbox for this specifically). **A real bug caught live during this exact verification, not anticipated**: the SLA sentence rendered "within 48hours" with no space — the identical JSX whitespace-collapse gotcha already documented and fixed elsewhere in this file (a text node's leading space after a `{expression}` is silently dropped when that node spans multiple SOURCE lines). Fixed the same way, with an explicit `{" "}`, and re-verified live afterward.

**6. Checked before fixing anything, per explicit instruction — both of the founder's proposed explanations turned out partially true, in a more specific way than either alone.** Confirmed directly against real data (`request 6f1067f9-...`, company "Sally"): `registration_country`/`uae_free_zone`/`customer_market_countries` were genuinely all null/empty, `computeJurisdictionApplicability()` correctly computed zero applicable jurisdictions from that, and `runTenderReadinessAudit()` correctly short-circuited to zero findings — the deterministic pipeline was never broken, exactly the founder's first hypothesis. But investigating WHY the data was empty surfaced the real, deeper gap the founder's second hypothesis pointed at: grepping the entire `src/app` tree found these three fields were read by Tender Readiness/Data Protection Compliance's jurisdiction logic and displayed read-only on the reviewer's own company-detail page — but had **never been settable anywhere in the client-facing app**, confirmed by reading [BusinessProfileForm.tsx](<src/app/(app)/business-profile/BusinessProfileForm.tsx>) directly (the "full field set" built 2026-08-04 never included them). Not a considered "we operate nowhere regulated" answer from the client — a missing feature that made the honest zero-jurisdiction outcome the ONLY possible outcome for every real client.

Both halves fixed, not just the display of the gap:
- **Real capture UI added to Business Profile** — a `Registration country` dropdown (United Kingdom, all 27 EU member states, Saudi Arabia, United Arab Emirates, plus a real free-text "Other" fallback) built from the exact canonical names `src/lib/modules/shared/regions.ts`'s normalize()-based matching recognizes, same "dropdown prevents ambiguity, ≠ claiming this app understands every country" reasoning already used for revenue-range bands; a conditional `UAE free zone` select shown only when UAE is selected; and a `Customer market countries` `TagInput`, matching the existing social-links/tools-stack pattern. Wired through `CompanyProfileFields`/`updateCompanyProfile()`/`company_profile_history` tracking, same discipline as every other profile field.
- **Reviewer-side display fixed** — [review-module/[requestId]/page.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review-module/[requestId]/page.tsx>) now computes `hasNoApplicableJurisdiction` server-side from the request's own already-persisted `intake_data.applicability` (frozen at submission time — an old request correctly keeps showing its own historical reasoning even after a later profile edit, confirmed live). [ModuleReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review-module/[requestId]/ModuleReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx>) replaces the bare "No findings." with a real explanation in that case, and replaces the procurement-answers section's dead-end "Generate" button (which could only ever throw the exact same "No applicable regulations" error every time) with an honest info message instead.
- **Client-side note added** to both Tender Readiness's and Data Protection Compliance's own applicability box, shown only when nothing currently applies: a plain-language explanation plus a real link to Business Profile, framed as "you can still submit, but this likely won't surface jurisdiction-specific findings until these are set" — not a hard block, since a client might genuinely operate nowhere regulated.

**Verified live end-to-end, real session, not assumed from code review** (Sally's real account, `sallym.abbas@gmail.com`): confirmed the real charcoal nav renders on `/tender-readiness` with no `?companyId=` in the URL; confirmed the pre-fix zero-jurisdiction note and Business Profile link rendered correctly; saved real `registrationCountry: "Germany"` / `customerMarketCountries: ["France"]` through the real form (via direct DOM event dispatch, the same technique already established in this codebase for when the higher-level browser-automation tool is flaky — confirmed via a direct DB read, including two new `company_profile_history` rows) and confirmed, on reload, both `/tender-readiness` and `/data-protection-compliance` correctly flipped to showing "EU AI Act" / "EU GDPR" — proving the full chain (Business Profile capture → persistence → deterministic jurisdiction computation → client-facing display) works, not just the isolated pieces. Submitted a real Tender Readiness request against this now-real jurisdiction data — got 2 real findings (the deterministic no-documentation finding plus a genuine LLM-classified "EU AI Act Risk Classification" finding), confirming the fix doesn't just clear the empty-state case but produces real, correct output once the data exists. Ran the full accept → approve → deliver cycle against this real request (confirming item 2's notifications, above) and separately confirmed, as the real reviewer account, that the ORIGINAL zero-jurisdiction request still correctly shows the new explanatory message and the fixed procurement-answers section.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, re-checked after the live-caught whitespace fix. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch verification scripts created during this pass deleted afterward, never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup. The real Tender Readiness request submitted and delivered during verification (`0af9fe11-...`, Sally's account) was left in the database as real proof, same retention precedent as every other first-of-its-kind live verification in this file.

## Seven real copy/functionality issues from live testing on Tender Readiness, checked and fixed (confirmed 2026-08-15)

Direct founder bug report from real live testing (a genuine submission, `852c2e4e-...`, through the actual UI), worked through individually.

**1 & 2 — applicability box copy and hierarchy, fixed together with a new shared component.** "Applicable jurisdictions (determined automatically, not something you select)" was internal engineering framing shown directly to the client; and the regulation name ("EU AI Act (4-tier risk classification)") was reading as the page's own headline rather than supporting detail within the real service identity (the page's own `<h1>Tender Readiness</h1>`). Fixed together via a new [ApplicableRegulationsBox](src/app/_components/ApplicableRegulationsBox.tsx), used by both Tender Readiness and Data Protection Compliance (same problem class existed on both, not just the one page reported — same "audit every module page" precedent as prior rounds): the heading is now real client-facing copy ("Which regulations apply to you, based on your Business Profile"), rendered as a small uppercase eyebrow label rather than a competing `<h2>`; each regulation renders at normal body weight with its technical descriptor moved into small muted supporting text instead of baked into one long parenthetical string. **Verified live via screenshot**, not just text extraction — "Tender Readiness" is unambiguously the largest, boldest text on the page; "EU AI Act — 4-tier risk classification" reads clearly as small, secondary detail beneath it. Confirmed the same fix on Data Protection Compliance too.

**3. Raw request UUID shown to the client — real bug, fixed.** "Request ID: 852c2e4e-..." was displayed directly in [ModuleSubmittedNotice](src/app/_components/ModuleSubmittedNotice.tsx) — the same class of internal-identifier leak already fixed once for conflict descriptions in the Reviewer Workspace. Removed entirely (the `requestId` prop and all its now-unused tracking state were removed from all three intake forms too, not just hidden from display, closing the dead-code trail cleanly). Verified via source read and a clean build — a deliberately lighter-weight verification for this specific fix (a trivial prop/text removal, not new logic) to conserve Groq quota rather than re-running a full live submission a third time this session.

**4. No client-facing explanation of "Procurement answers" — closed as part of building item 6.** The reviewer-facing explanation was already clear; nothing client-facing existed at all, confirmed by grep before writing anything. Real copy added to the new client detail page (item 6): "Draft answers to common AI-related procurement and tender questions, grounded in your reviewed findings above — ready to adapt and share with whoever's asking you to prove compliance." **Verified live** rendering correctly against the real, just-delivered request.

**5. Confirmed against the exact real request, not assumed.** Queried the DB directly for `852c2e4e-...`: the reviewer-facing `module_new_submission` notification fired correctly at real submission time (3 rows, one per reviewer, timestamped seconds after the request was created). No "approval"-stage email exists anywhere in this codebase, for either the core audit or any module — by design, mirroring how `approveReport()`/`approveModuleRequest()` have never notified the client, only delivery does (`report_ready`/`module_ready`). The request hadn't been delivered yet at the time of the report, so `module_ready` legitimately hadn't fired — not a bug. Delivered it as part of this pass (also needed to build/verify item 6 against something real) and confirmed `module_ready` fired correctly the instant it was.

**6. Real client-facing module detail view built, closing a gap flagged repeatedly since 2026-08-04 and never actually closed.** New page, [services/module/[requestId]](<src/app/(app)/services/module/[requestId]/page.tsx>) — deliberately session-scoped throughout, not the admin client: `module_requests`/`module_findings`/`procurement_answers`' own RLS policies (`20260806090000_module_requests_rls_fix.sql`) already correctly restrict the owning client to `status = 'sent'` rows, so a plain session-scoped query is both the simplest and the most secure implementation — a request that isn't this client's own, or isn't delivered yet, just returns zero rows, not a bypassable check this page has to get right itself. Findings/answers are still filtered to `approved`/`edited` in the application query, since RLS only gates on the PARENT request's status, not each individual finding's own `reviewer_status` — a rejected finding on an otherwise-`sent` request would otherwise leak through. Real findings render with the same severity-badge/missing-evidence-badge treatment already used on the core-audit client Report page (extracted, not reinvented). [Reports & History](<src/app/(app)/reports/page.tsx>) now links to this real page instead of "Detail view coming soon." The evidence-pack download route ([api/tender-readiness/[requestId]/evidence-pack](<src/app/api/tender-readiness/[requestId]/evidence-pack/route.ts>)) was extended to also allow the owning client to fetch their own delivered pack (previously reviewer-only) — the function itself already filters to approved/edited content internally, confirmed by reading it directly rather than assumed, so exposing it to the client is safe content-wise; the route adds a real second authorization path (reviewer OR owning-client-of-a-sent-request) rather than loosening the existing reviewer check. **Verified live end-to-end against the real, just-delivered request**: navigated to the real URL as Sally's real session, confirmed 5 real findings and 11 real procurement answers rendered correctly with the new client-facing explanation; confirmed the evidence-pack download now returns real markdown content (200, `text/markdown`) through the client's own session, not just the reviewer's.

**7. Real, structural Dashboard bug found and fixed — module/session/sprint status tiles were wrongly gated behind having a delivered CORE report.** The entire "Active status" section (module requests, session requests, the Execution Sprint tile) was nested inside `{latestReport && (...)}` — meaning any company with real active module requests but no delivered core-audit report yet would never see them on Dashboard at all, a structural gate on the wrong condition, not specific to any one request (Sally's own account happened to have a core report, so this wasn't visible in her case directly, but the bug is real and general). Moved the section out to its own block, gated only on `hasAnyStatusTiles`, rendering regardless of whether a core report exists. Fixed two more real, smaller bugs found in the same block while moving it: the module tile's "View" link pointed back at the module's own INTAKE page (with a now-unused `?companyId=`), not any result view, since no result view existed until item 6 above — now links to the real new detail page; and removed the duplicate copy of the section left behind by the extraction. **Verified live**: Sally's real Dashboard correctly shows "Active status" with two real "Tender Readiness — Delivered" tiles (each linking to the real, correct detail page) and one real "Reviewed — awaiting delivery" tile (correctly showing no link yet) — confirmed alongside the rest of her dashboard content, proving no regression to the report-present case.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, re-checked after every major change.
