## Final consolidated Dashboard/module fixes (confirmed 2026-08-15)

Seven items, worked through individually per direct founder request.

**1. Client email on submission/approval — a real gap surfaced by insisting on fresh confirmation instead of trusting the prior round's claim.** Queried every currently-pending `notifications` row: `module_new_submission` and `module_ready` rows this whole session (including the ones already reported as "confirmed fired" last round) all had `sent_at: null` — the row was correctly logged, but the actual email was never dispatched, since nothing runs `sendPendingNotifications()` automatically in local dev (only the production GitHub Actions cron does). Last round's "confirmed" claim was true at the "row created" level but not at the "email actually sent" level — exactly the gap the founder was right to ask about again rather than accept at face value. Ran `sendPendingNotifications()` for real: 11 sent, 2 failed cleanly (an orphaned recipient with no matching user, and a pre-existing `@example.com` test row — both expected, harmless, sent to nobody). Confirmed via direct DB read: the real `module_new_submission` rows for the most recent submission and the real `module_ready` row for the delivered request both now show a genuine `sent_at` timestamp. All destination addresses were the founder's own accounts (real reviewer identities, real disposable test reviewers, and Sally's own test client account) — confirmed individually before running the dispatcher, not assumed safe.

**2, 4, 5 — Discovery Session / Active status rules, validated then implemented together (they're one coherent design, not three separate fixes).** Confirmed the rule made sense before building: "Active status" now means genuinely active/in-progress only — module requests in `pending_review`/`approved`, non-Discovery sessions `requested`/`scheduled`, sprints `scoped`/`in_progress`. Anything terminal (module `sent`, session `completed`/`declined`, sprint `complete`) moves out entirely. Discovery Session gets its own carve-out on top of that: it never appears on Dashboard in ANY state (not even "requested") — it always lives in Reports & History instead, since it's a pre-evidence exploratory call with no Dashboard-worthy deliverable of its own in this app today. [dashboard/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx>) query filters implement exactly this; a real, separate bug fixed in the same pass — the sprint tile's `activeSprint` computation used to fall back to "the most recent sprint regardless of status" when none was `in_progress`, meaning a `complete` (terminal) sprint could reach the Active-status tile via that fallback, directly contradicting the section's own name. Removed the fallback. **Confirmed live**: Sally's real Dashboard, which previously showed 4 Tender Readiness cards (2 delivered + 2 in-review) and 2 Discovery Session cards, now shows exactly the 2 genuinely-in-review Tender Readiness cards and zero Discovery Session cards — items 2/4/5 resolving together exactly as expected, not three overlapping patches.

**3. Evidence-pack download removed from the client view; procurement-answers copy rewritten.** [services/module/[requestId]/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/services/module/[requestId]/page.tsx>) — the "Download full evidence pack (.md)" link is gone entirely from the client-facing page (the reviewer-only download in `ModuleReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx` is untouched, still the only place it's reachable). The description copy was rewritten to lead with what the questions actually mean to the client, using the founder's own suggested framing almost verbatim, rather than jumping straight to what to do with the answers. **Verified live** against the real delivered request — new copy renders correctly, no download link anywhere on the page.

**6. Progress stepper bug — real, root-caused, fixed.** [ProgressStepper.tsx](src/app/_components/ProgressStepper.tsx)'s `done = i < current` can never be true for the LAST step when `current` equals that step's own index — a genuine off-by-one affecting the "Report" step specifically whenever `journeyStatus.stage === "has_report"` (the one stage where there's nothing after it, so the "current, still in progress" visual treatment was always wrong for it). Fixed with an explicit `isJourneyComplete` case rather than a stage-specific hack, since the same class of bug would recur for any future terminal-stage addition. This is a SHARED component (also used on Evidence Intake, Business Profile, and Reports & History), so it was fixed regardless of item 7's outcome below. **Verified live** on Reports & History (which kept the stepper): all four steps — Profile, Evidence, Review, Report — now show real checkmarks, confirmed via a fresh page load against Sally's real delivered-report account.

**7. Reconsidered whether the stepper belongs on Dashboard — removed it there, kept it everywhere else.** Dashboard's own job (a returning-visitor status view) is different from Evidence Intake/Business Profile/Reports & History's (a first-time linear setup flow) — once a report exists, Dashboard's own real content (Top 3, AI Opportunity, roadmap) already answers "what's my current state" more specifically than a 4-step tracker restating "you're done" would; before a report exists, `NextStepBanner` (already present) already gives one clear next action without needing a parallel journey-position indicator next to it. Removed `<ProgressStepper>` from Dashboard only; the other three pages are unchanged. **Verified live**: Sally's real Dashboard no longer shows a stepper anywhere, while Reports & History (same session, same data) still does.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch verification scripts deleted afterward, never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup. No new or disposable test accounts created this pass — every verification ran against Sally's real, already-existing account and the real requests already in the database from prior rounds.

## Dashboard information-architecture redesign (confirmed 2026-08-15)

Direct founder request, grounded in checked current practice rather than opinion before implementing — the founder's own explicit standard. Confirmed via real search before touching any code: dashboard information architecture is described as "80% of dashboard quality" (visual design is "polish on a foundation that's already right"), with a consistent pattern across serious current sources — a clear executive-summary hero at the top, hierarchical layout (most critical data first, secondary detail progressively revealed below), and operational status/controls treated as secondary, not primary, content. Sources: [dashboard IA and cognitive overload](https://www.sanjaydey.com/saas-dashboard-design-information-architecture-cognitive-overload/), [UXPin's dashboard design principles](https://www.uxpin.com/studio/blog/dashboard-design-principles/), [fintech dashboard patterns on progressive disclosure](https://www.wandr.studio/blog/fintech-dashboard-design). This confirmed the founder's stated principle was accurate, not just asserted — the redesign proceeded on that basis.

**Reordered into the requested narrative** ([dashboard/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx>)):
1. **Real headline status line** — one honest sentence computed from real counts already loaded elsewhere on the page ("3 priorities identified, 2 AI opportunities flagged, 2 requests in review"), never a fabricated score. Deliberately renders nothing when there's genuinely nothing to summarize (a brand-new client with no report and no active requests) — `NextStepBanner` alone is the right hero for that case, not a hollow "0 priorities identified" sentence.
2. **Top 3 Priorities** — promoted to its own full-width section leading the substantive content, no longer sharing a 2-column row with AI Opportunity as if the two were equally primary.
3. **AI Opportunity & Readiness** — immediately after, subtitle rewritten to explicitly follow from the priorities above ("Given what we found above, here's where AI could genuinely help...") rather than reading as a disconnected section.
4. **Roadmap (30/60/90)** — unchanged content, now correctly positioned as "what to do about it, over time" following the diagnosis.
5. **Goal metric trend** (when present) — kept in the diagnosis-narrative group, right after the roadmap — still "what we found," not operational status.
6. **"Your active requests"** — the section previously named "Active status" and positioned first (above the diagnosis) is renamed and moved to its own clearly-labeled, deliberately secondary section below all diagnosis content — real operational status, not competing with the actual findings for attention. No logic changes from the 2026-08-15 Active-status rules built earlier the same day (still `pending_review`/`approved` modules, non-Discovery `requested`/`scheduled` sessions, `scoped`/`in_progress` sprints only) — this pass is purely about position and framing, not eligibility.
7. **Services and support** — unchanged, still last, still progressive disclosure.

**Self-review pass** (the founder's own stated acceptance criteria — "walk through this page with zero prior explanation," not "does each section individually work"): walked the new order end-to-end as a first-time reader would encounter it. No confusion points found — the headline correctly sets expectations for what follows without needing to name every category (that detail is progressively revealed further down, by design); the AI Opportunity subtitle's "given what we found above" phrasing reads coherently as a continuation of Top 3, not a restart; the still-null-synthesis case ("Not generated yet...") reads fine under the new subtitle since the promise ("here's where AI could help") and the honest "not yet" state don't actually conflict.

**Verified live** against Sally's real account (real report + 2 real Tender Readiness requests in review): headline rendered "3 priorities identified, 2 AI opportunities flagged, 2 requests in review" with real, correctly-computed counts; confirmed via screenshot that the headline genuinely reads as the visual hero (large, bold, directly under the page description) with Top 3 immediately below it at full width, AI Opportunity following with the new framing text, and "Your active requests" correctly demoted below all diagnosis content. The `!latestReport` (brand-new client, no headline) path was not re-exercised live this pass — its logic is a straightforward conditional already covered by TypeScript, and re-testing it would have meant disrupting Sally's kept real account rather than adding real verification value.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and scratch verification script deleted afterward, never committed.

## Reviewer-side action buttons — the same uncaught-RPC-failure class swept across every reviewer workspace (confirmed 2026-08-16)

Direct founder bug report: "Approve sprint plan" stayed stuck loading, and the same thing happens on every reviewer action button. Confirmed exactly right, and worse than one button — every single action handler across all three reviewer workspaces had the identical gap.

**Root cause, same class already fixed repeatedly on the client-facing intake forms (Tender Readiness, AI Reliability, Data Protection, Evidence Intake) 2026-08-15, but never propagated to the reviewer side**: every handler followed the pattern `setPending(true); await someAction(...); setPending(false);` with no `try/catch`. A genuine RPC-level failure — a rejected fetch, a platform timeout, an unhandled exception inside the Server Action itself — left `pending` stuck `true` forever, since the line that would reset it never ran. The button read as permanently "loading" with no error and no way to retry, exactly the reported symptom.

**Audited and fixed every action button across all three reviewer workspaces**, not just the one reported:
- [SprintReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review-sprint/[sprintId]/SprintReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx>) — `handleAccept`/`handleReject`/`handleSaveEdit`/`handleApprove` (the exact "Approve sprint plan" button reported)/`handleSaveCommentary`, 5 handlers.
- [ModuleReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review-module/[requestId]/ModuleReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx>) — `handleAccept`/`handleReject`/`handleSaveEdit`/`handleApprove`/`handleGenerateProcurementAnswers`/`handleAcceptAnswer`/`handleRejectAnswer`/`handleSaveAnswerEdit`, 8 handlers.
- [ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review/[reportId]/ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx>) — the core report workspace, 852 lines, the largest and most-used one: `handleSetPlanTier`/`handleAccept`/`handleReject`/`handleSaveEdit`/`handleResolveDispute`/`handleResolveConflict`/`handleMoveTop3`/`handlePromoteToTop3`/`handleApprove`/`handleDeliver`/`handleStartSprint`/`handleRerun`, 12 handlers.

Every handler now wraps its `await` in `try/catch/finally` — `finally` guarantees the loading flag always resets regardless of outcome, `catch` surfaces a real, visible error. Handlers that already had their own dedicated error state (`deliverError`/`sprintError`/`rerunError`/`procurementError`) keep using it, so a thrown exception lands there too, not just a resolved `{success: false}` — those handlers had partial protection (the "business logic failed" case was handled) but none had protection against a genuinely thrown/rejected promise. Handlers with no dedicated error slot share one new `actionError` state per file, rendered as a real `Alert` near the top of the page. One deliberate exception preserved: `handleStartSprint`'s success path does NOT reset its loading flag before `router.push()` — the button stays showing "Drafting tasks…" through the navigation transition, matching the original intended UX; only the failure paths (the `!result.success` branch and `catch`) reset it, since those are the only cases where the reviewer stays on the page.

**Checked, not assumed, whether the queue page's buttons ([queue/page.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/queue/page.tsx>)) had the same gap — they don't, architecturally.** Every action there uses a real `<form action={serverAction}>` binding (React/Next.js's native server-action form mechanism), not a client `useState(pending)` flag manually managed around an `onClick`+`await`. There is no client-side loading boolean to get stuck on that page — a thrown exception there is handled by Next.js's own error boundary, not an infinite spinner. Confirmed by reading `queue/actions.ts` directly rather than assumed safe by pattern-matching the file list.

**Verified live against the exact reported button, with a real, reproduced failure**, not just code-reviewed: created a real scoped Execution Sprint from a real approved finding (`createSprintFromFinding()`), accepted all 5 real tasks, signed in as a real reviewer, and opened the real workspace. Monkey-patched `window.fetch` to reject every call (`TypeError: Failed to fetch`, the same technique already established in this codebase for reproducing this exact failure class, since it can't be triggered from a healthy local backend) and clicked the real "Approve sprint plan" button via a genuine DOM `.click()` call. Confirmed via direct inspection of the live DOM: the button was **not** stuck disabled (`disabled: false`) and the real error alert rendered (`"Something went wrong reaching the server — please try again."`) — exactly the fix working, where before this pass it would have stayed disabled forever with nothing shown. Restored `fetch` and clicked the same button again for real: the sprint's status genuinely flipped to `in_progress` with real `start_date`/`target_end_date` stamped, confirming the fix introduces no regression to the working path.

**Deliberately not re-run for every one of the other 24 handlers individually** — the fix is mechanically identical (the same `try/catch/finally` wrapper around the same `await` pattern) across all of them, already confirmed via `tsc`/`eslint`/`build`, and the one live reproduction proves the pattern itself works correctly end-to-end against a real failure and a real recovery. Re-running the identical monkey-patch-and-click sequence 24 more times would not add real verification value over confirming the shared pattern once against the exact reported case.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch scripts deleted afterward, never committed. The test sprint created for this verification (`1612a745-...`, a real sprint against Nimbus Ledger Ltd) was left in the database — real proof of the fix working end-to-end, same retention precedent as every other first-of-its-kind live verification in this file.

## Final Dashboard redesign brief — all 10 items built and verified live (confirmed 2026-08-16)

Direct founder brief consolidating everything agreed across the whole Dashboard-redesign thread, explicitly framed as the final pass and tested against a stated bar: "a first-time founder should look at this page for 10 seconds and know exactly what's wrong, what it's costing them, and what to do next — with zero explanation needed." All 10 items built as one coherent pass, then live-verified in-browser (not just code-reviewed), with real screenshots.

**1. New Signals page** ([src/app/(app)/signals/](<src/app/(app)/signals/>)) — a genuinely new, standalone page (added to the shared nav in `(app)/layout.tsx`), not a duplicate of the Report page: a single unified list of every finding across the Core Audit's 5 lenses AND every delivered module (Tender Readiness/AI Reliability/Data Protection), filterable by source and severity (multi-select toggle chips, client-side). **Verified live** against Sally's real account — 24 real findings across Financial/Product/Commercial/AI & Governance/Tender Readiness rendered in one list; toggling off every source except Financial correctly narrowed the list to exactly her 3 real Financial findings, proving the filter is real, not decorative.

**2. Per-finding "Does not apply to us" feedback** — a real, new correctness signal, deliberately separate from the existing "Interested in help? Yes/Not now/Something else" (which is about *acting* on a finding, not disputing its validity). New `finding_feedback` table ([migration](supabase/migrations/20260816120000_finding_feedback.sql)) — **deliberately no FK on `finding_id`**, since it can reference either `lens_findings` or `module_findings` (disambiguated by a `finding_feedback_source` enum column), same "polymorphic reference validated in application code, not the DB" pattern already used elsewhere in this codebase where a single physical column must point at more than one table. `submitFindingNotApplicableFeedback()` ([src/lib/reports/finding-feedback.ts](src/lib/reports/finding-feedback.ts)) verifies company ownership and per-source finding ownership (via `lens_findings→reports→company_id` or `module_findings→module_requests→company_id`) before writing, using the admin client only after that check — same discipline as every other admin-client write in this codebase. New shared [FindingNotApplicableButton](src/app/_components/FindingNotApplicableButton.tsx), wired into both Signals and the client Report page, gated on `!isMissingDataFinding` (nothing to dispute about an evidence gap). **Verified live**: clicked "Doesn't apply to us?" on a real finding, confirmed the button flipped to "Noted as not applicable — thanks for the feedback," and confirmed via direct DB read that a real row (`finding_source: "lens_finding"`, correct `finding_id`/`finding_title`/`company_id`) was persisted.

**3. Client's stated goal, pinned on Dashboard** — a real goal-pin strip directly under the page subtitle (`primary_goal`/`secondary_goal`, an "Edit" link to Business Profile), not buried in a separate page. **Verified live**: "Your goal: Growth / Revenue Efficiency · also: Execution Speed."

**4. Delivered/completed services stay off Dashboard as cards** (confirmed, keeping the existing "Active status = non-terminal only" rule from the 2026-08-15 pass intact) — a new lightweight summary line instead: real parallel count queries (delivered core reports + delivered modules + completed sessions + completed sprints vs. the existing in-progress count) render as one sentence. **Verified live**: "4 services delivered, 3 in progress — View all in Reports & History."

**5. Single action banner at the top** — the #1 priority (from the report's own `top_3_finding_ids`, first entry), its real financial impact when quantified, its real cascade count when upstream of 2+ other findings, one CTA ("Start fixing this" → the full report). Renders nothing when there's no report yet (`NextStepBanner` is the right "what to do" answer for that case). **Verified live**: "HIGHEST PRIORITY RIGHT NOW — Critical Customer Revenue Concentration — Start fixing this" (no cost/cascade line for this specific finding, since Sally's #1 priority genuinely has neither a usable quantified impact nor a cascade count ≥2 — an honest empty state, not a bug; see the Signals verification above for proof the underlying mechanism does render real values when they exist).

**6 & 7. Top 3 Priorities — severity + financial impact inline, plus a real aggregated exposure line.** Each `<li>` now shows a severity badge and (when quantified) an inline "Estimated impact" line, matching the depth already present in AI Opportunity cards. `aggregateFinancialImpact()` ([src/lib/reports/financial-impact.ts](src/lib/reports/financial-impact.ts)) sums real per-finding `impactBandLow`/`impactBandHigh` across the top-3 set and renders an honest range with a genuine confidence caveat when not every finding is quantified ("based on N of M findings with a quantified estimate") — never false precision. **Real, disclosed simplification**: cross-currency summation isn't attempted (this app is UK-first V1, every real run to date has been GBP) — the aggregate uses whichever currency the first quantified finding reports.

**8. Real cascade count in the banner** — reuses `computeCascadeSignals()` ([src/lib/recommendations/cascade.ts](src/lib/recommendations/cascade.ts)) directly, the exact same deterministic cross-finding cascade computation the roadmap already uses (built 2026-08-14), not a second implementation or a generic hardcoded warning. Shown only when `cascadeCount >= 2`, with the real downstream finding titles as a hover tooltip.

**9. Every "Your active requests" card gets one explanatory line** — new `MODULE_EXPLANATION`/`SESSION_EXPLANATION`/`SPRINT_EXPLANATION` copy maps render one real sentence per card ("A bounded, paid implementation engagement fixing this one finding — track task progress on the sprint page," etc.), not just a bare status word. **Verified live** across all three card types on Sally's real account.

**10. Remove purely-informational sections** — audited every remaining Dashboard section against "what should I do / what happened": Goal metric trend (what happened — real numeric progression), Strengths by lens (what happened — real per-lens ratio), Roadmap (what to do, by timeframe), Services and support (what to do — real links). Nothing found to remove; every section already answers one of the two questions following the 2026-08-15 IA redesign pass — documented here as a real audit outcome, not skipped.

**Real, live-discovered data-quality bug found and fixed in the same pass, before screenshotting anything**: checking real accounts for a clean quantified-impact example surfaced two genuine cases of the LLM not following its own "set financialImpact to null when you can't responsibly estimate one" prompt rule cleanly — (1) a real "Cash Runway" finding with `impactBandLow: -150000, impactBandHigh: -75000` (a negative "estimated cost," which reads as a formatting bug to a client even though the number itself is real), and (2) a real "Gross Margin" finding with `impactBandLow: 0, impactBandHigh: 0, currency: "unknown", confidenceLevel: "insufficient"` — a degenerate placeholder the model should have nulled out instead. Neither was fabricated by this pass; both are real, pre-existing LLM output already in the database (Fulltest Analytics Ltd's real delivered report). Fixed with one centralized guard, `isUsableFinancialImpact()` ([src/lib/reports/financial-impact.ts](src/lib/reports/financial-impact.ts)), requiring both bands non-null, both non-negative, not a degenerate 0–0 pair, and `confidenceLevel !== "insufficient"` — wired into all three display sites (Dashboard's banner + Top 3, the Report page's per-finding display, and Signals), replacing each site's own previously-inconsistent ad hoc `!== null` check (the Report page's old check was the most permissive, rendering raw unformatted numbers with a `"?"` fallback — exactly where a client would have seen the negative/degenerate bug live). **Verified live against the exact real bad data**: Fulltest Analytics Ltd's report page correctly shows zero "Estimated impact" line for all four bad findings (the two above, plus two more `0–0/insufficient` cases on Execution findings), while a fifth real finding on the same report with a genuinely valid `£0–£10,000` low-(not insufficient-)confidence range still renders correctly — proving the guard neither under- nor over-suppresses.

**Verified live end-to-end, real accounts, both light and dark mode, 2026-08-16**: Sally's real account (Dashboard — goal pin, action banner, headline with real counts, Top 3 with severity badges, active-requests explanatory lines, delivered-services summary line, all confirmed via screenshot in both color schemes; Signals — full unified list, working source/severity filters, real quantified impacts correctly formatted with currency symbols and thousands separators, the "Doesn't apply to us?" button confirmed working with a real persisted DB row) and Fulltest Analytics Ltd's real account (the negative/degenerate financial-impact guard, confirmed suppressing exactly the bad values and nothing else). `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, re-checked after the guard fix and again after cleanup. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch verification scripts deleted afterward, never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup. The real `finding_feedback` row and the fresh 2026-08-16 timestamp it carries were left in the database as real proof, same retention precedent as every other first-of-its-kind live verification in this file.

**Not yet committed/pushed** — held for explicit go-ahead, same standing pattern as every other change this session.

## Execution Sprint: real client confirm-or-reselect step (confirmed 2026-08-18)

Direct founder question, answered honestly before building anything, per the standing "report current behavior first, then implement if the gap is confirmed" rule: **confirmed no** — a reviewer selecting a finding and creating an Execution Sprint had zero client confirmation step. `createSprintFromFinding()` immediately AI-drafted tasks, and `approveSprintTasks()` (once the reviewer cleared its own mandatory Accept/Edit/Reject gate) flipped the sprint straight from `scoped` to `in_progress` — fully client-visible, already started, with no notification even fired at proposal time. The client's "scoped" holding page didn't even name which finding had been picked.

**Fixed with a new leading `sprint_status` stage, `proposed`** (`alter type sprint_status add value 'proposed' before 'scoped'`), splitting the old single-step function into two:
- **`proposeSprintFinding(reportId, findingId)`** ([src/lib/execution-sprint/workspace.ts](src/lib/execution-sprint/workspace.ts)) — the reviewer's real entry point, unchanged in who triggers it or which finding they pick (still their call, not opened up to a free client choice from scratch). Creates the sprint in `proposed` status with the selected finding — deliberately does **not** draft tasks yet — and logs a real, new client-facing `sprint_proposed` notification (new `notifications.related_sprint_id` column, same structured-link precedent as `related_report_id`).
- **`confirmSprintFinding(sprintId, confirmedFindingId)`** — the real gap-closer, only runs once the client has acted. `confirmedFindingId` must be either the reviewer's original pick, or a finding the client had previously marked "interested in help" on for the SAME report (verified against `sprint_interest_requests`, re-checked defensively inside this function since its caller is a client-facing Server Action) — this is the "not a free choice from scratch" boundary the founder specified. Once confirmed, this runs the exact task-drafting work `createSprintFromFinding()` used to run immediately (reused `loadCompanyProfileForLens`/`loadGoalContext` from `@/lib/audit/load-profile` here for the first time, replacing an inlined copy — a small, real dedup along the way), persists `sprint_tasks` in `draft`, and only then flips the sprint to `scoped` — the exact same status the reviewer's own mandatory Accept/Edit/Reject gate (`approveSprintTasks()`) already required before, completely unchanged.

**Real client-facing UI** ([ProposedSprintConfirm.tsx](<src/app/(app)/execution-sprint/[sprintId]/ProposedSprintConfirm.tsx>)) — shown at `/execution-sprint/[sprintId]` whenever `status === "proposed"`: the reviewer's suggested finding with its real diagnosis, a radio choice between it and any real alternatives the client had marked "interested" on for this report (with an explicit note that this isn't an open choice), and a "Confirm this"/"Confirm my choice" button that calls the new `confirmSprintFindingAction()` (session-verified ownership + status check, then the real re-verification inside `confirmSprintFinding()` itself). **Reviewer sprint workspace** ([SprintReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review-sprint/[sprintId]/SprintReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx>)) gets a matching `proposed` holding branch — a real "waiting on client confirmation" message naming the proposed finding, not a confusing disabled Approve button over an empty task list. **Dashboard** ([dashboard/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx>)) — `proposed` added to the active-sprint status filter (it's non-terminal, same as `scoped`/`in_progress`), with its own status label ("Awaiting your confirmation") and explanation line; the task-count line is correctly skipped for `proposed` (no tasks exist yet) instead of showing a confusing "0 of 0 tasks done." **Reviewer's own entry point** ([ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review/[reportId]/ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx>)) — "Start an Execution Sprint" renamed "Propose an Execution Sprint," copy and button text updated to describe the real new flow.

**Verified live**, using this session's established "reviewer-side proven via direct function call, client-side proven via real browser click-through" split (reviewer auth/session mechanics were already proven repeatedly this session; this round focused on proving the new mechanism itself): called `proposeSprintFinding()` directly against a real approved finding on Sally's real, kept report — confirmed via direct DB read: `status: "proposed"`, `confirmed_at: null`, zero `sprint_tasks` rows, and a real `sprint_proposed` notification row with the correct `recipient_id` and `related_sprint_id`. The real reviewer sprint workspace correctly rendered the new "waiting on client confirmation" holding state, naming the real proposed finding. Signed in as Sally's real client session and drove the real `/execution-sprint/[sprintId]` UI through actual browser clicks: the proposed finding rendered with its real diagnosis; a real alternative finding (seeded via a genuine `sprint_interest_requests` row with `response: 'interested'`) rendered as a selectable option with the correct "not a free choice" framing; clicking it correctly re-selected the radio and changed the button text from "Confirm this" to "Confirm my choice" — real state, not decorative; clicking confirm correctly called the real Server Action.

**A real, separate, urgent issue surfaced during this exact verification, not caused by this work**: the confirm call failed with `Groq JSON completion failed`, and isolating it directly showed the real cause — a `404 model_not_found`, because `llama-3.3-70b-versatile` (the single hardcoded default model in [src/lib/ai-client/config.ts](src/lib/ai-client/config.ts), the one shared config point for every AI call in this entire app — lenses, all three modules, and now Execution Sprint) has been fully removed from Groq's available models for this API key (confirmed via a direct `GET /v1/models` call — it's genuinely gone, not a rate limit or transient failure). **This blocks every real AI call in the app right now, not just this feature** — flagged rather than silently patched, since picking a replacement model is a real product decision (different models mean different output quality/behavior across every lens and module, not a config value to guess at) and no `AI_MODEL` env override is currently set to fall back on. Confirmed the failure is atomic and clean on this exact new code path — no partial writes, `confirmSprintFinding()` throws before touching `sprint_tasks`/`execution_sprints`, and the client-facing error state rendered correctly with the button re-enabled (real proof the try/catch error handling works, not just the happy path) — re-confirmed via direct DB read after the failed attempt. The `proposed` sprint this created against Sally's real report was left in the database as real proof `proposeSprintFinding()` works end-to-end — once a replacement model is confirmed, the same sprint can be confirmed for real, continuing the same proof chain rather than starting a new one.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch scripts (including the synthetic `sprint_interest_requests` fixture row used only to exercise the reselect UI) deleted afterward, never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup.

**Not yet committed/pushed** — held for explicit go-ahead, same standing pattern as every other change this session.

## Real production incident: Groq model deprecation, fixed via a real side-by-side comparison (confirmed 2026-08-18)

Surfaced while live-verifying the Execution Sprint confirm step above, then explicitly escalated as top priority: `llama-3.3-70b-versatile` — the single hardcoded model backing `aiConfig.model` in [src/lib/ai-client/config.ts](src/lib/ai-client/config.ts), the one shared config point for every AI call in this entire app (all 5 lenses, all 3 modules, Execution Sprint task-drafting) — had been fully removed from Groq's available models for this API key. Confirmed via a direct `GET /v1/models` call, not assumed from the error alone: the model is genuinely gone, not a rate limit or transient failure. **This meant every real AI call in the app was broken**, not just the new confirm step that happened to surface it first.

**Real comparison run before picking a replacement, per explicit direction not to guess** — 2 candidates (`qwen/qwen3.6-27b`, `openai/gpt-oss-20b`) against 3 real prompts already used in this codebase (the Financial lens's `runDraft()`, AI Reliability Audit's conversational-mode `runAiReliabilityAudit()`, and Execution Sprint's `draftSprintTasks()`), run via the real functions with realistic evidence, model swapped per-run via `AI_MODEL` env — the exact mechanism `config.ts` already exposes, proving the swap really is a config change, not a rewrite.

- **`qwen/qwen3.6-27b` — failed 2 of 3, one of them structurally, not just a quality miss.** Financial lens failed with a genuine Groq-side `400 json_validate_failed`. AI Reliability's conversational mode failed outright with `"failed to render text output: ... No user query found in messages"` — a real, structural chat-template incompatibility: this codebase has **4 real call sites** (Tender Readiness, Data Protection Compliance, and both AI Reliability Audit modes) that build their entire prompt as a single system-role message with no separate user message — a pattern that worked fine on `llama-3.3` and works fine on `openai/gpt-oss-20b`, but qwen's chat template rejects outright. Fixing this for qwen would mean restructuring 4 real prompt-construction call sites — directly against the explicit "clean config change, not a rewrite" requirement — so qwen was ruled out on structural grounds alone, independent of the JSON-validation failure.
- **`openai/gpt-oss-20b` — passed cleanly, twice in a row.** Financial lens: correct findings, the deterministic benchmark-comparison sentence honored verbatim, correct `isMissingDataFinding`/`confidenceLevel: "insufficient"` handling for the sparse evidence category. AI Reliability conversational mode: exactly 1 finding for the genuine FAIL transcript, zero spurious findings for the correctly-handled PASS transcript — the exact "silent PASS/FAIL" failure class already documented and fixed once in this codebase's history, passing cleanly here. Execution Sprint: 6 well-scoped tasks both runs, all within the 28-day window, correct count range, real KPIs with directions. **Deliberately not `openai/gpt-oss-120b`** — this codebase has its own separate, already-documented real prior failure with that exact model under strict JSON schema mode (fully reverted at the time); this app's Groq calls use soft `json_object` mode, not strict schema mode, so that specific failure mode doesn't directly apply, but `-120b` wasn't re-tested and isn't the safe default to reach for again without its own comparison — `generateValidatedJson`'s schema-validation safety net (built after that original incident) is real and would catch a schema violation regardless of which model is configured, but a model that reliably produces correct output the first time is still the better default.

**Fixed at all three layers that actually matter, not just the code default**: `.env.local` (real local dev value) and `.env.local.example` (committed, documents the expected value for anyone setting up dev/deploy) both updated from `AI_MODEL=llama-3.3-70b-versatile` to `AI_MODEL=openai/gpt-oss-20b`; `config.ts`'s own fallback default updated too, with the full comparison result recorded in its docblock so a future reader doesn't have to reconstruct why this specific model was chosen. **Still genuinely open, disclosed rather than assumed handled**: this fix updates local `.env.local`/`.env.local.example` and the code default — it does **not** reach a deployed Vercel project's own env vars, which are configured separately in the Vercel dashboard (same "local file changes don't propagate to Vercel automatically" caveat already documented for `CRON_SECRET` elsewhere in this file) — `AI_MODEL` needs to be set (or cleared, so the new code default takes over) as a real Vercel project env var before this fix reaches production.

**Verified live, closing the loop on the interrupted Execution Sprint proof from earlier the same session**: re-attempted confirming Sally's real, still-`proposed` sprint (left clean and unconfirmed by the earlier model failure) through the real browser UI, using the real `.env.local` config with no manual override — confirmed via direct DB read: `status: "scoped"`, `confirmed_at` stamped, 5 real, well-scoped `sprint_tasks` rows in `draft`, ready for the reviewer's Accept/Edit/Reject pass. This is the real app's own code path succeeding end-to-end on the new model, not just the isolated comparison script.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout (including after clearing a stale `.next` cache reference to the deleted temporary debug route). Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch comparison/verification scripts deleted afterward, never committed.

## Six real bug/gap fixes from live testing, incl. a "same feature already built and shipped" honest check (confirmed 2026-08-19)

Direct founder batch, worked through individually. Item 6 turned out to already be resolved (shipped in commit `33de82b`, the same conversation's prior model-fix pass) — confirmed the code is genuinely still in place rather than assuming, then reported that honestly instead of re-investigating or re-building.

**1. "Top 3 priorities" — no longer a hardcoded count.** [src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx>) — the card now derives `urgentFindings` from every approved/edited finding at `critical`/`high` severity, however many that genuinely is, and titles the card via `computeUrgentLabel()`: "Your top priority (1)" for a single item, "Your {severity} priorities (N)" only when every shown finding genuinely shares that one severity, otherwise the honest-but-generic "Your top priorities (N)" for a mixed set — derived directly from the two examples given, not guessed. The reviewer's own curated `top_3_finding_ids` selection is untouched — it still drives the action banner's #1 pick and the roadmap derivation, a deliberate reviewer judgment call this pass doesn't touch; this is a genuinely separate, additional list. The headline sentence ("N priorities identified...") was also switched to the same honest count, for internal consistency — leaving it on the old fixed-≤3 number while the card below it counted something different would have shown two different numbers both labeled "priorities" on the same page. **Verified live** against Sally's real account: 8 real critical/high findings (mixed severities) correctly rendered "Your top priorities (8)," with all 8 titles, severities, and quantified impacts listed.

**2. Reviewer-facing overdue-delivery alert — real gap, not just a missing badge.** [src/app/(reviewer)/queue/page.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/queue/page.tsx>) — confirmed by grep before building: the queue had **no listing at all** for module requests once a reviewer approved them (the page only ever queried `status = 'pending_review'`), so "reviewed and approved" modules disappeared from the queue entirely with nothing to remind the reviewer delivery was still owed — this needed a new section, not just a badge on an existing one. New "Awaiting delivery" section lists every `module_requests` row at `status = 'approved'`, with the exact same visual "Overdue" badge pattern as the existing "Ready for review" section. Modules have no client-edit-window step (they go straight to `pending_review` on submission), so there's no separate `submitted_at`/`edit_window_closes_at` pair to anchor a deadline against the way core reports do — reused the existing DB-backed `getTotalTurnaroundHours()` target (the same 72h-by-default number core reports already enforce) against `created_at`, which genuinely is the real submission moment for a module request, rather than inventing a second, module-specific SLA number. **Verified live**: all 5 real approved-but-undelivered module requests across two real companies correctly listed, all 5 correctly flagged Overdue (all genuinely past 72h — the oldest since 2026-08-02).

**3. Client-facing overdue-delivery copy — exact requested wording, one real bug caught fixing it.** Same Dashboard module card, conditional on the same overdue check: "This was reviewed and approved on [date] and is taking a little longer than expected to reach you — we're on it, and you'll get an email the moment it's ready." **Real bug caught live, not assumed clean**: the first render showed "8/15/2026and is taking" — no space — the exact same JSX whitespace-collapse gotcha already documented and fixed elsewhere in this codebase (a plain space immediately after a multi-line `{expression}` text node is silently dropped). Fixed with an explicit `{" "}` after the date expression, matching the established pattern; re-verified live afterward with the space correctly present.

**4. Sprint page narrative intro, KPI unit, and change-request explainer — all three, exact requested copy.** [ExecutionSprintClient.tsx](<src/app/(app)/execution-sprint/[sprintId]/ExecutionSprintClient.tsx>) — added the exact intro sentence above the task list; the "KPI unit" ask had no dedicated schema field to pull from (`kpi_description`/`kpi_target_value`/`kpi_direction` only, no `kpi_unit`) — `kpi_description` itself (e.g. "Percentage of budget variance identified and documented") already IS the honest unit label, so it's now shown as the `Actual` input's `hint` rather than inventing a new field for the same information; added the one-line "Ask your reviewer to adjust a task's owner, due date, or scope." directly under "Request a change." **Verified live** against Riverbank Analytics Ltd's real `in_progress` sprint — all three render exactly as requested, confirmed via screenshot.

**5. "Want to work on a different priority instead?" — real gap, now connected.** Dashboard's sprint card links to `/reports/{reportId}` (the report page where the existing `SprintInterestButton`/"Interested in help implementing this?" mechanism already lives on every eligible finding) rather than deep-linking to one specific "different" finding — there's no single well-defined "the" alternative among potentially several eligible findings, so linking to the full report where the client can browse and pick any of them honestly matches how the mechanism already works. Deliberately hidden while `status === "proposed"`, since during that stage the client is already being asked to pick a finding via the sprint's own confirm-or-reselect page (item 6) — a second, separate "want something else" link there would be redundant. **Verified live**: renders correctly on Sally's real `in_progress` sprint card, correctly linking to her real report.

**6. Client confirm-or-reselect step before a Sprint begins — already resolved, confirmed not re-investigated blind.** This is the exact same gap and fix already built, live-verified, and shipped earlier the same session (see "Execution Sprint: real client confirm-or-reselect step" above, commit `33de82b`) — confirmed via a direct grep that `proposeSprintFinding()`/`confirmSprintFinding()`/the `proposed` sprint status are still genuinely present in the codebase before reporting this back, rather than assuming or silently re-building it.

A real React-purity lint rule caught two inline `Date.now()` calls introduced while building items 2/3 (Server Components must render as pure functions) — fixed by matching this codebase's own pre-existing, already-shipped idiom for "now" comparisons (`new Date()` construction, not the bare `Date.now()` static method), not by suppressing the rule.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, re-checked after the whitespace-bug fix. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch verification scripts deleted afterward, never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup.

Committed and pushed (commit `a55ab92`), per explicit go-ahead.

## Real, dedicated `kpi_unit` schema field for Sprint task KPIs (confirmed 2026-08-19)

Direct founder decision, following straight from item 4 of the six-item batch above: rather than continuing to borrow `kpi_description` as the client-facing "Actual" input's hint (the interim fix shipped in commit `a55ab92`), add a real, dedicated unit field to the schema — populated at draft time, editable by the reviewer, shown directly next to "Actual."

**Complexity/risk, reported before starting, per standing discipline**: assessed and confirmed low — one additive nullable column, a well-precedented one-field addition to an already-proven AI-drafting schema (`kpiDescription`/`kpiTargetValue`/`kpiDirection` all already work this exact way), and a small, mechanical wiring pass through backend persistence + two already-existing reviewer/client UI surfaces. No new architecture.

**Backfill decision — made as a confirmed default, not escalated, per the founder's own framing ("your call, flag if you think it needs my input")**: left NULL for every pre-existing row, no automated backfill attempted. Heuristically inferring a clean unit (`"%"`, `"days"`, `"£"`...) from `kpi_description`'s free text would be exactly the kind of fabricated-structured-data-from-free-text this codebase has repeatedly and explicitly refused to do elsewhere (the `directly_supports`/`directly_affects` goal-relevance fixes, the recommendation-library keyword-matching discipline, the export-signature research). The UI falls back to `kpi_description` when `kpi_unit` is null — precisely today's already-shipped interim behavior — so existing rows are functionally unaffected either way, just without a fabricated-looking unit tag bolted onto old data.

**Schema** ([supabase/migrations/20260819090000_sprint_tasks_kpi_unit.sql](supabase/migrations/20260819090000_sprint_tasks_kpi_unit.sql), pushed live) — `alter table sprint_tasks add column kpi_unit text;`, nullable, no backfill, full rationale in the migration's own docblock.

**AI drafting** ([src/lib/execution-sprint/draft-tasks.ts](src/lib/execution-sprint/draft-tasks.ts)) — `kpiUnit: z.string()` added to `draftedTaskSchema`; `SYSTEM_PROMPT` gained a new rule ("a short label for what kpiTargetValue is measured in — e.g. '%', 'days', 'hours', '£', 'prospects', 'tickets/week'... never a repeat of the full kpiDescription sentence") and the corresponding OUTPUT SCHEMA JSON entry.

**Backend persistence** ([src/lib/execution-sprint/workspace.ts](src/lib/execution-sprint/workspace.ts)) — `confirmSprintFinding()`'s task insert now writes `kpi_unit: t.kpiUnit`; `SprintTaskEdit` gained `kpiUnit?: string`; `editSprintTask()` gained the matching conditional update line — same pattern as every other KPI field, no new logic shape.

**Reviewer UI** ([SprintReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review-sprint/[sprintId]/SprintReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx>)) — `TaskCard`'s KPI line now appends the real unit when present (`target 5 customers`, no trailing artifact when null); `EditForm` gained a real "Unit" `Input` (placeholder `%, days, £...`) alongside the existing target-value/direction fields, wired into its own local state and the save payload. Both `page.tsx` select statements (reviewer and client) updated to select the new column.

**Client UI** ([ExecutionSprintClient.tsx](<src/app/(app)/execution-sprint/[sprintId]/ExecutionSprintClient.tsx>)) — task display line updated the same way as the reviewer's; the "Actual" input's `hint` now reads `task.kpi_unit ?? task.kpi_description ?? undefined` — supersedes the interim fix from the six-item batch (which read `kpi_description` unconditionally) with a real unit when one exists, falling back to the old behavior for null rows.

**Verified live end-to-end, real Groq call plus real browser sessions, not just code-reviewed**:
- A real `draftSprintTasks()` call (real Groq, no mocking) against a synthetic Commercial finding produced 6 tasks, every one with a genuinely varied, sensible `kpiUnit` ("items", "words", "%") — confirming the schema and prompt work, not just parse without erroring.
- `editSprintTask({kpiUnit: "%"})` called directly against a real, kept pre-migration row (Sally's account) correctly persisted `kpi_unit: "%"` and flipped `reviewer_status` to `edited`; reverted to the exact original state (`null`/`draft`) afterward via direct DB read confirming the revert.
- **Reviewer workspace, real browser session** (signed in as the real reviewer via the established `admin.generateLink()` + `verifyOtp()` technique through a temporary, never-committed `/api/debug-set-session` route): loaded Sally's real `scoped` sprint, confirmed the 5 pre-migration tasks correctly show no unit artifact (`target 5 (higher is better)`, not `target 5 undefined`); clicked Edit on the first task, confirmed the new "Unit" input renders correctly; typed `customers`, saved, confirmed the task display immediately re-rendered as `target 5 customers (higher is better)` and the status flipped to `edited` — then reverted the row to its exact original state (`kpi_unit: null`, `reviewer_status: draft`) via direct DB write, confirmed via read-back.
- **Client page, real browser session** (signed in as Sally): loaded her real `in_progress` sprint — confirmed all 3 remaining pre-migration tasks show no unit artifact in the KPI line and correctly fall back to `kpi_description` as the "Actual" hint (e.g. "Percentage of complete monthly CAC data points collected"). Temporarily set a real `kpi_unit: "%"` on one task via direct DB write, reloaded: confirmed the KPI line now reads `target 100 % (higher is better)` and the "Actual" hint now reads the real unit (`%`) instead of the long description — proving the real-value path renders correctly on the client side too, not just the reviewer side. Reverted to `null` afterward via direct DB write, confirmed via read-back.

**A real, unrelated environment failure surfaced and resolved mid-task, not caused by this feature**: partway through, this session's own filesystem access to the entire project directory (not just one file) was suddenly denied at the OS level (`Operation not permitted` on `ls`/`cat`/`git status`, traced to a revoked/lost macOS Full Disk Access grant for the nested `claude-code` app bundle inside `Claude.app`). Diagnosed by testing progressively higher directory levels (confirming it wasn't scoped to one file), identifying the exact process chain via `ps`, and confirming via `System Events` that the file was still visible at the OS level while direct file I/O was blocked — consistent with a TCC (Transparency, Consent, and Control) permission issue, not a corrupted repo or a Claude Code bug. Resolved once the founder re-granted Full Disk Access in System Settings and fully quit-and-reopened the app (a simple toggle flip without a full process restart did not take effect). Two empty, 0-byte lock-artifact files (`.!<pid>!page.tsx`) were left behind in two directories from the failed access attempts during the outage — confirmed harmless (the real `page.tsx` files were untouched) and deleted as part of this pass's cleanup.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, re-checked after the temporary debug route was removed. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch verification/DB-check scripts deleted afterward, never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup. No test data left in an altered state — both real, kept accounts (Sally's `scoped` and `in_progress` sprints) were restored to their exact pre-verification values.

Committed and pushed (commit `83f7be0`), per explicit go-ahead.

## Dashboard: Execution Sprint given its own summary line, goal "Edit" link fixed (confirmed 2026-08-19)

Direct founder follow-up, 4 real questions/issues on the redesigned Dashboard. Per explicit instruction, #3 and #4 were investigated and confirmed by reading the actual code before anything was built — not guessed at. #1 (headline mixing finding-counts with active-request-counts) and #2 (the "Top priorities (N)" card doing double duty as both an exhaustive severity count and an implied curated "Top 3") were reported alongside #3/#4 but **no direction was given on them in this round** — they remain open, not addressed here.

**#3, confirmed by reading [dashboard/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx>) directly**: yes, the Execution Sprint was folded into the generic delivered/in-progress count alongside module requests and completed/scheduled sessions — `deliveredCount` included `complete` sprints, `inProgressCount` included `1` for any active sprint, with no way to tell from the number alone that one of those items was a categorically different kind of engagement (a bounded, paid, multi-week implementation project) than "we sent you a report."

**#4, confirmed by grepping every write to the `goals` table and every write to `reports.top_3_finding_ids`**: two layered findings. (a) There is currently no UI/action anywhere in the app that lets a client change `primary_goal`/`secondary_goal` post-onboarding — the only `INSERT` is at signup ([onboarding/actions.ts](src/app/onboarding/actions.ts)), the only `UPDATE` touches the unrelated `desired_future_state_primary`/`secondary` narrative field ([desired-future-state.ts](src/lib/goals/desired-future-state.ts)); Business Profile's own goal card renders the goal name read-only. (b) Even if the goal could be changed, nothing would retroactively re-rank an existing report — `top_3_finding_ids` is written exactly once at report-creation time ([run-audit.ts](src/lib/audit/run-audit.ts), a deterministic default ranking from each finding's severity/`goalRelevance` *as computed at that generation moment*) and the only other writer is the reviewer's manual `reRankTop3()` ([workspace.ts](src/lib/reviewer/workspace.ts)); each finding's own `goalRelevance` is baked into `ai_draft`/`reviewer_edited_content` at LLM-generation time and never recomputed. A report is a genuinely frozen snapshot — the only way to reflect a new goal is a brand-new audit run. Also flagged, not part of the original ask but directly relevant: the Dashboard's "Your goal: ... **Edit**" link routed to Business Profile, which doesn't actually let you edit the goal — only the narrative field underneath it.

**Direction confirmed, both built** (real goal-editing correctly deferred as its own substantial piece of scope — would need to define whether/how it triggers a new audit, pricing implications — not built here):

1. **Execution Sprint given its own line, separate from the module/session summary.** `deliveredCount`/`inProgressCount` no longer include sprint counts (module + delivered/completed sessions only); a new `sprintSummaryCount` (complete sprints) plus the existing `activeSprint` drive a second, explicitly-labeled line: `Execution Sprint: {N} complete{, }{1 in progress} — View in Reports & History.` — handles both branches cleanly (both present, either alone, or neither, in which case the line doesn't render at all).
2. **The misleading "Edit" link removed entirely**, replaced with honest copy pointing at the real mechanism per the founder's own suggested wording: "Want to pursue a different goal? Submit new evidence to start a fresh audit reflecting it." — linking to `/evidence-intake`, not a broken promise of in-place editing. The goal pill itself is now purely a read-only display; the new sentence sits directly beneath it.

**Verified live, real browser sessions, real accounts, real screenshots — both branch combinations of the sprint line**:
- **Riverbank Analytics Ltd** (1 `complete` sprint + 1 `in_progress` sprint, 1 delivered core report, 1 requested + 1 scheduled session): confirmed live — "1 service delivered, 1 in progress — View all in Reports & History." (module+session only, sprint no longer folded in) directly followed by "Execution Sprint: 1 complete, 1 in progress — View in Reports & History." on its own line. Goal pill correctly showed "Cash Flow / Margin Efficiency" with the new honest copy and no Edit link, `href="/evidence-intake"` confirmed via the accessibility tree and a direct DOM query.
- **Sally** (0 complete sprints, 1 `scoped`-status sprint counted as active, 4 delivered services, 2 active requests): confirmed live — "4 services delivered, 2 in progress" (correctly dropped from the old 5/3 that used to fold the sprint in) followed by "Execution Sprint: 1 in progress — View in Reports & History." — confirmed the single-branch case renders cleanly with no stray "0 complete" or trailing-comma artifact. Goal pill correctly showed both primary and secondary goals with the same fixed copy.

**One real, unrelated dev-artifact issue hit during verification, not an app bug**: after deleting the temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route, `tsc --noEmit` initially still failed on a stale `.next/dev/types/validator.ts` reference to the deleted route — a leftover Next.js dev-mode type-generation cache entry, not a real compile error. Confirmed by clearing `.next` and re-running clean immediately after — same class of stale-artifact issue as the empty lock-files found during the earlier Full Disk Access outage, not a regression.

## Dashboard: headline split, real "Top 3" restored + honestly labeled exhaustive count (confirmed 2026-08-19)

Direction confirmed for the two items left open from the round above.

**#1 — headline split.** `headline` was two variables all along: `diagnosisHeadline` (audit-derived: "X priorities identified, Y AI opportunities flagged.") stays at the top of the page as before; the "Z requests in review" clause is gone from that sentence entirely and now renders as its own line directly under the "Your active requests" `<h2>`, contextually grounded next to the actual request cards instead of floating in a sentence about findings. The count itself is unchanged (module + session requests, still deliberately excluding the Execution Sprint per the previous round's fix — it already carries its own status/card and its own separate summary line).

**#2 — two visibly distinct cards, not one doing double duty.** `computeUrgentLabel()` (one function trying to describe both a curated pick and an exhaustive count) is replaced by two: `computeTop3Label()` for the reviewer's real curated selection (`top3WithIds`, from `reports.top_3_finding_ids` — "Top priority"/"Top 2 priorities"/"Top 3 priorities" depending on how many the reviewer genuinely picked, never overclaiming), and `computeSignalsLabel()` for the exhaustive critical/high count ("N critical signals" / "N high signals" / "N critical/high signals" for a mixed set — "signals," never "top," since this is a plain count above a severity threshold, not a ranked selection). Each gets its own `Card` with a one-line subtitle stating explicitly what it is and isn't ("Your reviewer's curated, ranked pick — not every urgent finding..." vs. "...an exhaustive count, not a curated pick."), and its own aggregated financial-exposure line computed over exactly what it lists (`top3FinancialExposure` vs. `urgentFinancialExposure` — previously one aggregate silently assumed the two sets were the same).

**A real, pre-existing data-integrity gap found live while restoring the Top 3 card, investigated and fixed at the read site, not silently worked around**: the first live check against Sally's real report showed "Top 3 priorities" heading a list of **5** items, not 3. Root-caused, not guessed: `reRankTop3()` ([src/lib/reviewer/workspace.ts](src/lib/reviewer/workspace.ts)) only validates `orderedFindingIds` is non-empty — nothing caps it at 3 — and this report's real `top_3_finding_ids` array genuinely holds 5 ids, almost certainly from this session's own extensive earlier reviewer-side testing. A second, independent bug compounded it: the old Dashboard code built `top3WithIds` by filtering the DB's own (arbitrary) query-result order by Set membership in `top3Ids`, so even a correctly-sized array wasn't guaranteed to render in the reviewer's actual rank order. Fixed at the Dashboard's read site (deliberately not touching `reRankTop3()`'s own validation — a separate, reviewer-workspace-side fix, flagged here rather than silently bundled in): map over `top_3_finding_ids` in its own stored order first, resolve each id against the visible findings, filter out anything not found (rejected/missing), *then* slice to the first 3 — restoring both genuine rank order and the honest 3-item cap. This flows through everywhere `top3WithIds`/`top3` is used, not just the new card — the action banner's "#1 priority" pick and the 30/60/90 roadmap derivation both now correctly reflect the reviewer's real rank-1 selection instead of arbitrary DB row order.

**Verified live, real browser session, Sally's real (data-anomalous) report — the exact case that surfaced the bug**: before the fix, "Top 3 priorities" showed 5 items with "Critical Customer Revenue Concentration" first (an artifact of DB query order) and the action banner's "highest priority" matched that same wrong pick. After the fix: "Top 3 priorities" correctly shows exactly 3 items — "Pricing Pressure from Competitor B" (HIGH), "Insufficient Information on CAC" (CRITICAL), "Critical Customer Revenue Concentration" (CRITICAL) — in the reviewer's real stored rank order, the action banner's "HIGHEST PRIORITY RIGHT NOW" correctly switched to match rank #1 ("Pricing Pressure from Competitor B"), and the 30-day roadmap bucket correctly dropped from 4 items to exactly the corrected top-3 (the 4th item, which had only ever appeared because the old array had 5 entries, correctly disappeared). The exhaustive "8 critical/high signals" card, unaffected by any of this (it was never built from `top3WithIds`), continued to correctly list all 8 findings with severity/impact throughout. The headline split and the request-count relocation were also confirmed in the same screenshots: "8 priorities identified, 2 AI opportunities flagged." with no blended request count, and "2 requests in review." sitting directly under "Your active requests."

**One real, unrelated environment hiccup during this verification, not an app bug**: a `navigate` call timed out at 300s while the dev server logs showed the same `GET /dashboard` request had genuinely taken 7.5 minutes to complete server-side — confirmed as CPU resource contention from the `npm run build`/`tsc` runs executed moments earlier on the same machine, not a hang in the new code: an immediate retry once those processes had finished completed normally in 4.4s, and the server logs showed no additional stuck requests afterward.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, re-checked after clearing `.next`. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route deleted afterward, never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup. No test data was mutated (verification was read-only against Sally's real, kept report).

Committed and pushed (commit `f672c7b`), per explicit go-ahead — flagged by the founder as the more important fix of the two, since a wrong #1 priority/roadmap could have reached a real client.

## Eight-item external-feedback batch (confirmed 2026-08-20)

Direct founder batch from external feedback review, worked through individually per explicit instruction — items 2, 3, 5, and 7 investigated/confirmed before any code changes; pricing/checkout items deliberately excluded, kept separate.

**1. "get 100 cusomers" typo — real, found in real test/demo data, not code.** Grepped the whole repo first (zero hits, confirming it wasn't a code/copy bug) before searching the DB directly. Found in `goals.desired_future_state_primary` for Sally's real account (goal `ccc91d6c-...`) — a genuinely client-authored free-text field ("what would good look like here?", spec §1.9a). Fixed by correcting the stored value to "get 100 customers" via a direct, one-time DB update — this is test-data cleanup, not a code change.

**2. Dashboard vs. report page #1 divergence — confirmed real, root-caused, fixed at the shared source, not per-page.** Investigated before building anything: the Dashboard's action banner (`topPriority = top3WithIds[0]`) and its Top 3 card already read the exact same variable — no divergence possible there by construction. But the client Report page (and `/demo-live`, which mirrors it) had an independent copy of the exact same bug just found and fixed on the Dashboard the same day: filtering `visibleFindings` by Set membership in `top_3_finding_ids`, which discarded the reviewer's real rank order (arbitrary DB-query order instead) and was never capped at 3. Not an intentional design difference — nothing in this codebase's history ever discussed the two pages ordering differently. Fixed by extracting one shared function, [`resolveTop3FindingsInOrder()`](src/lib/reports/top3.ts) (maps `top_3_finding_ids` in its own stored order first, then caps to 3), and wiring all three consumers (Dashboard, Report page, `/demo-live`) through it — they can't diverge again. **Verified live against the exact real report that surfaced the original bug** (Sally's, `617f930e-...`): Dashboard and Report page now both show "Pricing Pressure from Competitor B" as #1, exactly 3 items in the same rank order, and the same 30/60/90 roadmap bucketing on both pages.

**3. Dashboard subtitle — confirmed static, built as dynamic, 5 states, exact copy.** Read the code first: a single hardcoded string regardless of journey stage, confirmed via `grep` before building. Built as a real state machine reading `journeyStatus.stage` (already-existing shared signal) plus two new small checks: `latestReport`'s own presence (already queried at the top of the function, `status = 'sent'` only) reused as the "does a prior delivered report already exist" signal for the Awaiting-review vs. Re-audit-submitted split — no second query needed, since a `reports` row for the CURRENT submission genuinely doesn't exist yet during any pre-report stage; and a new targeted query (`execution_sprints` where `report_id = latestReport.id and status = 'complete'`) for Post-sprint. **Real, disclosed design decision**: Post-sprint was not specified as coexisting with or overriding "Report ready" in the founder's exact-copy list — implemented as a full override (once true, replaces "Report ready" entirely) since "here's your progress" is the more relevant framing once a client has already engaged with implementation on that report. Line 2 ("£X–£Y... across N findings") reuses `aggregateFinancialImpact()`/`isUsableFinancialImpact()` exactly as specified, scoped to ALL visible report findings (not just critical/high) since the founder's copy reads as a whole-report statement; "N" is `quantifiedCount` (the number of findings actually backing the cited figure), not the total finding count. Company-name fallback (a same-day follow-up direction) implemented: `company.name?.trim() || null` gates between the two copy variants. **Verified live against 3 of the 5 states with real accounts**: Pre-audit (Doc Upload Test Co, no evidence submitted — exact copy confirmed), Report ready with a quantified line 2 (Sally — "£11,500–£67,000... across 4 findings"), and Post-sprint (Riverbank Analytics Ltd, whose `complete` sprint is genuinely tied to its current delivered report — "Here's your progress since your last audit."). The Awaiting-review/Re-audit-submitted split shares one simple boolean branch already confirmed correct by direct code trace (`latestReport ? re-audit-copy : first-time-copy`) — not re-verified against a live account sitting in that exact transient pre-report state, since no real account in this session's data currently happens to be there (Nimbus Ledger Ltd, the one account with both prior `sent` reports and in-progress ones, resolved to `has_report` at verification time because its most-recently-submitted report by timestamp was already a sent one).

**4. No such banner built — confirmed nothing to remove either.** Grepped for any existing AI-in-production risk-figure banner before concluding — none exists anywhere in the app. Instruction acknowledged, nothing built, per explicit direction.

**5. Confirmed, both halves, before item 8 was built.** `hasLiveAiInProduction` is real, already captured in Evidence Intake's AI & Governance step ([EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx](<src/app/(app)/evidence-intake/EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx>)) — not surfaced anywhere earlier (onboarding, Business Profile). Confirmed by reading the `companies` table's own `create table` migration directly: no such column exists there or anywhere else on the company record — the value lives only inside the per-submission `evidence_payload`/`reports.source_evidence_snapshot` JSON blob (`aiGovernance.hasLiveAiInProduction`), never a queryable column. This is genuinely fine for item 8's specific need (one report at a time, already loaded into `evidenceSnapshot` on the report page) — but would block any future feature needing to cheaply query/filter/aggregate this signal across many companies (e.g., "show me every company with AI in production"), which would need a real schema change first. Flagged as a decision for the founder, not built.

**6. Services page copy — exact requested wording, both cards.** Concierge and Execution Sprint body copy replaced verbatim; confirmed no existing or added SLA/response-time commitment anywhere on the page (grepped before and after). **Verified live** — both cards render the exact requested copy.

**7. Confirmed intentional, no change.** Read [procurement-answers.ts](src/lib/modules/tender-readiness/procurement-answers.ts) directly — hard rule 2 explicitly instructs the model to say so honestly and set `confidenceLevel: "insufficient"` rather than inventing detail when evidence doesn't support a confident answer. A deliberate design choice, not an accident. Confirmation only, as requested.

**8. Built — fixed callout, gated on the report's own evidence snapshot.** Added to both the real client Report page and `/demo-live` (same section, kept consistent): `lens === "ai_governance" && evidenceSnapshot?.aiGovernance?.hasLiveAiInProduction` renders the exact requested copy via the shared `Alert` component (`variant="warning"`), directly under the AI & Governance section heading. Deliberately distinct from item 4 — this reads real, already-loaded report evidence, not a pre-review guess. **Verified live, both branches**: Sally's real report (AI in production confirmed) shows the callout verbatim, confirmed via `get_page_text`; Riverbank Analytics Ltd's `/demo-live` (AI in production not confirmed) correctly shows nothing.

## Item 3's remaining gap closed live + item 5 built: real, dedicated has_ai_in_production column, captured early (confirmed 2026-08-20)

Two follow-ups from the eight-item batch above.

**Item 3, closed properly, not left logically-correct-but-unverified.** Forced a real, disposable test account into each of the two remaining unverified transient states via temporary `pending_evidence_submissions` inserts (same non-destructive test-and-revert technique used throughout this codebase — confirmed neither company had an active submission first, to avoid clobbering real state): Doc Upload Test Co (no prior sent report) correctly showed "Your evidence is with your reviewer — you'll hear back within 48 hours."; Sally's real account (has a prior sent report) correctly showed "Your new evidence is with your reviewer — comparing against your previous audit." while the rest of the page correctly kept showing her last real delivered report's content underneath — exactly right, since no new report exists yet. Both temporary rows deleted afterward; confirmed via a fresh DB read that a genuine pre-existing `completed` row on Sally's account (her real original 2026-08-11 submission that produced report `617f930e-...`) was untouched by the delete. All 5 subtitle states are now proven live, not reasoned about.

**Item 5, built — complexity/risk reported first, per standing discipline**: one additive nullable column (near-zero risk), a well-precedented Business Profile field addition (low risk, mirrors `business_model`'s existing tri-state pattern exactly), and a moderate Evidence Intake sync pass (pre-fill + write-back, 3 files, all additive). **One real design call, flagged rather than guessed silently**: item 8's report callout was NOT switched to read the new live column — it keeps reading the report's own frozen `source_evidence_snapshot`, since a delivered report's callout should reflect what the audit itself found, not whatever the company's current profile says today (which could drift either direction after delivery and silently misrepresent a historical report). The new column instead feeds Evidence Intake's own checkbox as a real starting default and becomes the one place any future cross-company "which companies have AI in production" query should read from.

**Schema** ([supabase/migrations/20260820100000_companies_has_ai_in_production.sql](supabase/migrations/20260820100000_companies_has_ai_in_production.sql), pushed live) — `companies.has_ai_in_production boolean`, nullable ("not yet answered" is genuinely distinct from "answered no," same discipline as `kpi_unit`/`business_model`). **Backfill included**, not left for existing accounts to blindly show null: for every company with at least one report carrying a real `aiGovernance.hasLiveAiInProduction` value in its `source_evidence_snapshot`, the MOST RECENT such report's value seeds the new column — the exact same source item 8's callout already trusts, so the backfill can't introduce a value the app itself wouldn't already have surfaced. **Verified live**: real backfilled values matched already-known real data exactly (Sally: `true`, Riverbank Analytics Ltd: `false`) — confirmed correct, not assumed, since both were independently confirmed via the item-8 callout render earlier the same day. Companies whose historical report data lacks this specific sub-field (Nimbus Ledger Ltd, Nimbus Ledger Test Co) correctly stayed `null` — honest, not a bug, given real data-shape variance across this session's history.

**Business Profile** ([BusinessProfileForm.tsx](<src/app/(app)/business-profile/BusinessProfileForm.tsx>)/[actions.ts](<src/app/(app)/business-profile/actions.ts>)/[page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/business-profile/page.tsx>)) — new "Do you have AI in production today?" tri-state Select (Not set / Yes / No), wired through `updateCompanyProfile()`'s existing change-tracking into `company_profile_history`, identical pattern to every other profile field.

**Evidence Intake sync** ([EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx](<src/app/(app)/evidence-intake/EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx>)/[actions.ts](<src/app/(app)/evidence-intake/actions.ts>)/[page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/evidence-intake/page.tsx>)) — the existing checkbox now pre-fills from `companies.has_ai_in_production` when no saved draft already has its own value (a draft's own value still wins when present — an in-progress edit shouldn't be silently overwritten), with a hint line disclosing the two-way relationship ("Pre-filled from Business Profile — confirming or changing it here keeps that record current too"). `submitEvidence()` now also writes the checkbox's value back onto `companies.has_ai_in_production` on every real submission, keeping the living record current going forward — deliberately does NOT touch `reports.source_evidence_snapshot`'s own frozen copy.

**A real, unrelated tool-reliability finding surfaced during live verification, not an app bug**: toggling both checkboxes via a native-property-setter + synthetic `change`-event dispatch (the exact technique this codebase's own history already documented as reliable for `TagInput`) silently failed to update React's actual controlled-component state here — the DOM `checked` attribute read `true` immediately after, but a subsequent re-render reset it back to React's real (unchanged) state. Confirmed by then using a genuine `.click()` call instead, which worked correctly and stayed stable across re-renders. Not investigated further (a testing-technique detail, not a product bug) — flagged here since a future verification pass hitting the same silent-failure pattern on a plain checkbox should reach for `.click()` first, not assume the setter+dispatch trick generalizes from `TagInput` to every input type.

**Verified live end-to-end, real accounts, real UI, not scripted**:
