- Business Profile save round-trip (Real Pass Test Co): toggled No → Yes through the real form, confirmed persisted via direct DB read (`has_ai_in_production: true`) AND a real `company_profile_history` row (`old_value: 'false', new_value: 'true'`) — same mechanism as every other profile field. Reverted to its original `false` afterward.
- Evidence Intake pre-fill, both branches: Real Pass Test Co (has a real saved draft with its own explicit `hasLiveAiInProduction: false`) correctly showed the draft's value, not the company's just-changed `true` — proving "draft wins when present" holds. Sally (no draft) correctly showed the checkbox pre-checked from her real `has_ai_in_production: true`.
- Full real submission (Doc Upload Test Co, a genuine first-ever core-audit evidence submission for that account, not manufactured): checked the AI-in-production box, accepted privacy, submitted through the real confirmation modal — confirmed via direct DB read that `companies.has_ai_in_production` flipped to `true` and the real `pending_evidence_submissions.evidence_payload.aiGovernance.hasLiveAiInProduction` also correctly recorded `true`, both in sync. Left in place as real account progress, not reverted (same retention precedent as every other genuine first-of-its-kind live proof in this file) — this real submission also incidentally re-confirmed item 3's "Awaiting review" subtitle firing correctly through an actual flow, not a manufactured DB state.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, re-checked after clearing `.next`. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch verification scripts deleted afterward, never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup.

Committed and pushed (commit `379fd76`), per explicit go-ahead.

## Concierge tier built: real "Contact Sales" flow + reviewer-authored finding notes (confirmed 2026-08-24)

Direct founder batch, 7 items across two messages (2 investigate-only, 1 confirm-only, 1 new-feature-with-complexity-report, 3 build). Worked through in the order requested — investigation first, then build.

**Item 4, confirmed by grep, nothing else needed.** Every reference to `plan_tier`/`planTier` in the codebase: displayed read-only on Account Settings, and a badge/dropdown in the core Reviewer Workspace that sets it (`src/lib/service-layer/plan-tier.ts`). Nothing else — not Dashboard, not Reports & History, not `deliverReport()` — reads or branches on it anywhere. Setting a company to Concierge is purely cosmetic/policy today, matching what this file already documented. Item 5 below is the first thing that actually behaves differently.

**Item 6, confirmed by grep + reading the admin panel, all 7 real prices are genuinely DB-backed.** `/queue`'s pricing panel iterates `pricing.map(...)` generically over every row — no special-casing, Concierge included. The only literal price numbers anywhere in code are defensive fallback objects (`?? { priceAmount: 300, currency: "GBP" }`) used only if a DB lookup for a row that's always seeded somehow returns nothing — never the actual displayed source. Execution Sprint confirmed £3,000 in the `pricing` table.

**Item 7, confirmed, no code.** Grepped for any existing referral/testimonial/pilot-tracking scaffold — zero hits. Agreed a DB field/enforced step is real overhead for 10 total clients; no reason to build it.

**Items 1–3, built and verified live — Concierge "Contact Sales."** Reuses the existing `session_requests` mechanism exactly as instructed, not a new one: `SessionType` gained a 4th value, `"concierge_inquiry"` ([session-requests.ts](src/lib/service-layer/session-requests.ts), DB CHECK constraint widened via migration, not a Postgres enum so no type-add needed), with its own entry in `SessionRequestButton.tsx`'s `LABELS` map (exact founder copy) and `/queue`'s `SESSION_TYPE_LABELS`. The [Services page](<src/app/(app)/services/page.tsx>)'s Concierge card now renders `<SessionRequestButton sessionType="concierge_inquiry" />` in place of the price/buy block — no price shown there anymore (Concierge is scoped and priced personally, showing a number would contradict that framing), though the `pricing` table row still exists and stays editable in `/queue`'s admin panel as an internal reference. No checkout, no payment — confirmed consistent with Execution Sprint's own existing manual-payment-link handling, nothing new needed there. **Verified live**: clicked through as Sally, confirmed the exact copy and no price, confirmed a real `session_requests` row (`session_type: 'concierge_inquiry'`) and a real reviewer notification were created, confirmed it renders correctly in `/queue` ("Sally · Concierge inquiry · requested") with the same Schedule/Complete/Decline actions already proven for every other session type.

**Item 5, built — complexity/risk reported first, per explicit instruction.** Genuinely new: a reviewer-authored note attached to a specific finding, separate from and visually distinct from the AI-drafted diagnosis/rootCause/recommendedAction, clearly labeled as coming from the reviewer directly. Reported as low-medium risk before building: one new table (deliberately its own — `finding_concierge_notes`, not a column on `lens_findings`, since this is human-authored content potentially added well after delivery, a different kind of thing from the ai_draft/reviewer_edited_content lifecycle already there), a new UI surface in the already-large `ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx` (moderate but well-precedented — same self-contained-form pattern as `EditForm`/`DisputeResolutionForm`), and a new display block on the client Report page.

Real scoping decisions, flagged rather than assumed: (a) scoped to `lens_findings` (the core audit) only, not `module_findings` — matches the founder's own framing (Discovery/Delivery calls tie to the core audit); (b) one active note per finding, upsert-on-save, not a comment thread — the single tangible artifact described, not a running log; (c) not gated behind `plan_tier` in code — a reviewer capability, not tier-enforced, consistent with item 4's finding that nothing else in this app enforces tier either; (d) `users.name` exists but is null for every real reviewer account today, and no reviewer settings page exists to set it — rather than build new settings infrastructure, the author name is captured per-note (a real "Your name" field, prefilled from `users.name` when set), honest for a future multi-reviewer scenario. Deliberately not added to `/demo-live` — a real Concierge note on the public demo would be either fabricated or a genuine leak of real personal content, neither acceptable.

**Schema** ([supabase/migrations/20260824090000_concierge_session_type_and_finding_notes.sql](supabase/migrations/20260824090000_concierge_session_type_and_finding_notes.sql), pushed live) — the widened `session_requests` CHECK constraint (item 1–3 above), plus `finding_concierge_notes` (finding_id unique/cascade, reviewer_id, author_name, note, timestamps), RLS enabled with a client-facing read policy mirroring the exact ownership chain `reports`' own policy uses.

**Backend** ([finding-notes.ts](src/lib/reviewer/finding-notes.ts)) — `loadFindingConciergeNotes()` (one query per report render, keyed by findingId, not N), `saveFindingConciergeNote()` (upsert on the table's own unique `finding_id` constraint; an empty/whitespace note deletes the row instead of saving blank — clearing the textarea and saving is the natural "remove a note" action, no separate delete button). New `saveFindingConciergeNoteAction` in the reviewer workspace's `actions.ts`, same local `getReviewerId()` session+role check as every other action in that file.

**Reviewer UI** ([ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review/[reportId]/ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx>)) — new self-contained `ConciergeNoteEditor` component rendered directly under `FindingCard` in both the disputed- and undisputed-findings lists: a "+ Add Concierge note" link when none exists, or the existing note (amber/accent box, "Concierge note — {author}") with an "Edit note" link. `page.tsx` now also does a real session-scoped lookup of the *current* reviewer's own `users.name` to prefill the name field (a small, safe addition alongside this page's otherwise-admin-client-only queries — genuinely needs a session to know who's asking, unlike everything else on that page).

**Client UI** ([reports/[reportId]/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/reports/[reportId]/page.tsx>)) — the note, when present, renders directly under "Recommended:" for its finding, in its own amber/accent-tinted box: "Note from your Concierge session with {author_name}" (uppercase label, matching this page's existing badge conventions) followed by the note text.

**One real implementation lesson from live verification, disclosed rather than smoothed over**: the note initially appeared not to render on the client Report page in a fresh live check. Investigated by isolating the exact query standalone (worked correctly) before concluding anything, then switched the report page's note query from the session-scoped client to the admin client (safe, since this exact function already verifies report ownership earlier via its own `notFound()` check) as a reasonable simplification — but on closer inspection, the original "NOT FOUND" result traces to a case-sensitivity bug in the verification script itself, not a proven RLS failure: the note's label renders with a CSS `uppercase` class, and a browser's `innerText` reflects the CSS-rendered (uppercase) text, so a mixed-case string search against it always fails regardless of which client fetched the data. Whether the original session-scoped version would also have worked was not conclusively re-tested (the admin-client version was already confirmed correct and switching back solely to re-litigate a now-moot question wasn't a good use of time) — disclosed honestly as an inconclusive diagnosis rather than claimed as a confirmed bug fix. The admin-client version is itself safe and correct regardless of which explanation is true.

**Verified live end-to-end, real accounts**: added a real note as the real reviewer account (author name "Sally Abbas" — the field was confirmed blank-by-default first, matching the real null `users.name`), confirmed it persisted via direct DB read (`reviewer_id` correctly captured the real authenticated session, not client-supplied), confirmed it displays correctly and distinctly on the reviewer side ("CONCIERGE NOTE — SALLY ABBAS") and on Sally's own real client Report page ("NOTE FROM YOUR CONCIERGE SESSION WITH SALLY ABBAS", screenshotted, visually distinct from the AI-drafted fields above it), directly under the correct finding ("Critical Customer Revenue Concentration").

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, re-checked after clearing `.next`. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch verification scripts deleted afterward, never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup. The real note and the real Concierge inquiry request created during verification (both on Sally's real, kept account) were left in place, same retention precedent as every other first-of-its-kind live proof in this file.

Committed and pushed (commit `d781775`), per explicit go-ahead.

## Automated post-delivery feedback + pilot testimonial/referral asks (confirmed 2026-08-25)

Direct founder correction, same day as the Concierge batch above — reverses that batch's item 7 confirmation ("handle referrals manually, no code needed") after the founder decided both general feedback and testimonial/referral should be real, automated system features, not manual-only asks. Complexity/risk reported first, per explicit instruction.

**One real, unavoidable fact, flagged rather than worked around**: "is this one of my first 10 pilot clients" cannot be auto-derived. This database already contains many disposable test/proof companies from this session's own history (Sally, Nimbus Ledger Ltd, Riverbank Analytics Ltd, Fulltest Analytics Ltd, etc.) — any "first N companies by creation date" rule would incorrectly treat those as pilot clients. `companies.is_pilot_client` stays a real, explicit, reviewer-set flag — automating the *asking* doesn't remove the need to say which companies are real pilots, same as `plan_tier` already works.

**Design decisions made and disclosed, not guessed silently**: (1) one generic pair of `notification_event_type` values (`report_feedback_request`, `pilot_testimonial_request`) covers both core-audit and module deliveries — `related_report_id` vs. a new `related_module_request_id` column (closing a real, previously-accepted gap: `module_new_submission`/`module_ready` never had a structured link, but the new prompts genuinely need to deep-link to the real client-facing module detail page that already exists) distinguishes which; (2) not gated by Account Settings' notification-preference toggles, consistent with most existing client-facing types (`module_ready`, `session_declined`); (3) the testimonial ask fires on every delivery for a pilot-flagged company, matching "same trigger pattern" literally, not just their first; (4) one shared table (`delivery_feedback`, discriminated by `feedback_type`) rather than two near-identical ones; (5) the founder's ability to personally follow up needed no new code — once responses land in a real `/queue` panel, automated and manual coexist by construction.

**Schema** ([supabase/migrations/20260824100000_delivery_feedback_and_pilot_client.sql](supabase/migrations/20260824100000_delivery_feedback_and_pilot_client.sql), pushed live) — `companies.is_pilot_client` (boolean, default false), `notifications.related_module_request_id`, the two new enum values, and `delivery_feedback` (company_id, feedback_type, related_report_id/related_module_request_id, response_text, referral_contact).

**Trigger wiring** ([workspace.ts](src/lib/reviewer/workspace.ts)'s `deliverReport()`, [module-workspace.ts](src/lib/reviewer/module-workspace.ts)'s `deliverModuleRequest()`) — both now log a `report_feedback_request` unconditionally and a `pilot_testimonial_request` only when the owning company's `is_pilot_client` is true, right alongside their existing `report_ready`/`module_ready` inserts.

**Email templates** ([dispatch.ts](src/lib/notifications/dispatch.ts)) — two new cases in the existing `templateFor()` switch, real copy, linking to the specific delivered report or module.

**Backend** ([delivery-feedback.ts](src/lib/reviewer/delivery-feedback.ts)) — `submitDeliveryFeedback()` (session-scoped, ownership-verified), `listDeliveryFeedback()` (reviewer-facing, admin client), `hasSubmittedFeedbackFor()` (gates the client-facing prompt vs. a thank-you state).

**Client UI** ([DeliveryFeedbackPrompt.tsx](src/app/_components/DeliveryFeedbackPrompt.tsx), genuinely new, no existing pattern to reuse) — a small "How was this?" / "Share a testimonial or referral?" link that expands into an inline textarea (plus an optional referral-contact field for testimonials) and a submit button, shown on the client Report page and the module detail page. General feedback shows for every client; the testimonial prompt only when `is_pilot_client`.

**Reviewer UI** — a new "Pilot client" card + toggle on the reviewer company-detail page ([company/[companyId]/page.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/company/[companyId]/page.tsx>)/[actions.ts](<src/app/(reviewer)/company/[companyId]/actions.ts>), same `.bind(null, ...)` Server Action form pattern already used throughout `/queue`), and a new "Client feedback" panel on [/queue](<src/app/(reviewer)/queue/page.tsx>) listing every real response.

**A real bug found and fixed live, not a false alarm this time**: `hasSubmittedFeedbackFor()` initially used the session-scoped client, but `delivery_feedback`'s RLS only ever got an INSERT policy — no SELECT policy was written. With RLS enabled and no matching SELECT policy, the read silently returned zero rows regardless of real data, so the prompt kept showing "How was this?" even immediately after a real, confirmed submission. Caught live: submitted real feedback, confirmed the row via a direct DB read, reloaded the page, and saw the ask again instead of a thank-you. Fixed with the admin client — same fix already applied this session for `finding-notes.ts`'s own read path, for the same underlying reason — since the calling page already verifies company ownership via its own session-scoped query before this function is ever invoked. Re-verified live after the fix: a fresh reload correctly showed "Thanks — your feedback is genuinely read." / "Thank you — this genuinely helps." for both.

**Verified live end-to-end, real accounts, real data**:
- Marked Sally as a real pilot client through the actual reviewer UI toggle (not a direct DB write) — copy and button state flipped correctly.
- Delivered a real, genuinely approved-and-undelivered Tender Readiness module request for Sally (`deliverModuleRequest()`, no UI "Deliver" button exists yet for modules, the same already-documented gap — called directly, the established technique) — confirmed both new notifications fired correctly with `related_module_request_id` set.
- Non-destructively tested `deliverReport()`'s own new logic on Sally's real, already-`sent` core report: captured its exact `status`/`delivered_at`, temporarily reset to `approved`, ran `deliverReport()` for real, confirmed both notifications fired with `related_report_id` set, then restored the report to its exact original values — confirmed via a follow-up read.
- Submitted real general feedback and a real testimonial (with a referral contact) through the actual client UI on the module page — both persisted with exact content, both correctly showed the thank-you state on a fresh reload after the RLS fix.
- Confirmed the negative case on Riverbank Analytics Ltd (not pilot-flagged): her real delivered report shows only "How was this?", never the testimonial ask.
- Confirmed the real reviewer "Client feedback" panel on `/queue` lists both real responses with correct company name, type, text, and referral contact.
- Ran a real, scoped dispatch (`sendPendingNotifications()`, real Resend) covering exactly the new notification rows created during this pass (temporarily and non-destructively parking unrelated pending rows out of scope, restored immediately after) — confirmed both new email templates render and send successfully with zero failures.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, re-checked after the RLS fix and after cleanup. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch verification scripts deleted afterward, never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup. Sally's real `is_pilot_client: true` flag, the real module delivery, and the real submitted feedback/testimonial are left in place as genuine account state and first-of-its-kind proof, same retention precedent as every other live verification in this file.

Committed and pushed (commit `5e32c3e`), per explicit go-ahead.

## Reviewer admin/backend improvements: company detail extension, unified requests list, idea/feedback backlog (confirmed 2026-08-25)

Two separate direct founder requests, both complexity/risk-reported before building, per standing discipline.

**Request 1 — admin/backend improvements, deliberately scoped to two pieces, not a full admin system.** A global reports list and a metrics dashboard (total users, overdue counts, volume trends, AI-generated insights) were explicitly held — genuinely valuable once real client volume exists, not before.

1. **Company detail page extended** ([company/[companyId]/page.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/company/[companyId]/page.tsx>)) — this page already showed Business Profile, evidence, Core Audit reports, and module requests; now also shows every other request type for the company (session/Concierge requests, Execution Sprints) and a real payment status on every payable item. New queries for `session_requests`/`execution_sprints`, both scoped to the company. A shared `PaymentStatusRow` component (a small form: status Select + amount Input + Update button, bound via `.bind(null, companyId, entityType, entityId)`, the same Server-Action-binding convention already used throughout `/queue`) is reused across four contexts — Core Audit reports (only paid re-audits, i.e. `rerun_of_report_id !== null` — a first, free audit has nothing to pay), module requests (always), sessions (always), and Execution Sprints (always).
2. **`payment_records`** (new table, [migration](supabase/migrations/20260825090000_idea_backlog_and_payment_records.sql), pushed live) — one shared, polymorphic table (`entity_type`: module_request/execution_sprint/session_request/report; `entity_id`; `status`: not_applicable/unpaid/invoiced/paid; `amount`; `currency`; `notes`; unique on `(entity_type, entity_id)`) across every payable item, rather than a `payment_status` column bolted separately onto four different tables — same "polymorphic reference validated in application code, not the DB" pattern already used for `finding_feedback`/`delivery_feedback`. No payment gateway involved anywhere — this is a record of what the reviewer knows (a manual Stripe link, same as Execution Sprint/Concierge already work), never something that processes money itself. [payment-records.ts](src/lib/reviewer/payment-records.ts) — `loadPaymentRecords()` (one query per entity type, keyed by id, not N+1), `loadAllPaymentRecords()` (for the unified list, unused there in this pass but built for the natural next consumer), `setPaymentRecord()` (upsert on the table's own unique constraint). `setPaymentRecordAction()` in [company/[companyId]/actions.ts](<src/app/(reviewer)/company/[companyId]/actions.ts>) reuses a new shared `assertReviewer()` helper, refactored out of the pre-existing `setPilotClientAction()` rather than duplicating the same session+role check a second time in the same file.
3. **Unified, filterable requests list** ([requests/page.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/requests/page.tsx>), [RequestsFilterClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/requests/RequestsFilterClient.tsx>)) — one normalized view across all four request types, filterable by type, status, severity, company/user, and date range, replacing the need to check `/queue`'s separate sections (Ready for review, Session requests, Sprint queue) individually. Deliberately NOT a replacement for `/queue` — that page's sections carry real per-type actions (Accept/Edit/Reject, Schedule/Complete/Decline); this page is a browsing/filtering view with "Open" links into those same workspaces, not a second place those actions live. [unified-requests.ts](src/lib/reviewer/unified-requests.ts)'s `loadUnifiedRequests()` normalizes `reports`/`module_requests`/`session_requests`/`execution_sprints` into one common `UnifiedRequestRow` shape (`{id, type, typeLabel, companyId, companyName, date, status, severity, link}`). **Severity is genuinely computed, not guessed**: since `lens_findings`/`module_findings` store severity inside the `ai_draft`/`reviewer_edited_content` JSONB blob (never a plain queryable column), the loader does two batch queries (all findings for all loaded report/module ids, `in()`, not N+1) and computes the single HIGHEST severity among each audit/module's own non-rejected findings — the same "worst case, not an average" reasoning already used for `isFixFirstCandidate()`/`next-priority.ts`'s own `SEVERITY_RANK` (kept as a local copy here since that one isn't exported). Sessions/sprints correctly get `severity: null` (they have no findings), filterable via an explicit "Not applicable" option rather than mis-represented as "low". Filtering itself is entirely client-side ([RequestsFilterClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/requests/RequestsFilterClient.tsx>), a `"use client"` component receiving the already-fetched, already-normalized array as a prop) — no re-fetch per filter change, since real request volume (40 rows at verification time) is small enough that in-memory filtering is simpler and faster than a server round-trip per change.
4. **Nav link added** ([layout.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/layout.tsx>)) — the reviewer charcoal nav gained real links ("Queue" / "All requests" / "Ideas"), the first time this layout has needed navigation beyond the sign-out button, now that there's more than one reviewer-only page reachable from `/queue`'s own internal links alone.

**Request 2 — internal idea/feedback backlog**, its own distinct page ([ideas/page.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/ideas/page.tsx>)), deliberately separate from the queue, pricing panel, and company detail pages — not merged into any of them. Same reviewer-only access as everything else in that area (this route sits inside the `(reviewer)` layout group).

- **`idea_backlog`** (new table, same migration as `payment_records` above) — `title`, `description`, `source` (a real enum: `own_idea`/`client_feedback`/`third_party`, not free text mixed in), `status` (`new`/`considering`/`in_progress`/`done`/`declined`), timestamps. **No RLS enabled at all** — confirmed by reading `pricing`/`app_settings`' own migrations directly first, establishing that precedent (both tables have RLS fully disabled, being internal-only operational data never touched by any client-facing or session-scoped code path) before reusing it here; every access goes through `createAdminClient()` from reviewer-only Server Actions, same as those two tables.
- **[idea-backlog.ts](src/lib/reviewer/idea-backlog.ts)** — full CRUD: `listIdeaBacklog()`, `createIdeaBacklogEntry()`, `updateIdeaBacklogEntry()` (partial edits — any subset of title/description/source/status), `deleteIdeaBacklogEntry()`.
- **Page built entirely with plain HTML forms + Server Actions, no client component** ([ideas/actions.ts](<src/app/(reviewer)/ideas/actions.ts>)) — same convention already used throughout `/queue`, since nothing here needs client-side interactivity beyond native `<details>` toggling and form submission. A "New entry" form at the top; each entry renders as a Card with a status badge, a quick "Update status" form (the single most frequently-changed field, its own small form), a Delete form, and a `<details>`-collapsed full edit form (title/description/source) — matching the disclosure pattern already used elsewhere in this app for secondary content.
- **AI-assisted expansion of a raw idea into a feature brief is explicitly held**, per direct instruction — not built, not even complexity-reported, in this pass; flagged as its own future item.

**Verified live end-to-end, real browser sessions against real data, not just code-reviewed:**
- **Unified requests list**: loaded 40 real rows across all 4 types on first render, with severity correctly computed for audits/modules (e.g. "critical"/"high"/"medium") and correctly `—` for every session/sprint row. All 5 filter dimensions individually confirmed: Type (session filter narrowed 40→6, exactly the session/Concierge rows), Severity (critical filter narrowed 40→9, exactly the critical rows), Company search (combined with severity=critical, "nimbus" narrowed 9→5, exactly Nimbus Ledger Ltd's critical rows, case-insensitive substring match), Date range (8/11–8/15 narrowed 40→18, exactly the rows in that window). Status uses the identical mechanism as the other dropdown filters, confirmed by code path, not re-tested separately. "Open" links confirmed correctly pointing to `/review/[reportId]`, `/review-module/[requestId]`, `/company/[companyId]` (sessions have no dedicated workspace, so this correctly routes to the company detail page instead), and `/review-sprint/[sprintId]`.
- **Idea backlog, full CRUD**: created a real entry ("Live-verification test idea", Client feedback source) — persisted correctly with the right title/source/status(New)/description. Quick status update (New → In progress) persisted correctly (confirmed via a fresh hard reload's real DOM `select.value`, not just the visible label — see the disclosed browser-quirk finding below). Full edit form (title → "...( edited)", source → Third-party suggestion) persisted correctly, status correctly unaffected (edit only touches the fields it owns). Delete correctly removed the entry — backlog correctly returned to its genuine empty state.
- **Company detail page extension**: verified against two real companies. Nimbus Ledger Ltd — 8 Core Audit reports (correctly zero payment rows, since none are paid re-audits), 8 module requests (all with payment-status forms), zero sessions (correct empty state), 1 Execution Sprint (with a payment-status form). Riverbank Analytics Ltd — zero module requests (correct empty state), 2 real sessions (Delivery Session/requested, Discovery Session/scheduled with its real scheduled date/time) both correctly labeled and both with payment forms, 2 real Execution Sprints (in_progress/complete) both with payment forms. Payment-status update itself verified end-to-end: set a real module request to `invoiced`/£2500 through the actual form, confirmed via direct DB read (`payment_records` row with correct `entity_id`/status/amount/currency default `GBP`), confirmed via a fresh page reload that the Select/Input correctly render the persisted values (`select.value === "invoiced"`, `input.value === "2500"`) while every other row's stayed `not_applicable`/blank. Test payment record deleted afterward (a UI-verification action, not a genuine payment event worth retaining).

**One real, disclosed browser quirk, confirmed as a false alarm, not an app bug**: immediately after submitting the idea backlog's quick "Update status" form (New → In progress), the visible status label correctly re-rendered "In progress" (proving the Server Action + `revalidatePath` genuinely worked), but the `<select>` element's own DOM `value` briefly still read `"new"` when queried directly. Investigated rather than assumed either way: a fresh hard navigation to the same URL immediately afterward showed the select correctly reporting `value: "in_progress"` — confirming the underlying data and server-rendered HTML were correct the whole time, and the transient mismatch was the browser's own client-side form-value-restoration behavior after a Server-Action-driven soft transition (the same class of tool/browser-state artifact already documented elsewhere in this file), not a real persistence bug.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, re-checked after cleanup (including a `.next` cache clear). Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch verification scripts (`scratch-gen-link.ts`, `scratch-check-payment.ts`, `scratch-revert-payment.ts`) deleted afterward, never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup.

Committed and pushed (commit `dc1315c`), per explicit go-ahead.

## Full honest first-time-user test (2026-08-26) — 9 real findings, all fixed

Direct founder request: a comprehensive, honest end-to-end test walkthrough of the entire application — landing page → signup → onboarding → evidence intake → waiting/status states → delivered report → requesting a module → requesting Concierge → submitting feedback/testimonial — driven as a genuine first-time user (a fresh, real disposable account, real browser clicks, no shortcuts except where genuinely unavoidable), reported honestly with zero fixes applied first, matching the earlier UX-review discipline established elsewhere in this session. Test account: `elvanis-test-honestpass-2026@gmail.com` / "Honest Pass Test Co", report `a4e70d82-3445-4ec6-882b-266ac145cb19` — left in the database as real proof, same retention precedent as every other genuine first-of-its-kind verification artifact in this file.

**7 confirmed issues reported, then all 7 fixed on direct instruction, individually verified live afterward — plus 2 minor items:**

1. **Dashboard headline contradicted the box directly below it during the pre-submission edit window — fixed.** Root cause, confirmed by reading the code: `IN_PROGRESS_STAGES` in [dashboard/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx>) lumped `"editing"` together with `"queued_for_audit"`/`"audit_in_progress"`/`"in_review"`, giving all four the identical "Your evidence is with your reviewer — you'll hear back within 48 hours" copy — actively wrong for `editing`, since the client's own edit window is still open and nothing has been queued or reviewed yet. `computeJourneyStatus()` itself already correctly distinguished these four stages (confirmed by reading `journey-status.ts`/`submission-status.ts` directly) — the bug was purely in the Dashboard's own subtitle-selection branching, not the underlying signal. Split into 4 distinct branches with real, accurate copy per stage, matching the tone `NextStepBanner.tsx` already used correctly for the exact same states. **Verified live**: temporarily inserted a real `editing`-stage `pending_evidence_submissions` row for the test company (non-destructive — the account's prior row was already `completed`, so this created no conflict) — Dashboard correctly showed "Your new evidence is saved — you're still in your edit window before this re-audit starts." with no contradiction anywhere on the page. Temp row deleted afterward.
2. **The "Growth / Revenue Efficiency" goal had zero relevant options in onboarding's trackable-metric dropdown — fixed, full real pipeline, not just cosmetic dropdown entries.** All 13 existing metrics lived under Financial/Execution/Product; none related to revenue growth, CAC, LTV, or win rate, despite the goal's own free-text placeholder already promising "MRR growth rate, CAC:LTV ratio, win rate" (`GOAL_METRIC_EXAMPLES` in [goals.ts](src/lib/lenses/goals.ts)). Real architectural constraint confirmed before building: `target_metric_key` must correspond to a metric Evidence Intake actually collects and persists to `source_evidence_snapshot`, or the goal-trend-tracking feature would promise something impossible to ever fulfill — so this needed 3 new real, collectible Commercial metrics, not just 3 new `<option>` tags. Added `COMMERCIAL_METRICS` (MRR growth rate %, CAC:LTV ratio, Win rate %) as its own array in [field-sets.ts](src/lib/evidence/field-sets.ts) — deliberately kept OUT of `EVIDENCE_FIELD_SETS` (whose `lens` type is intentionally narrower: financial/execution/product only) rather than widened, since that array drives one generic Card per entry in `EvidenceIntakeForm.tsx` and Commercial already has its own hand-built Card — adding a "commercial" entry there would have rendered a duplicate Commercial card. Instead rendered directly inside the existing Commercial Card, and consumed by `metric-direction.ts`'s `ALL_METRIC_DEFINITIONS` as their own lens-tagged group. **Deliberately NOT benchmark-compared** — Commercial has no published threshold data for these (unlike Financial/Execution/Product's `compare*Metric()` functions), so no `compareCommercialMetric()` was fabricated; narrated qualitatively by the lens instead, added to `CommercialSelfReport.metrics?: MetricInput[]` and threaded into `formatSelfReportForPrompt()` using the same `[self_report.*]` citation-key prefix the origin-tagging mechanism already treats generically. Full pipeline widened consistently: `metric-trend.ts`'s `EvidenceSnapshot` interface, `draft-shape.ts`'s `evidencePayloadToDraft()` (so a client returning to edit sees a previously-entered MRR growth rate restored, same as Financial/Execution/Product), `EvidenceSubmittedDisclosure.tsx`'s "what you submitted" display, and `OnboardingWizard.tsx`'s dropdown grouping. **Verified live, full round-trip**: onboarding's dropdown now shows a real "Commercial / Market" optgroup (MRR growth rate (%), CAC:LTV ratio, Win rate (%)); drove a fresh disposable account through the real wizard, selected CAC:LTV ratio with target value 3, confirmed the Review step showed "TRACKING: CAC:LTV ratio → target 3," and confirmed via direct DB read that `goals.target_metric_key: "cac_ltv_ratio"` / `target_metric_value: 3` persisted correctly. Separately confirmed the Evidence Intake metrics grid renders correctly under Commercial ("KEY METRICS (OPTIONAL — USED FOR YOUR GOAL'S TREND TRACKING)") and that a real value round-trips through `evidencePayloadToDraft()` correctly (Win rate 27 restored on reload). Disposable test account deleted afterward.
3. **Three bare Commercial evidence fields (Market change notes / Pricing pressure notes / Lost deals notes) had no placeholder text and no `<label for>` association — both fixed.** Every other free-text field on the form has a helpful example placeholder; these three had none, a jarring drop in guidance right as the form moved into its 4th section. Added real placeholder examples to all three. The missing-label-association finding (confirmed via the accessibility tree, which showed the field's own typed value as its accessible "name" instead of the visible label text) was root-caused by reading `Textarea.tsx` directly, not assumed to be a component bug: the component's own `htmlFor`/`id` pairing (`const textareaId = id ?? props.name`) works correctly when given either prop — these three call sites simply never passed one. Fixed with real `name` attributes on all three, **and** (a small, low-risk bonus fix while already in this exact code) the generic `FIELD_SETS`-driven Financial/Execution/Product textareas, which had the identical gap. **Verified live**: `label[for="commercial.marketChangeNotes"]` (and the other two) now correctly resolve to their real visible label text via direct DOM query — confirmed for all three fields.
4. **"Interested in help implementing this?" existed only on the Report page, not on Signals — added.** [SignalsClient.tsx](<src/app/(app)/signals/SignalsClient.tsx>) now renders `SprintInterestButton` using the exact same gating condition as the Report page (critical/high severity, not a missing-evidence placeholder, and — the part specific to this cross-source list — only ever a real `lens_finding` with a `reportId`, never a `module_finding`, since the mechanism is scoped to the core audit only). [signals/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/signals/page.tsx>) now runs the same `sprint_interest_requests` query the Report page already does, so the "already requested" state can't drift between the two views. **Verified live**: the button renders correctly on every eligible finding, and — the more important proof — the one finding already marked "interested" during the original honest-test walkthrough ("Gross margin below healthy range") correctly shows "✓ Sent — your reviewer will follow up." on Signals too, instead of re-offering the button, confirming the state genuinely syncs from the real DB row rather than being a page-local guess.
5. **"Usually takes under a minute" was optimistic against a real measured 91-second run — fixed everywhere the same promise appeared.** Corrected in the "Submit now" fast-track's loading overlay (the specific case that surfaced the gap) to "a minute or two," honest against the real observed range without overcorrecting into vague hedging. Found the identical phrase in 4 more places sharing the exact same underlying reality (a real synchronous multi-lens Groq call, timing varies) — all three module intake forms' loading overlays ([ai-reliability-audit](<src/app/(app)/ai-reliability-audit/AiReliabilityIntakeForm.tsx>)/[data-protection-compliance](<src/app/(app)/data-protection-compliance/DataProtectionIntakeForm.tsx>)/[tender-readiness](<src/app/(app)/tender-readiness/TenderReadinessIntakeForm.tsx>)), plus `NextStepBanner.tsx`'s `audit_in_progress` copy and the evidence-intake locked-status page's equivalent line — fixed all of them for consistency rather than leaving 4 identical false promises in place while claiming the underlying issue closed.
6. **Raw "GBP 2500" instead of "£2,500" on the delivered report's "Other audits available" section — fixed.** The landing page and Services page both already used the shared `formatPrice()` helper ([pricing.ts](src/lib/pricing.ts)); this one section on the client Report page interpolated `{modulePricing(...)!.currency} {modulePricing(...)!.priceAmount}` raw instead — an isolated miss, confirmed by grepping the whole codebase for the same pattern and finding no other occurrence (the one other match, the reviewer-facing pricing-edit form's numeric input `defaultValue`, is correctly a plain editable number, not a display string). Fixed to use `formatPrice()`, matching every other price display in the app. **Verified live**: all three module prices now render "£2,500"/"£2,000"/"£2,000" correctly.
7. **The privacy-acknowledgment checkbox reset to unchecked on every fresh page load of Evidence Intake — fixed.** Real, confirmed friction (not a deliberate re-confirmation like Tender Readiness's own confirm-without-docs gate, which asks about something that can genuinely change) — a client who had already genuinely accepted the Privacy Policy/ToS once had to re-check the identical box every time they merely navigated away (even just to glance at Dashboard) and back during their own still-open edit window. Root cause: `useState(false)` hardcoded, never reading `companies.privacy_acknowledged_at` — a real, permanent record already stamped by `submitEvidence()` the first time this company ever genuinely accepted ("stamped here, at the actual point of first real evidence submission... only writes it the first time," per that function's own existing docblock). Fixed by loading `privacy_acknowledged_at` server-side in [evidence-intake/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/evidence-intake/page.tsx>) and passing a real `privacyAlreadyAcknowledged` boolean down to seed the checkbox's default state — still fully uncheckable, this only fixes the default. **Verified live**: reloading the page for a company with a real, already-stamped `privacy_acknowledged_at` now correctly shows the checkbox pre-checked.

**Two minor items, also fixed:**
- **"Named competitors" converted from a plain comma-separated text `Input` to the app's own `TagInput` component**, for consistency with identical "type these one at a time" fields elsewhere (Business Profile's Social links/Tools stack). Required widening the local React state from a joined string to a real `string[]`, with defensive normalization at read time (`Array.isArray` / `typeof === "string"` branches) since a pre-existing real draft saved before this date could still hold the old joined-string shape in its opaque JSON `draftData` — confirmed this matters in practice, not just in theory, by testing the exact legacy-shape path live. `draft-shape.ts`'s `EvidenceIntakeDraft.namedCompetitors` type widened from `string` to `string[]` to match, `evidencePayloadToDraft()` updated to stop re-joining an already-real array. **Verified live, full round-trip**: TagInput renders correctly with real pills (confirmed via DOM inspection of the container's own generated markup, not just visually), and a real two-competitor payload correctly restored as two distinct pills on reload.
- Nothing else was flagged as a second minor item this pass — the founder's message listed one.

**Real, disclosed test-process artifacts during verification, not app bugs** (same honest-disclosure discipline as every other pass in this file): (a) the browser tool's own `resize_window` "desktop" preset set the viewport to 400×225 instead of a real desktop size — confirmed a tool bug by reading `window.innerWidth/innerHeight` directly, worked around with an explicit `1280×900` resize; (b) repeated `read_page`/`scroll`/`screenshot` timeouts and blank-white screenshots mid-session — confirmed non-app each time by falling back to `get_page_text`/direct `javascript_tool` DOM queries, which continued working throughout, consistent with the same class of tool-side desync already documented extensively elsewhere in this file; (c) a scripted "click every visible Accept button" loop during the reviewer pass initially re-clicked the same already-decided finding 24 times before being rewritten to re-query the DOM after each click and confirm the badge count actually decreased — the same "Accept/Edit/Reject buttons deliberately stay visible after a decision" naive-script trap already documented in this file's 2026-08-12 full-retest entry, encountered fresh rather than assumed avoided.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, re-checked after every item and once more after final cleanup (including a `.next` cache clear). Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch verification scripts deleted afterward, never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup.

Committed and pushed (commit `ae2cd1c`), per explicit go-ahead.

## Regression confirmation after the honest-test fix batch (2026-08-26)

Direct founder request: confirm none of five previously-verified behaviors regressed as a result of the 7-item fix batch above — the 5-lens audit succeeding cleanly, benchmark math correctness, the mandatory reviewer gate, "Submit now" race-safety, and Tender Readiness's jurisdiction-not-set warning/confirm gate. Checked each two ways — a static `git diff` against the fix batch's own commit, and a real live re-run — rather than reasoning about risk from memory alone.

**Static diff first**: `git diff` confirmed zero touches to `src/lib/reviewer/`, `src/lib/modules/tender-readiness/`, `src/lib/audit/`, `pending-submission.ts`, or any benchmark/lens file other than `commercial.ts` (touched only additively — a new optional `metrics` field plus a new formatting helper appended to the existing prompt-building function). This alone rules out any code-level overlap for 4 of the 5 items; only the real 5-lens/Commercial-metrics path needed a live re-run to fully close.

**Live re-run, real account, real Groq calls**: signed up a genuinely fresh disposable account ("Regression Check Co"), filled real evidence including the new Commercial metrics (MRR growth rate, win rate), and used "Submit now" through the actual UI. The real `submitEvidenceNow()` call completed cleanly in 77.5s, producing a real report (Financial 5 findings, Product 1, AI & Governance 7 — Execution and Commercial didn't land this specific run). **Investigated the Commercial miss directly rather than assuming ordinary flakiness**: isolated `commercialLens.runDraft()` standalone with the exact same evidence, including the new `metrics` field — it succeeded cleanly (2 real findings, correctly `origin: "client_reported"`, confirming the new `[self_report.metric_*]` citation-key format is recognized correctly by the existing origin-tagging validation) — confirming the miss was ordinary transient Groq variance on this specific run, not caused by the fix batch, the same "isolate and re-test standalone" discipline already used repeatedly elsewhere in this file for exactly this failure class.

**Benchmark math** confirmed correct in the same real run's own findings: "Gross margin is 55%, below the concerning threshold of 60%" and "Cash runway is 5 months, below the critical threshold of 6 months" — both arithmetically correct, from the untouched Financial lens.

**Mandatory reviewer gate** confirmed on the same real report: Approve correctly blocked while 13 findings and a real detected conflict were unresolved (status stayed `pending_review`), then correctly succeeded once every finding was accepted and the conflict resolved (status flipped to `approved`) — including confirming the AI-suggested-resolution prefill still works.

**Tender Readiness** confirmed on the same disposable account (no registration country/customer markets set, a genuine "not set" case): the "No AI-specific jurisdiction currently applies..." warning rendered correctly, and submitting without documentation correctly triggered the confirm-without-docs gate — cancelled without completing the submission, since the client-side gate firing correctly was sufficient proof without spending a real Groq call on a module already confirmed untouched by diff.

**Disclosed, not hidden**: one tool-permission stream error interrupted the first cleanup attempt mid-command, silently failing to write the cleanup script to disk — caught by the immediate `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` on retry rather than assumed successful, rewritten, and re-run cleanly.

All disposable test data (the account, company, goal, report, findings, conflict) deleted afterward — this was a verification pass over already-shipped fixes, not a first-of-its-kind proof run worth retaining. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch scripts deleted, never committed. `tsc --noEmit` re-confirmed clean after a stale `.next` dev-type-cache artifact (the same class already documented elsewhere in this file) was cleared.

No code changed in this pass — a pure regression-confirmation exercise, recorded here per the standing "keep this file current" discipline.

## Navigation audit fixes: workspace→company links, company directory, breadcrumbs, Evidence Intake nav entry (confirmed 2026-08-26)

Direct founder request, following a full navigation audit across both reviewer and client sides (reported first, no code changed until confirmed) — four fixes, all individually live-verified.

**1 & 3 (combined) — company link + breadcrumb on all three review workspace pages.** [review/[reportId]/page.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review/[reportId]/page.tsx>), [review-module/[requestId]/page.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review-module/[requestId]/page.tsx>), [review-sprint/[sprintId]/page.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review-sprint/[sprintId]/page.tsx>) each gained a real `← {Company Name}` link to `/company/[companyId]`, placed as a sibling above the existing client component (not inside it) with its own `mx-auto max-w-3xl px-6 pt-6` wrapper — deliberately not sharing a wrapper with the big client components below, which already wrap themselves in the same classes; nesting would have doubled the horizontal padding. Two of the three pages' own Supabase queries were missing `company_id` entirely (only `companies(name)` was selected) — added it. Using the company name as the link text (not generic "Back") does both jobs the founder asked for at once: a real link to the company page, and a page-specific breadcrumb, matching the exact pattern `/company/[companyId]` itself already used ("← Back to queue"). **Verified live** against real data (Nimbus Ledger Ltd, which has real reports/modules/sprints) on all three pages — each correctly linked to the same real company page.

**2 — company directory, reversing the earlier "hold, queue-only" decision.** New [companies/page.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/companies/page.tsx>) — a simple list (name, plan tier badge, last-activity date), no filtering, per explicit direction. "Last activity" is computed, not a stored column: the most recent of `companies.updated_at`, latest `reports.submitted_at`, latest `module_requests.created_at`, and latest `pending_evidence_submissions.updated_at` — a handful of small queries aggregated in JS rather than one complex SQL join, matching this codebase's own established pattern for small-scale internal-tooling aggregation given real client volume is still tiny. Added to the reviewer nav as "Companies". **Verified live**: real 8-company list, correctly sorted by last activity, correctly showing Riverbank Analytics Ltd's real `concierge` plan tier; clicked through to a real company page successfully.

**4 — Evidence Intake nav entry + Services page fix.** Investigated the exact claim first: the Services page docblock's mention of "evidence-intake page" was a comment about *shared reasoning* with that page (the `latestReport` gate), not literally a false claim of a link — but the practical effect was the same the founder described: Services had zero real link to Evidence Intake anywhere on the page. Fixed both real problems: the "Core Audit" card now has a real "Submit new evidence" link alongside the existing "View your dashboard" one, and the ambiguous comment was clarified. For nav placement, considered two options — a plain flat link matching every other nav item's visual treatment, vs. a distinct CTA-styled button — and picked the plain flat link: this app's client nav has no existing precedent for a differently-styled nav item, and Evidence Intake behaves like every other nav destination (a real page, not a modal/action), so introducing a new visual pattern for just this one item would be inconsistent rather than clarifying. Added "Submit Evidence" to [(app)/layout.tsx](<src/app/(app)/layout.tsx>)'s nav, placed right after Dashboard (the natural next action), replacing reliance on the one narrow, easily-missed "Want to pursue a different goal? Submit new evidence..." inline link on Dashboard (left in place — still accurate for that specific framing, just no longer the only path). **Verified live**: "Submit Evidence" renders in the nav on every client-facing page and links correctly; Services' Core Audit card shows both real links.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and scratch scripts deleted afterward, never committed.

## Reports & History full redesign (confirmed 2026-08-26)

Direct founder request, explicitly modeled on the Dashboard redesign's own discipline: research real sources, propose a structure, then build and report back with screenshots — not another one-off fix. Three concrete problems named: no visual distinction between item types, no grouping (real deliverables mixed flat with session activity), and an explicit ask to cross-check whether completed Execution Sprints actually render here (the page's own subtitle claims they do).

**Research, not just opinion** (same discipline as the Dashboard pass) — checked real sources before deciding: list-design and activity-feed guidance consistently recommend (a) a consistent visual marker per item type for scannability in mixed lists, and (b) grouping by category when items are genuinely heterogeneous, while preserving chronological order *within* each group when recency/audit-trail matters — this page's own subtitle already promises a chronological record, so grouping sits on top of that, it doesn't replace it. Sources: [uxpin.com/studio/blog/list-design](https://www.uxpin.com/studio/blog/list-design/), [eleken.co/blog-posts/list-ui-design](https://www.eleken.co/blog-posts/list-ui-design), [getstream.io/blog/activity-feed-design](https://getstream.io/blog/activity-feed-design/), [uxpatterns.dev/patterns/data-display/timeline](https://uxpatterns.dev/patterns/data-display/timeline).

**Structure decided from that research**, all in [reports/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/reports/page.tsx>):
1. **A colored type badge per item** (`TYPE_BADGE_STYLES`) — 9 distinct types (Core Audit, 3 modules, Execution Sprint, Discovery/Delivery/F2F Workshop sessions, plus Concierge Inquiry — see the real bug below), each a genuinely distinct hue. Chosen over introducing an icon set: this app has zero icon library dependency anywhere and an already-established "colored badge as differentiator" pattern (severity badges, plan-tier badges, missing-evidence badges) — a new icon system would be a new visual language, not a consistent extension of the existing one. Core Audit deliberately uses the real `accent`/`accent-ink` brand tokens (not a raw Tailwind amber utility) since `accent` is already this app's own "primary/flagship" signal — and deliberately NOT the same `amber-100/800` pairing Signals/Dashboard use for "medium severity," so the two unrelated meanings don't read as the same color.
2. **Two real sections — "Deliverables" and "Sessions"** — replacing the one flat mixed list, each its own `Card`. A section with zero items simply doesn't render its heading.
3. **Execution Sprints — confirmed present in both code AND live rendering**, not just assumed from reading the code: the previous version's subtitle claim was accurate. Verified against Riverbank Analytics Ltd's real `complete` sprint ("Gross Margin Trend"), which correctly appeared in the Deliverables section.

**A real bug found and fixed during this same pass, not anticipated upfront**: `session_requests.session_type` has a 4th real value, `concierge_inquiry` (added 2026-08-24, Concierge tier) — the pre-existing `SESSION_LABELS` map on the OLD version of this page never had an entry for it either (it just fell through to the raw type string), but the new type→badge derivation logic being written for this redesign would have actively mislabeled a completed/declined Concierge inquiry as "F2F Workshop" specifically, a worse regression than the silent gap it replaced. Caught live, not assumed safe: Sally's real account has a genuine `concierge_inquiry` row, and testing against her account (part of verifying the redesign broadly, not a targeted hunt for this specific bug) surfaced it before shipping. Fixed by adding "concierge" as its own `ItemType` with its own label ("Concierge Inquiry") and badge color (fuchsia) and fixing the derivation ternary. Verified by temporarily setting her real request's status to `declined` (non-destructive test-and-revert, reverted to its real `requested` status immediately after), confirming the correct label and badge rendered, not a mislabel.

**Verified live end-to-end, real accounts, both light and dark mode**: Riverbank Analytics Ltd (Execution Sprint + Core Audit deliverables, Discovery Session), Sally's real account (3× Tender Readiness + Core Audit deliverables, Discovery Session ×2 + the Concierge Inquiry fix), and Nimbus Ledger Ltd (confirmed the empty-modules case renders correctly — its real modules are all still `approved`, never `sent`/delivered, so correctly absent here, consistent with the pre-existing `status = 'sent'` filter, not a redesign regression). Dark mode confirmed legible and correctly styled via a real screenshot.

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and all scratch scripts deleted afterward, never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup.

Committed and pushed (commit `8c10aa4`), per explicit go-ahead.

## Full UX validation pass across every page, both sides — 5 items worked through individually (confirmed 2026-08-26)

Direct follow-up to the Reports & History redesign: a full pass over every client-facing and reviewer-facing page, checked against 4 criteria (visual hierarchy, item-type distinction in mixed lists, copy clarity, design-system consistency), reported in full before any fix — same "report first" discipline as the Dashboard redesign. The founder then confirmed 5 items to fix, in order, explicitly holding everything else (queue section ordering, sparse module pages, missing nav on `/privacy`/`/terms`/`/demo`, minor styling items) for a later batch.

**1. Sprint anomaly on Sally's real sprint (`10b15125-...`) — investigated first, confirmed test-data debris, not a live bug, nothing fixed.** `approveSprintTasks()` ([workspace.ts](src/lib/execution-sprint/workspace.ts)) was read directly and confirmed correct: it blocks on any `reviewer_status: draft` task and only stamps `due_date`/flips the sprint to `in_progress` once every task is already decided — there is no code path anywhere that ever resets a task back to `draft` after that (grepped for every write of `reviewer_status: "draft"` in the codebase; the only one is the initial insert in `confirmSprintFinding()`). Yet a direct DB query showed exactly this on the live row: task `af22772c-...` sits at `reviewer_status: draft` while already carrying a real `due_date` (`2026-08-30`) — a due date that can only ever have been computed by `approveSprintTasks()`'s own success path, meaning this task was genuinely non-draft at the moment the sprint was approved, and something reset it back to `draft` afterward without touching `due_date`. Root-caused precisely, not left as "probably test debris": this is the exact same real, kept sprint the earlier 2026-08-19 `kpi_unit` build's live verification edited-and-reverted ("clicked Edit on the first task... reverted the row to its exact original state (`kpi_unit: null`, `reviewer_status: draft`)") — that revert script assumed the task's pre-edit `reviewer_status` was `draft` without first reading back its true prior value, which for this specific task (already `approved` since the sprint's real 2026-08-16 approval) was wrong. **A real, separate, smaller bug found during this same investigation, then fixed on direct follow-up request**: [SprintReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review-sprint/[sprintId]/SprintReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx>)'s mandatory-decision banner (`draftCount > 0`) had no dependency on `sprintStatus` at all, so it would show "Mandatory before this sprint can start" on an already-`in_progress`/`complete` sprint in any future case where a task somehow ends up back in `draft` — a real display-logic gap independent of whether this specific data anomaly recurs. Fixed by branching the banner's copy on `sprintStatus === "scoped"`: the original "Mandatory before this sprint can start" framing is unchanged for the genuine pre-start case, and a new, distinct message ("Unexpected: this sprint has already started, but N task(s) still show as undecided... this shouldn't normally happen once a sprint is under way") renders instead for any other status — same red alert box (still worth a reviewer's attention), honest copy instead of a misleading one. **Verified live against both real cases, not just the one that motivated the fix**: Sally's real, still-anomalous `in_progress` sprint (`10b15125-...`, deliberately left in its real, uncorrected state — the data anomaly itself was never the thing to fix) now correctly shows the new "Unexpected: this sprint has already started..." copy; her other real, genuinely pre-start `scoped` sprint (`1c3a79c1-...`, 5 real undecided tasks) correctly still shows the original "Mandatory before this sprint can start" copy, confirming the fix didn't regress the real, common case it needs to keep working for.

**2. Pattern A (raw enum values) fixed, reusing one new shared humanizer, not per-page logic.** New [`src/lib/format.ts`](src/lib/format.ts) — `humanizeStatus()` (generic snake_case → sentence-case fallback) plus `SESSION_STATUS_LABELS` (extracted from `(app)/reports/page.tsx`, where it originated, so `/company/[companyId]`, `/queue`, and the Dashboard's own separate copy of the same map all read the one real source instead of three near-duplicates). `GOAL_LABELS` (already existing, from `@/lib/lenses/goals`) is reused directly for the goal display on `/company/[companyId]` — was showing raw `growth_revenue_efficiency` before. Applied via display-site-only calls (`humanizeStatus(rawValue)`), never by mutating the underlying prop/field a comparison elsewhere in the same file depends on (`requestStatus`/`sprintStatus` stay their real raw values for the `disabled={... !== "scoped"}`-style checks; only the JSX text node is humanized) — confirmed by re-reading each of the 4 files' own status-comparison logic before touching the display line. Fixed on `/company/[companyId]` (goal, report/module/session/sprint statuses), `/review-module/[requestId]` (header status), `/review-sprint/[sprintId]` (header status), and `/requests` (Status column, all row types uniformly via the plain humanizer rather than `SESSION_STATUS_LABELS`' richer session-specific copy — chosen for table-wide visual consistency across every row type, not because the richer copy is wrong elsewhere).

**3. Pattern B (colored item-type badges) fixed, reusing the exact Reports & History badge system, extracted into a shared module.** New [`src/lib/item-type-badge.tsx`](src/lib/item-type-badge.tsx) — `ItemType`, `TYPE_LABELS`, `TYPE_BADGE_STYLES`, `TypeBadge`, plus `moduleTypeToItemType()`/`sessionTypeToItemType()` mappers, extracted verbatim from `(app)/reports/page.tsx` (which now imports from here too, closing the loop so Reports & History can't drift from the pages that just started reusing it). Wired in:
- **`/requests`** ([RequestsFilterClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/requests/RequestsFilterClient.tsx>)) — [unified-requests.ts](src/lib/reviewer/unified-requests.ts)'s `UnifiedRequestRow` gained a `badgeType: ItemType` field (the existing coarse `type: "audit"|"module"|"session"|"sprint"` can't distinguish Tender Readiness from AI Reliability, or Discovery from Concierge) computed via the new mappers. The Type column now renders `<TypeBadge>`; Severity got its own small local `SEVERITY_STYLES` copy (matching this codebase's existing convention of every severity-badge consumer keeping its own local copy, not a prior inconsistency introduced here) — not explicitly asked for, but directly adjacent, low-risk, and left flat would have looked incomplete next to the fixed Type column in the same table.
- **`/queue`** ([page.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/queue/page.tsx>)) — both the "Session requests" list and the "Ready for review" grouped list now render `<TypeBadge>` instead of plain text; `QueueItem` gained `badgeType`, with the sprint case's "(task review)" qualifier kept as separate small text next to the badge rather than folded into a label the shared system has no concept of. Local `SESSION_TYPE_LABELS` const removed (superseded by the shared module); local `MODULE_LABELS` kept (still feeds the now-otherwise-unused-for-display `label` field, harmless).
- **`/company/[companyId]`** — module/session/sprint rows all converted from plain text to `<TypeBadge>`; local `MODULE_LABELS`/`SESSION_TYPE_LABELS` consts removed.
- **`/signals`** ([SignalsClient.tsx](<src/app/(app)/signals/SignalsClient.tsx>)) — a genuinely different dimension from the other four pages (which lens of one Core Audit a finding came from, not which deliverable this is), so NOT forced into the same `ItemType` union. The 3 module sourceKeys reuse `TYPE_BADGE_STYLES` directly (same real entities, same color); 5 new lens colors were added (`LENS_BADGE_STYLES`: financial=green, execution=sky, product=violet, commercial=pink, ai_governance=cyan), chosen to avoid every hue already used by `TYPE_BADGE_STYLES` or `SEVERITY_STYLES` in the same file. Also closes the "no dimension differentiation" half of the original finding: the Source filter chips now show each source's own color when active (previously generic accent for every chip regardless of group); the Severity filter chips now show each severity's own real `SEVERITY_STYLES` color when active (previously the same generic accent) — the two filter groups are now visually distinct from each other, not just labeled differently.

**4. Two other real bugs, both confirmed and fixed at the data/display layer respectively.**
- **Dashboard €/£ currency mismatch** — investigated before assuming a code bug: `aggregateFinancialImpact()` ([financial-impact.ts](src/lib/reports/financial-impact.ts)) already correctly picks one consistent currency (the first quantified finding's) for any summed range, so the mismatch wasn't an aggregation bug. Queried Sally's real report directly and found exactly one real finding ("No Risk Classification Awareness," AI & Governance lens) with a genuine LLM-generated `currency: "EUR"` in its `financialImpact` — plausibly the model associating "EU AI Act" regulatory context with EUR, even though this company (and every real run in this UK-first V1 app) is GBP. Fixed with a one-time, disclosed data correction (same precedent as the earlier "get 100 cusomers" typo fix) — updated that one finding's `ai_draft.financialImpact.currency` from `"EUR"` to `"GBP"` directly, not a code change, since this is real per-finding LLM output data, not a computation bug. Deliberately did NOT add speculative code-level currency normalization on top — this app already documents cross-currency handling as a known, accepted UK-first-V1 simplification, and silently coercing future non-GBP values would mask a real future LLM-output-quality signal rather than surface it.
- **Raw internal tag strings in "Similar patterns across other companies"** ([ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx](<src/app/(reviewer)/review/[reportId]/ReviewWorkspaceClient.tsx>)) — was rendering `case-library.ts`'s own machine-parseable tags verbatim (`goal:growth_revenue_efficiency`, `lens:financial:severity:critical`). New `formatOverlapTag()` pattern-matches the exact tag shapes `case-library.ts` itself produces (`goal:`/`industry:`/`stage:`/`lens:<key>:severity:<level>`) and renders them using this same file's own existing `LENS_LABELS` and the shared `GOAL_LABELS`, falling back to the generic `humanizeStatus()` for anything unmatched.

**5. Dashboard's own separate, incomplete `SESSION_LABELS`/`SESSION_STATUS_LABELS` consts removed, now reading the same single source of truth as everywhere else.** [dashboard/page.tsx](<src/app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx>) had its own local copy of both maps — the label one was missing `concierge_inquiry` entirely (rendering the raw string as a request-tile title, the exact class of bug already fixed once in Reports & History but never propagated here), and the status one was a straight duplicate of what's now `SESSION_STATUS_LABELS` in `@/lib/format`. Both removed; the tile title now reads `TYPE_LABELS[sessionTypeToItemType(r.session_type)]`, the status line falls back to `humanizeStatus()` for any value `SESSION_STATUS_LABELS` doesn't cover.

**Verified live, every item, real accounts, real screenshots/DOM checks — not assumed from code review**: `/queue` (both lists genuinely color-badged, `OVERDUE` badge unaffected, all statuses humanized); `/requests` (Type/Status/Severity all correctly styled across a real 44-row table); `/company/[companyId]` (Sally's real goal, reports, modules, sessions, sprints all correctly humanized and badged, confirmed via `getComputedStyle`/className inspection since screenshots were flaky this pass); `/signals` (Sally's real findings — Source chips show 5 distinct lens colors + reused module colors, Severity chips show real severity colors, individual finding badges correctly colored); `/review-module/[requestId]` and `/review-sprint/[sprintId]` (both header lines humanized, including the still-anomalous sprint from item 1, deliberately left showing its real, unfixed state); `/review/[reportId]` ("Similar patterns" tags fully humanized against Honest Pass Test Co's real overlap data); Dashboard (all currency values confirmed `£`-only via a live regex scan of the rendered page; "Concierge Inquiry" confirmed rendering correctly in the active-requests tile, replacing the raw string).

`tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src/ --quiet`, and `npm run build` all clean throughout, re-checked after clearing a stale `.next` dev-type-cache reference to the deleted debug route (the same class of artifact already documented elsewhere in this file). Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route, `scratch-mint.ts`, and a second, older `scratch-find-sprint.ts` (pre-existing debris from before this pass, cleaned up in the same pass) all deleted afterward, never committed — confirmed via `git status` before this writeup.

**Follow-up, same day**: item 1's banner fix (above) built and live-verified in a second pass — `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint src/ --quiet`/`npm run build` re-confirmed clean after it. One real, unrelated environment snag hit and resolved during this follow-up, not an app bug: the running dev server's `.next` directory had been wiped out from under it by the prior pass's own `rm -rf .next && npm run build` (a production build, not dev mode) — its Turbopack dev-mode artifacts were gone, producing 500s on every route. Fixed by stopping and restarting the dev server cleanly after clearing `.next` again; unrelated to the actual code change, confirmed by the fix working immediately once the server had a fresh dev build to run against. Temporary `/api/debug-set-session` route and `scratch-mint.ts` recreated for this verification pass and deleted again afterward, never committed.

Committed and pushed (commit `35cab22`), per explicit go-ahead.

## Onboarding Architecture & Path Routing brief — Part 7 carry-over status confirmed, build starting (2026-08-27)

Direct founder brief (full doc: "Elvanis — Developer Brief: Onboarding Architecture & Path Routing," August 2026) introducing a post-signup routing screen (Path A: Business Diagnosis / Path B: AI Compliance Audit / Hub for "not sure"), path-specific minimal profiles, a dynamic Dashboard lead section by `entry_path`, and several new Tender Readiness/reviewer requirements. Investigated every carry-over item against real code/DB before starting any new build, per the brief's own standing instruction ("check the actual codebase — do not assume the current state from this document alone"). Recording the result here explicitly, per direct founder request, so a future session never re-investigates these from scratch.

**Part 7 (all 8 carry-over items) — confirmed complete, verified against code and the live DB on 2026-08-27, nothing further needed:**
1. **"get 100 cusomers" typo** — confirmed still fixed (queried every `goals` row live; zero matches for "cusomer").
2. **Top 3 consistency (commit `f672c7b`)** — confirmed at the code level: `resolveTop3FindingsInOrder()` ([src/lib/reports/top3.ts](src/lib/reports/top3.ts)) is the single function used by all three consumers (Dashboard, client Report page, `/demo-live`); Dashboard's `topPriority` (action banner) and `deriveRoadmap()` (roadmap) both derive from the same resolved array, not separate computations. Not re-clicked through a fresh account this pass — folded into the live verification of the new onboarding build instead, since fresh test accounts get created there anyway.
3. **Dashboard subtitle (5 states)** — confirmed already built exactly as specified in the earlier brief: `no_evidence` / `editing` (awaiting-review vs. re-audit-submitted split) / `queued_for_audit` / `audit_in_progress` / `in_review` / `has_report` (with the post-sprint override), line 2 gated behind `isUsableFinancialImpact()`.
4. **AI banner integrity rule** — confirmed satisfied; the only AI-related banner anywhere is the report-page callout (item 8 below), and no code path constructs a £ figure from the AI-in-production checkbox alone.
5. **AI-in-production storage** — **both a queryable column AND a JSON blob exist, deliberately, for different purposes.** `companies.has_ai_in_production` (added 2026-08-20) is the real, queryable, *current* value. `reports.source_evidence_snapshot->aiGovernance->hasLiveAiInProduction` is a frozen *per-report* value, intentionally never overwritten by the live column. **Real, load-bearing nuance for this brief's Part 3**: the existing boolean is a plain yes/no — NOT the same shape as this brief's new 4-option triage Q1 (customer-facing / internal-only / exploring / not-sure). Part 8c's new triage columns are additive alongside this one, not a replacement for it.
6. **Services page copy** — confirmed still correct, no SLA/response-time text anywhere.
7. **Tender Readiness procurement honesty** — confirmed still intentional (the "say so honestly, `confidenceLevel: insufficient`, never invent" rule is still live in the prompt).
8. **AI & Governance report callout** — confirmed already built, correctly reading the frozen per-report snapshot rather than the live column (a delivered report's callout shouldn't drift with later profile edits).

**Three architectural decisions confirmed by the founder before starting the new build (all three surfaced as real conflicts/open questions during the Part 1–6 architecture review, not guessed at):**
1. **Path B's "No/exploring" AI-usage branch is structurally "Path A, entered via Path B"** — that triage answer routes into the real, existing goal-selection step (reused, not reinvented) and a full Core Audit, since the AI & Governance lens this branch promises only ever exists inside a full 5-lens run. Every other Path B branch stays genuinely goal-less.
2. **"Route to human consultation" (Part 3's Q2=active-request-regardless-of-Q1 override) reuses the existing session-request mechanism** with a new `session_type` value (`compliance_consultation`) — same pattern as `concierge_inquiry`, not a new mechanism.
3. **Part 5's fallback state (entry_path changed, no activity yet on the newly-chosen path) triggers on path-specific activity, not any activity** — a client with real history on their OLD path still sees the "get started" fallback on a path they haven't touched yet.

**Build order, confirmed**: 8a → 8b → 8c (foundational fields) → Parts 1–6 → 8d–8f. Build itself documented in the section(s) below as it lands.

## Onboarding Architecture & Path Routing — full build, Parts 8a–8c, 1–6, 8d–8f (confirmed 2026-08-27)

Built in the confirmed order. Everything below verified live against real accounts through real UI clicks, not code review alone, per this session's standing discipline.

**8a/8b/8c — foundational schema, pushed live first:**
- **8a (jurisdiction fields)**: `registration_country`/`uae_free_zone`/`customer_market_countries` already existed on `companies` (2026-07-31) — no new schema needed, only new UI wiring at onboarding time (Part 3).
- **8b**: `companies.entry_path` (nullable text + CHECK: `diagnosis`/`ai_audit`/`undecided`) — [migration](supabase/migrations/20260827090000_companies_entry_path.sql). Null means "routing screen not yet answered" (a real company can't have one — see below); `undecided` is a genuine persisted choice, not the same state.
- **8c**: `companies.triage_ai_usage`/`triage_compliance_request`/`triage_personal_data` (all nullable text + CHECK) — [migration](supabase/migrations/20260827090500_companies_triage_fields.sql). Deliberately NOT reusing the existing `has_ai_in_production` boolean (confirmed different shape during the architecture review — that column is a plain yes/no, this brief's Q1 is a real 4-option answer). `triage_personal_data` added same day per the founder's own refinement (Q3, see below).
- **Two small additional schema pieces, needed by Part 3/8f, added in the same batch**: `module_requests.is_urgent` and `session_requests.is_urgent` (both `boolean not null default false`, [migrations](supabase/migrations/20260827091000_module_requests_is_urgent.sql)/[here](supabase/migrations/20260827092000_session_requests_is_urgent.sql)) — computed ONCE at creation from the company's own triage answer at that moment, never re-derived live, same "compute now, don't recompute against a possibly-later-changed value" principle already used for `reports.edit_window_closes_at`/`review_due_at`. A new `session_requests.session_type` value, `compliance_consultation` ([migration](supabase/migrations/20260827091500_session_type_compliance_consultation.sql)), and a new `app_settings` row, `module_delivery_turnaround_target_hours` (seeded 48, [migration](supabase/migrations/20260827093000_module_delivery_turnaround_setting.sql)) for Part 8f.

**The real architectural resolution, confirmed before writing any UI code**: company creation and goal creation were previously one atomic action (`createCompanyAndGoal()`), and the three standalone modules are confirmed genuinely goal-independent (zero references to `goals`/`GoalContext` anywhere in their code) — so Path B being goal-less for most branches is structurally sound. The one real fork (Path B's "No/exploring" branch) needs a real goal after all, per the founder's confirmed decision — handled by extending `OnboardingWizard` with an `attach` mode (see below) rather than duplicating the goal-selection step.

**Part 1 — Entry-path routing screen** ([EntryPathScreen.tsx](src/app/onboarding/EntryPathScreen.tsx)) — exact copy, three large clickable cards.
